Civ3 rules compendium

How Civilization III plays, written to guide the engine's rules and AI. It covers the base game (1.29f), Play the World (1.27f) and Conquests (1.22), with Conquests as the target. Scenarios, mods and the special Conquests game modes (Regicide, Mass Regicide, Elimination, Capture the Flag/Princess, victory-point games) are left out.

Every rule cites its source: a CivFanatics or Apolyton thread, a Firaxis statement, a patch readme, or the Civ3 Complete install (Civilopedia.txt, the editor help, labels.txt, and conquests.biq read with QueryCiv3). Each section ends with what is Disputed and what is UNVERIFIED. Don't treat those as rules: an engine choice made for one of them says so in a code comment, and the project owner's own rulings take precedence over forum sources.

Quotes taken through a web summarizer may differ slightly from the page. Check the page before relying on exact wording.

  • 1. Combat and units
  • 2. City economy
  • 3. Happiness, culture and borders
  • 4. Science, techs and wonders
  • 5. Governments
  • 6. Diplomacy, espionage and AI attitude
  • 7. Map, terrain, resources, workers, barbarians and huts
  • 8. AI behaviour, difficulty levels, civ traits, scoring and victory
  • Appendix: where the game still differs

1. Combat and units

Scope: base game, Play the World (PTW) and Conquests (C3C), patched (1.29f / PTW 1.27f / C3C 1.22). Scenarios, mods and the special C3C game modes (Regicide, Elimination, Capture the Princess, victory-point games) are out of scope. C3C is the target ruleset. Where vanilla or PTW differ, the difference is called out.

Sources used

Local files are from a Steam Civilization III Complete install (.../Sid Meier's Civilization III Complete/). Quotes from local files are exact. Quotes from web pages were pulled out with a fetch tool that summarizes pages, so short web quotes should be spot-checked against the page before anything leans on their exact wording.

Key Source
PEDIA Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt, the C3C 1.22 in-game Civilopedia. Cited by entry key (GCON_Combat, PRTO_Galley, ...).
PEDIA-V / PEDIA-PTW Text/Civilopedia.txt (vanilla 1.29f) and civ3PTW/Text/Civilopedia.txt (PTW 1.27f).
EDHELP Conquests/Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm, the official C3C editor help (decompiled). Cited by page name.
BIQ Conquests/conquests.biq (C3C 1.22 default rules), as imported into this repo's C7/Lua/civ3/ruleset.json by ImportCiv3 (_Console/RegenerateRuleset). Only fields that come from the BIQ are used.
README-V README.txt, the vanilla patch notes up to 1.29f, plus a "Changes" list of differences from the printed manual.
README-PTW civ3PTW/readme.txt (PTW patch notes up to 1.27f).
README-C3C Conquests/readme.txt (C3C 1.20 to 1.22).
CFC-ARMY https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/introductory/the-basics-of-armies/ (C3C only)
CFC-LEAD https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/military/creating-and-using-leaders/
CFC-SINK https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/sinking-chances-of-seafaring-and-non-seafaring-civilizations/ ("All tests done with Conquests, v1.22.")
CFC-RULES https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/editor-rules-explained.210035/ (Bluemofia's C3C editor field guide)
CFC-CSE https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/combat-system-explained.7679/ (Valant2, 2001-2002, vanilla)
CFC-RET https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-do-retreats-work.100878 and https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/retreating-units.104978/
APO-ZOC https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/124182-zoc-defensive-bombardment (Jan 2004, includes alexman, Firaxis)

Note: the repo file C7/Civilopedia/Text/Civilopedia.txt is OpenCiv3's own text ("It is not Civ3's Civilopedia"), so it is not used as a source. The repo's ruleset also carries a promotionChance per experience level; that field is not in the BIQ (the BIQ EXPR record holds only a name, base hit points and a retreat bonus, see QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Expr.cs), so it is not a source either.

1.1 Combat resolution

  • Four kinds of combat. Ground/naval combat, bombardment, missile attacks and air combat. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "There are four types of combat: ground/naval combat, bombardment, missile attacks, and air combat."
  • A battle is fought in rounds until one side has no hit points left; the loser dies, the winner may be damaged. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "The two units trade blows until one of them loses its health bar and is destroyed. The winner may be damaged (its health bar is reduced)."
  • Each round costs the loser exactly 1 hit point. Civ3 has no firepower. Every round removes one HP.
    • CFC-CSE (Valant2): "During battle each time the unit loses on a battle roll they loose 1 hp." and "If the unit wins the turn, it causes 1hp of damage to the opponent."
    • https://forum.freeciv.org/f/viewtopic.php?p=97232 (Akechi, from memory): "There is no Firepower status in Civ 3. all combat round deals 1 damage to each round loser."
    • Pre-release Firaxis statements on firepower are not available; the Apolyton thread https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/45717-probability-and-combat (Grunthex) says: "Uh, units have between 2 and 5 hp, not 10. Depending on their veterancy."
  • Per-round odds are A / (A + D), with A the attacker's modified attack and D the defender's modified defense. Community-derived, never confirmed by Firaxis in the sources found.
    • CFC-CSE (Valant2): "Crude Turn Win % = (attackers offensive rating)/ ((attackers offensive rating)+(defenders defensive rating))"
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/made-attacker-win-probability-page.7886/page-2 (Grendel): "the probability of the attacker winning one round (causing a HP loss in the defender) is a/(a+x)."
    • The same thread gives the whole-battle odds as a negative binomial: "(a / (a+x))^y * (x / (a+x))^z * nCr(y+z-1, y-1)", summed over z = 0 .. b-1 (b = attacker HP, y = defender HP, z = rounds the attacker loses).
  • Bombard odds use the same ratio, with bombard strength in place of attack. EDHELP's own example shows a 50% chance at equal values. EDHELP Building (Defensive Bonuses): "a unit with a bombard strength of 4 would have a 50% chance of destroying a building with a defense rating of 4." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/bombard-question.19648 (dunk): a bombard-8 cannon against a defense-3 cavalry has "an 8 out of 11 chance of hurting the cavalry."
  • Random seed is saved with the game (reloading replays the same results) unless the rule is turned off. README-V 1.21f: "Added Preserve Random Seed rule. Turning this off will allow random seeds to be changed when a saved game is reloaded (which makes combat different...)." Dan Magaha (Firaxis), https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/as-promised-a-civ-iii-combat-test-map.15208/: "the game was designed so that save + reload wouldn't be a very effective or efficient way to cheat"
  • Defender choice. The game defends a stack with its strongest available defender. UNVERIFIED (no source found that states the exact rule, for example whether HP or experience break ties).
  • Units without attack can't attack; units without defense are captured, not fought (see 1.13).
  • Stealth attack lets the attacker pick its target in a stack (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Stealth_Attack: "Stealth attack allows a unit to specify which enemy unit -- in a stack -- it will attack." Submarines have it (PEDIA PRTO_Submarine: "Submarines have $LINK<Stealth Attack=GCON_Stealth_Attack>").
  • Collateral damage (C3C ability). EDHELP Create Craters and Collateral Damage: if the defender "takes damage, then: If the defending unit is not in a city, the artillery/bombard value of the attacking unit is used to conduct a bombard attack on that space to determine if any terrain improvements are also destroyed. If the defending unit is in a city, the the artillery/bombard value of the attacking unit is used to conduct a bombard attack on that city".
  • Craters (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Craters: "When a bombardment attack which eliminates the last unit or terrain improvement in a square, a crater may be formed. Like pollution, the presence of craters reduces a square's production, commerce, and food output by one."
  • "Starts Golden Age" units. EDHELP Unit Abilities: "If victorious in combat, this unit triggers a golden age".

Healing

  • 1 HP per turn, only in friendly or neutral territory, only if the unit does not move. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "adding one $LINK<block=GCON_Experience> per turn back to its health bar. To do so, it ^* must be in friendly or neutral territory. ^* must not move for a complete turn."
  • Full heal in one turn: ground units in a city with Barracks, ships with a Harbor, air units with an Airport. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "A city with a $LINK<barracks=BLDG_Barracks> heals ground units completely in one turn." (same wording for Harbor and Airport).
  • Battlefield Medicine (small wonder, needs 5 Hospitals) allows healing in enemy territory. PEDIA BLDG_Battlefield_Medicine: "Allows military units to heal in enemy territory. ^{Requires:} The civilization must have at least five hospitals".
  • BIQ heal-rate table: friendly_field 1, neutral_field 1, hostile_field 0, city 2. (The "city 2" value means a city without Barracks heals 2 HP per turn if the import is correct. Not confirmed by a text source.)

1.2 Experience and hit points

  • Hit points by experience level. BIQ EXPR and PEDIA agree.

    Level HP (blocks) Retreat bonus (BIQ)
    Conscript 2 34
    Regular 3 50
    Veteran 4 58
    Elite 5 66

    PEDIA GCON_Experience: "Conscripts 2 ^Regulars 3 ^Veterans 4 ^Elite units 5".

  • Unit HP bonus (editor, since 1.21f): added to the level's base HP, from -20 to +20, never below 1 HP. All stock units have 0 (UNVERIFIED for every unit; the BIQ hpBonus field is 0 for the units checked). EDHELP HP Bonus: "This bonus is added to the base hitpoint value (determined by the unit's experience level) ... the unit's total number of HP will never drop below 1."

  • What produces each level. Cities build regulars; a Barracks (Harbor for ships, Airport for planes) builds veterans; draft and barbarian-hut units are conscripts; elite only comes from combat. PEDIA GCON_Experience: "Normally, cities produce regulars. But if a city has a $LINK<barracks=BLDG_Barracks>, it produces [veterans]. ... [Elite units] cannot be built: they gain this status only through actual combat experience." PEDIA BLDG_Harbor / BLDG_Airport: "produce $LINK<veteran=GCON_Experience> naval units" / "air units instead of regulars." Sun Tzu's Art of War gives a Barracks in every city on its continent (PEDIA BLDG_Art_of_War: "Puts a $LINK<barracks=BLDG_Barracks> in every city on the same continent.").

  • Battlefield promotion after a win. PEDIA GCON_Experience: "When one of your units is victorious in battle, there is a chance it will gain combat experience and be promoted to the next level." The chances are not in the Civilopedia or BIQ. Community figures:

    Promotion Non-militaristic Militaristic
    Conscript to Regular 1/2 doubled
    Regular to Veteran 1/4 1/2
    Veteran to Elite 1/8 1/4
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/barbarians-gaining-experience.115547 (Theoden, #18): odds roughly 1 in 2, 1 in 4 and 1 in 8, "Double the chances for militaristic civs and halve them when fighting barbarians."
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/heroic-epic-militaristic-civs.26455 (Catt, #2): "A veteran militaristic unit has a 1 in 4 chance of being promoted to elite after a victorious battle against an AI civ." and "A veteran non-militaristic unit has a 1 in 8 chance." Catt (#6) credits this to a Soren Johnson post that is not reproduced.
    • PEDIA GCON_Strengths (Militaristic): "combat $LINK<experience=GCON_Experience> is gained more quickly."

  • Barbarians do not promote (C3C 1.22 test). Same thread 115547 (Theoden, #14, C3C 1.22): "all dying without any of the mech infs gaining any experience." This is about barbarian units gaining levels, not about your units promoting while fighting barbarians (see Disputed).

  • A damaged unit keeps its level. Experience is the level, not the current HP. A veteran at 2/4 HP is still a veteran for retreat and promotion. Apolyton (Ethelred) https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/75784-fast-units-no-longer-retreating-at-low-health: "The editor's retreat setting is for the experience level not the hit points."

  • Units that made a leader are marked. README-V 1.29f: "Added an '*' next to experience level name for units that have produced a leader." Whether such a unit can make a second leader is UNVERIFIED.

1.3 Great Leaders (military)

  • A leader can appear when an elite unit wins a battle. PEDIA GCON_Leaders: "A {Military leader} is born in combat. This [can] occur when an $LINK<elite unit=GCON_Experience> wins a battle."
  • Chance per elite win: 1/16; 1/12 with the Heroic Epic; half that when the win is on defense. Not in the BIQ or Civilopedia. Community figures, consistent across sources:
    • CFC-LEAD: "Each time one of your elite units wins a fight against a rival, there is a 1 in 16 chance that a leader will appear." / "your chance of getting a leader improves to 1 in 12 when you win a fight with an elite." / "The chances of getting a leader from a successful defense are 1/2 the chances while attacking."
    • https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-play-the-world/80836-how-a-leader-is-born (Catt): "The chance of a leader generation is 1/12, 1/16, 1/24, or 1/32, depending on circumstances".
    • PEDIA BLDG_Epic: "Increases the chances of the appearance of $LINK<leaders=GCON_Leaders> from victorious combat."
  • No leaders from fighting barbarians. CFC-LEAD: "You can not get a leader by fighting a barbarian."
  • The militaristic trait does not change leader odds (it only raises promotion odds). Thread 26455 (Catt): "The odds of leader generation for ALL civs, militaristic or not, is 1/16 without the Heroic Epic".
  • Armies do not generate leaders. Thread 80836 (Catt): "Armies cannot generate leaders." Killing an army can (Theseus, same thread: "You can, however, generate a GL by an elite killing an Army.").
  • Only one military leader alive at a time, but several can appear in one turn. CFC-LEAD: "You can only have one leader at a time." / "You can however produce more than one leader in one game turn." The second statement looks inconsistent with the first; how it works (whether the limit is checked at turn start) is UNVERIFIED.
  • Leader unit. BIQ: Leader costs 0, A/D 0/0, movement 3. The unit the editor spawns is the "Battle Created Unit" (CFC-RULES: "Determines which unit is spawned when an elite unit wins a battle."; BIQ battleCreatedUnit: Leader). A leader cannot fight and is captured or destroyed if caught alone (see 1.13).
  • Uses (C3C): make an army, or finish an improvement or small wonder in the city it is in. It cannot hurry a Great Wonder. PEDIA GCON_Leaders: "A Military Leader can be used to either create an $LINK<army=PRTO_Army> or to $LINK<hurry=GCON_Hurry_Production> production of an Improvement or Small Wonder in a city. When a military leader does either of these actions he disappears."
  • Vanilla and PTW: leaders hurry anything, including Great Wonders. PEDIA-V / PEDIA-PTW GCON_Leaders: "Leaders can be used to either create an $LINK<army=GCON_Armies> or to $LINK<hurry=GCON_Hurry_Production> production in a city." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-is-a-leader-and-how-do-i-get-one.21132 (Fez_Monk): "Using the leader is the only way of hurrying a wonder."
  • C3C scientific leaders (from being first to a tech) can hurry Great Wonders. They belong with science, not combat. PEDIA GCON_Leaders: "A Scientific Leader can also be used to $LINK<hurry=GCON_Hurry_Production> the construction of any [improvement] (including a Great Wonder)".
  • A unit that makes a leader can be renamed. README-V: "Added the ability to rename units if they produce a great leader during combat."

1.4 Armies

  • Capacity 3 units, 4 with the Pentagon. The Pentagon needs 3 armies in the field. PEDIA BLDG_Pentagon: "[The Pentagon] increases the unit capacity of all the civilization's armies from three to four. ^{Requires:} The civilization must have at least three $LINK<armies=GCON_Armies> in the field." (BIQ Army capacity 3.)
  • Built by a leader in any city, or built (400 shields in C3C) in the city with the Military Academy. PEDIA GCON_Armies: "Armies can be built in cities that have the $LINK<Military Academy=BLDG_Military_Academy> or a $LINK<Leader=GCON_Leaders> in them." BIQ: Army shieldCost 400. The CivFanatics FAQ v3.0 (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/misc/civilization-iii-faqstrategy-guide-v3-0/) says "you can use 200 shields to construct an army". That is probably the vanilla cost (see Disputed).
  • At most one army per 4 cities. PEDIA GCON_Armies: "you must have at least four cities for every army you create." EDHELP Cities Needed to Support an Army: "Default is 4 cities." CFC-ARMY: dropping below the ratio keeps existing armies but blocks new ones.
  • Military Academy and Heroic Epic both need a victorious army first. PEDIA BLDG_Military_Academy: "The city with the [Military Academy] can build $LINK<armies=GCON_Armies> without the benefit of a $LINK<leader=GCON_Leaders>. Armies from this civilization are more effective in combat. ^{Requires:} The civilization must have had a victorious $LINK<army=GCON_Armies>." PEDIA BLDG_Epic has the same requirement.
  • Units in an army pool their hit points; the whole army must be killed to kill any unit in it. README-V ("Changes" from the manual): "Armies now literally share the hit points of the units within it. For example, an army of 3 regular spearman would have hit points equal to 9. To kill any unit in the army, the entire army must be eliminated."
  • Loading. Units can be loaded or unloaded only while the army is still in the city that made it; after it leaves, the contents are fixed. README-V: "Once that army leaves the city of it's construction, you cannot add or remove units from the army." BIQ allowUnloadFromArmy: false. Units in an army cannot be upgraded (CFC-ARMY: "Units in armies cannot be upgraded."). Only units with both attack and defense can be loaded (CFC-ARMY: "Only combat units (those with an attack and defense) can be loaded into an army.").
  • Abilities. Armies have zone of control and blitz (vanilla 1.17f on), movement = slowest unit + 1 (C3C), pillage (PTW 1.04f on), and see 2 tiles.
    • README-V 1.17f: "Armies now have the zone of control and blitz ability."
    • README-C3C: "an Army unit also has the "Blitz" capability (it can attack twice in the same turn), its movement rate has been increased by one, and units in an Army heal faster than they would if they were not within the Army unit."
    • README-V: "The only unit abilities that an army can inherit is mobility, and only when all units in that army have that ability."
    • README-PTW 1.04f: "Added the Pillage ability to Armies."
    • PEDIA GCON_Armies: "An army can "see" up to two squares away, due to its intrinsic scouts."
    • CFC-ARMY: "When no units have yet loaded into the army, the army will only have a movement of one."
  • Army combat strength (C3C). The fighting unit gets a share of the others' strength: Attack = A + (TA / N), N = 4 with the Military Academy, else 6, rounded down. Defense is the same with defense values. CFC-ARMY: "A is the attack of the unit currently doing the fighting." / "TA is the attacks of all the units added together." / "N is 4 if you own the military academy, else just 6." / "This formula is rounding fractions down." Whether TA includes the fighting unit itself is as written in the article ("all the units").
  • Which unit fights. PEDIA GCON_Armies: "An army uses one, some, or all its internal units to fight a battle, depending upon the course of the fight." CFC-CSE (Valant2, vanilla): "When an army attacks or defends the first unit continues to fight/defend until the unit is down to 1hp."
  • Army healing. CFC-ARMY: "Armies heal at the rate of one HP per unit in the army." and "armies are also able to heal in one turn in a city containing barracks." CFC-ARMY also says armies heal in enemy territory without Battlefield Medicine ("This can also be done in enemy territory even before battlefield medicine.").
  • Army retreat. Whether armies can withdraw is disputed (see Disputed).

1.5 Retreat (withdrawal)

  • Fast units withdraw when losing, unless the opponent is also fast. PEDIA PRTO_Horseman: "move fast and will [withdraw] from combat if losing (unless fighting another fast unit)." (PRTO_Cavalry has the same wording.)
  • Since 1.17f, withdrawal is a die roll that depends on experience. README-V 1.17f: "Mobile units now have to make a die roll to determine if they withdraw. Success is also modified by their experience level." Before that, fast units always withdrew (thread 100878, The Last Conformist: "In Original Release Vanilla, units always retreated if able.").
  • Retreat bonus by level (BIQ, editable since 1.21f): conscript 34, regular 50, veteran 58, elite 66. README-V 1.21f: "Editor: Added Retreat Bonus controls to Combat Experience page." CFC-RULES: "Determines the ratio of retreating when a fast unit is fighting a slow unit." EDHELP Retreat Bonus: "The higher the number listed for the retreat bonus, the more likely it is that the unit will successfully retreat when losing a fight."
  • "Fast" means more movement points than the opponent. CFC-CSE (Valant2): retreat requires higher movement and unused movement points. The exact comparison (base movement or moves left) is UNVERIFIED.
  • When the roll happens (community-tested): when the losing fast unit is about to drop to its last HP, the opponent has 2 or more HP, and the fast unit has movement left.
    • CFC-RET 104978 (Offa, #9): "Retreat possible if attacking unit drops from 2 HP to 1 HP, and is implemented before the attacker attacks with 1HP left." / "Zero chance if defender has 1 HP, or fast defender." / "Only one chance per turn to retreat, barring odd circumstances."
    • CFC-RET 100878 (boogaboo): the roll applies only if the opponent has 2HP or more, the retreating unit has 1HP, and it would have at least 1/3 movement point left after the attack.
    • A retreating unit survives with 1 HP and does not move (IbnSina, 100878: "Retreat does not require any special ground - no movement actually takes place.").
  • Retreat formula (best guess, not confirmed): P(retreat) = attacker retreat bonus / (defender retreat bonus + 50). CFC-RET 104978 (Offa): "prob of retreat = (attacker retreat bonus) / (defender retreat bonus + 50)", described as "thought to be". BomberEscort (quoted in 100878): "(100*Attacker Retreat Bonus)/(50+Defense Retreat Bonus)" and "When the Attackers retreat value is 50 percentage points greater than the Defender, the Attacker always retreats." Measured success ranges for fast attackers in valid cases (same post): "elite fast-attacker is 55-75% retreat"; veteran 50-66%; regular 43-56%; conscript 29-38%.
  • Defending fast units can also withdraw when attacked by a slower unit. CFC-RET 100878 (boogaboo): the retreat bonus applies "both to retreating when failing to attack and to retreaing when failing to defend." Disputed by Offa's "fast defender" zero-chance line (see Disputed).

1.6 Defense modifiers

  • Terrain bonuses (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat and BIQ TERR agree:

    Terrain Defense Move cost (BIQ) Wheeled can enter (BIQ)
    Grassland, Plains, Desert, Floodplain, Tundra +10% 1 yes
    Coast, Sea, Ocean (and Fresh Water Lake) +10% 1 n/a
    Marsh (C3C) +20% 2 no
    Forest +25% 2 yes
    Jungle +25% 3 no
    Hills +50% 2 yes
    Volcano (C3C) +80% 3 no
    Mountains +100% 3 no

    PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat: "^Marsh 20% ^River 25%* ^Forest 25% ^Jungles 25% ^Hills 50% ^Volcano 80% ^Mountains 100%". Vanilla and PTW have no Marsh or Volcano rows (PEDIA-V and PEDIA-PTW tables stop at the 14 terrain rows plus River).

  • River: +25% for a defender attacked across a river. PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat: "* This bonus applies to the defender if attacked from the other side of a river." BIQ/EDHELP River: "Default is 25."

  • Structural bonuses (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat:

    Source Bonus
    Fortified +25%
    Fortress +50%
    Barricade (C3C) +100%
    Town Walls (town only) +50%
    Civil Defense +50%
    City (size 7-12) +50%
    Metropolis (size 13+) +100%

    "^Fortified Units +25% ^Fortress +50% ^Barricade +100% ^Town Walls +50% ^Civil Defense +50% ^City +50% ^Metro +100%". EDHELP gives the defaults for the General-page fields: Town: "Default is 0 (no bonus)." City: "Default is 50." Metropolis: "Default is 100." Fortifications: "Default is 25." Fortress: "Default is 50." City size levels (BIQ): town up to 6, city up to 12 (maximumLevel1CitySize 6, maximumLevel2CitySize 12). Vanilla's table has no Civil Defense row (PEDIA-V); PTW adds it (PEDIA-PTW).

  • Bonuses add up (they are not multiplied). EDHELP Fortifications: "Note that this bonus is cumulative with other terrain and fortification-related defensive bonuses." EDHELP Defense Bonus (Improvement Properties): "This bonus is cumulative with other improvement and general defense bonuses". CFC-CSE (Valant2): "Bonus percentages add, so 2 50% bonuses result in a 100% total bonus." See Disputed for the multiplicative view.

  • Walls: +50% in towns only, and land bombard defense 8. PEDIA BLDG_Walls: "[Walls] can be built only in town-size settlements and provide a land bombardment defense of eight, and a 50% defensive bonus to units defending in the town. When a town becomes a city, the walls no longer provide a defensive bonus." BIQ: Walls combatDefenseBonus 0.5, bombardDefense 8, flag canOnlyBeBuiltInTowns. Walls do not add to the size 7+ bonus (Mike B., Firaxis, quoted by Catt in https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/70027-question-about-great-wall: "It has no effect on Cities or Metropolises (since walls are ineffective for these pop. levels)."). README-PTW: "Fixed bug which caused walls not to be obsoleted by growing from a town to a city."

  • Civil Defense: +50% at any size. PEDIA BLDG_Civil_Defense: "[Civil Defense] can be built in any size settlement and provides a 50% defensive bonus to units defending in the town. Civil Defense can only be built in a city that has a barracks." README-C3C: "Civil Defense requires Electronics".

  • Great Wall. C3C: a free Wall in every city on its continent, and double combat values against barbarians. PEDIA BLDG_Great_Wall: "Doubles unit combat values versus $LINK<Barbarians=GCON_Barbarians>. ^Places a $LINK<Wall=BLDG_Walls> in each city of the continent on which it is built." Vanilla/PTW: doubles walls instead. PEDIA-V BLDG_Great_Wall: "The [Great Wall] doubles the effects of $LINK<Walls=BLDG_Walls> in cities that have them." Mike B. (Firaxis, via thread 70027): "The Great Wall doubles the defensive value of Walls in all Towns that have them."

  • Cities in resistance give no defense bonus. PEDIA GCON_Terrain_Combat: "Cities with resisters do not give defensive bonuses."

  • Terrain under a city still counts. CFC-CSE (Xerxes314): "Terrain under a city does affect its defense value." The printed manual (p. 110, quoted by royfurr in thread 7886): "Terrain bonuses are figured in BEFORE THE CITY SIZE AND WALLS TAKE EFFECT."

  • Fortify. +25%; fortify, fortress and walls stack per EDHELP's "cumulative" wording. Some players believed the wall or fortress replaces fortify (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/defense-bonuses-question.21715). See Disputed. Which units cannot fortify is UNVERIFIED.

  • Fortress. +50%, gives zone of control to units inside. PEDIA GCON_Fortresses: "They get a 50% defensive bonus if attacked. ^* They have a $LINK<zone of control=GCON_ZOC> and fire at passing enemy units without receiving return fire." Built after Construction (same entry).

  • Barricade (C3C). Built on a fortress, +100%, stops enemy ground movement into the tile. PEDIA GCON_Barricades: "barricades automatically stops movement of a ground unit entering its square (unless that unit belongs to the same civilization currently controlling the barricade's square, or if that unit has a $LINK<Right of Passage=GCON_Agreements> agreement through that land." / "Barricades become a fortress if their square is pillaged."

  • Radar Tower (C3C): +25% attack and defense to friendly units within 2 tiles, once. PEDIA GCON_Radar_Towers: "The range of the effected area is 2 tiles. Friendly units within the Radar Towers range get a +25% attack and defense bonus. This bonus can only be applied once, even if multiple towers overlap."

  • Coastal Fortress: naval bombard defense 8, and it bombards passing enemy ships. It gives no defense bonus to land units. PEDIA BLDG_Coastal_Fortress: "A [Coastal Fortress] gives the city a naval bombardment defense of eight. ^The fortress automatically bombards passing enemy ships." README-PTW: "Fixed Civilopedia entry for Coastal Fortress by removing reference to, "50% defense bonus from naval attacks". There are no direct naval attacks on land units -- only bombardment." (The BIQ still holds a naval defense bonus of 50 for it, which per this note has no target.)

  • Attacker bonuses. Terrain gives no attack bonus (CFC-CSE: "terrain does not give any offensive bonuses what so ever"). The only attack modifiers found are the Radar Tower and the bonus against barbarians.

  • Rounding of modified strengths. Unknown. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-combat-calculator.454569/ (vmxa): "I think it is rounded down, after all modifiers are applied." Xerxes314 (CFC-CSE): "I don't know why anybody would think that there's rounding involved at any stage of the calculation." UNVERIFIED.

Versus barbarians

  • Attack bonus against barbarians, by difficulty (editor DIFF field, added in 1.16f). README-V 1.16f: "Editor: Added Barbarian Combat Bonus to difficulty level tab." Values from https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-difficulty-level-bonuses.37490/ (Pyrkaige, read from the PTW/1.29f editor): "Attack bonus against barbarians: 800 400 200 100 50 0" for Chieftain, Warlord, Regent, Monarch, Emperor, Deity. Arathorn's later post in the same thread gives Demigod 25 and Sid 0 (C3C levels).

    Chieftain Warlord Regent Monarch Emperor Deity Demigod Sid
    +800% +400% +200% +100% +50% 0 +25%? 0

    EDHELP Attack Bonus Against Barbarians: "Determines the attack bonus enjoyed by any player (human or AI) against barbarians." Whether it is attack only or also defense is UNVERIFIED (the field name says attack).

  • Great Wall doubles combat values against barbarians (all versions; quoted above).

1.7 Bombardment

  • Bombard stats: strength, range, rate of fire. EDHELP Rate of Fire: "Rate of fire defines how many times a bombarding unit will attack per turn." CFC-RULES: "Determines the number of times the unit initiates a bombard check when bombarding. (0-10)". EDHELP Bombard Range: "how many squares away a unit may bombard cities or units."

  • Non-lethal bombard can't kill: it stops at 1 HP. PEDIA GCON_Combat: "Bombardment inflicts losses to the defender's health bar but never completely destroys them." CFC-RULES: lethal bombard lets a unit "kill units when bombarding, and to bombard units with 1 HP."

  • A failed bombard roll hurts nobody; the bombarding unit never takes damage. freeciv thread (Akechi): "If attacker lose round during this combat, attacker bombard unit does not receive damage." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/artillery-civ3-vs-civ4.145967 (search excerpt): a bombard "does not expose the artillery to harm."

  • Each hit removes 1 HP. CFC-CSE (Valant2): "I assume all successful bombarbments on a military units only results in 1hp lost." Thread 19648 (warpstorm): "You fire twice. Each shot is tallied up individually."

  • Bombarding a city can damage a unit, kill 1 citizen, or destroy a building. Thread 19648 (Park Ranger) lists "destroy an improvement," "kill 1 population point," and "damage a defending unit"; and "bombarding will not kill citizens if the city is only size 1."

  • Building and citizen defense against bombard: 16 (since 1.17f; BIQ 16). README-V 1.17f: "Building and citizen defense against bombardment increased to 16." BIQ buildingDefensiveBonus 16, citizenDefensiveBonus 16. EDHELP still says "Default is 4" (stale text; see Disputed). Walls add land bombard defense 8 and a Coastal Fortress naval bombard defense 8 (quoted above). How those 8s combine with 16 is UNVERIFIED.

  • Bombard can hit tile improvements outside cities, and PTW+ airfields, outposts and radar towers. README-PTW 1.01f: "You can destroy through bombarding Airfields, Outposts and Radar Towers." README-PTW: "Fixed bug where auto-bombard would not auto-bomb multiple terrain improvements in neutral territory."

  • Ships in port are extra vulnerable (C3C). PEDIA GCON_Bombardment_in_Ports: "naval units "in port" (i.e., in a coastal city square being bombarded) are especially vulnerable to bombardment attacks." The size of the effect is UNVERIFIED.

  • Lethal bombard (editor ability since 1.21f; used by stock units in C3C only). README-V 1.21f: "Editor: Added new unit abilities to allow bombarding to be lethal against land and/or sea units." C3C stock units with it (PEDIA): Cruise Missile, Bomber, Stealth Bomber, Stealth Fighter, F-15, Hwach'a (land and sea); Fighter, Jet Fighter, Dromon (sea only). Examples: PRTO_Bomber: "The Bomber has both Lethal Land and Sea Bombardment." PRTO_Fighter: "The Fighter has Lethal Sea Bombardment." Vanilla/PTW have no lethal units in the stock rules (freeciv thread, Akechi: "If vanilla Civ 3, there is no Lethal Land/Sea Bombardment unit.").

  • Stock bombard units (BIQ, C3C): bombard / range / rate of fire.

    Unit B R RoF
    Catapult 4 1 1
    Trebuchet (C3C) 6 1 1
    Cannon / Hwach'a 8 1 1
    Artillery 12 2 2
    Radar Artillery 16 2 3
    Cruise Missile 16 4 3
    Archer / Bowman 1 0 1
    Longbowman / Musketeer 2 0 1
    Guerilla 3 0 1
    TOW Infantry 6 0 1
    Frigate 3 1 2
    Ironclad / Destroyer / AEGIS Cruiser 6 1 / 1 / 2 2
    Cruiser 7 1 2
    Battleship 8 2 2
    Bomber 12 0 3
    Stealth Bomber 18 0 3

    Range 0 units can only bombard defensively (see next item). Air units use operational range instead of bombard range.

  • Defensive bombard ("Artillery Free Shot"). When a land tile holding a bombard-capable unit is attacked, each such unit (other than the one defending) fires once at the attacker before the battle. README-V ("Changes"): "Artillery Free Shot: When a land tile with a bombard capable unit in it is attacked, that bombard unit will take one free shot at the attacking unit." APO-ZOC (Harovan): "The defense bombard is used once in a turn per unit with this feature and only, if the unit isn't the defender itself." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/zones-of-control.682034/ (Flintlock, #20): "Yes, defensive bombard only uses bombard strength." In C3C, Archers and Longbowmen got a range-0 bombard for this (APO-ZOC, Willem: "As of Conquests, Archer/Longbowmen units now get one as well"). Whether the free shot can kill (with a lethal unit) is UNVERIFIED.

  • Cruise missiles die after bombarding. CFC-RULES: "a unit which, when bombarding, will die after it is done bombarding." PEDIA GCON_Combat: missiles "never return to its base and the attacks can destroy enemy units."

  • Artillery have no attack or defense and must be escorted. PEDIA PRTO_Artillery: "Artillery has no attack or defense value, so they should be protected by an escort." If caught alone they are captured (see 1.13).

  • Bombard uses the attack for the turn. freeciv thread (Akechi): "if unit does not have Blitz ability, this unit can only one attack per turn". Whether bombarding costs all movement is UNVERIFIED.

1.8 Zone of control

  • ZOC does not block movement; it gives a free shot at an enemy moving past. PEDIA GCON_ZOC: "Such units attack enemies that enter the zone of control without initiating a full battle." EDHELP / CFC-RULES: "Determines if the unit will automatically bombard passing units." APO-ZOC (Harovan): "In Civ2 or CtP the movement would be blocked due to ZoC, in Civ3 it is still possible, but the other civ gets a free shot on the passing unit."
  • Units with ZOC (C3C): AEGIS Cruiser, Armies, Cavalry, German Panzer, Mech Infantry, Modern Armor, Mongol Keshik, Ottoman Sipahi, Modern Paratroops, Radar Artillery, Russian Cossack, Spanish Conquistador, Tank, and any unit in a fortress or barricade (PEDIA GCON_ZOC, GCON_Fortresses, GCON_Barricades). Vanilla also lists Marines and (plain) Paratroops and lacks the PTW/C3C unique units (PEDIA-V GCON_ZOC).
  • Trigger: an enemy moves from one tile next to the ZOC unit to another tile next to it. APO-ZOC (geniemalin): the shot triggers only when an enemy moves between two squares adjacent to the unit. https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/110683-how-do-the-zone-of-control-work (CerberusIV): "It only works when an enemy unit moves from a tile adjacent to the fortress to another tile also adjacent to the fortress".
  • Each shot takes 0 or 1 HP and never kills. Thread 682034 (Theov): "The outcome is binary and costs one HP." Ozymandias (same thread, quoting civ-wiki.de): "The control zone has no effect on units that have only one health point."
  • Hit chance (reverse-engineered, C3C): 1 - (D*16 / (D*16 + A)), D = the moving unit's defense with no terrain bonus, A = the higher of the ZOC unit's attack or bombard, using the strongest ZOC unit in the stack. Thread 682034 (Flintlock, #3): "1 - (D16 / (D16 + A))"; "The game will always select the strongest available unit to try to do damage."; Ozymandias: "The defense value of the moving units does not enjoy bonuses from the terrain." This implies low odds (for example A 16 vs D 2: 16/48 = 33%).
  • Walls in a town give ZOC (claim). Thread 682034 (Ozymandias, quoting civ-wiki.de): "All units in a settlement (size 1 to 6) with a city wall automatically have a control zone." UNVERIFIED.

1.9 Movement

  • Terrain costs are in the table in 1.6 (BIQ): 1 for flat land and water, 2 for hills, forest and marsh, 3 for mountains, jungle and volcano. EDHELP Movement Cost: "Determines the base movement cost for a tile of the selected terrain type."
  • Roads: 1/3 movement point per tile regardless of terrain. PEDIA TFRM_Road: "units moving along roads expend one third of a movement point per square, regardless of terrain type." BIQ movementAlongRoads 3.
  • Railroads: zero movement. PEDIA TFRM_Railroad: "Units moving along a railroad expend zero movement points." Railroads need Steam Power plus Iron and Coal (same entry). README-C3C: "Railroads will always appear under captured cities if you have Steam Power."
  • Rivers give no movement bonus in Civ3 (rivers run between tiles). Jon Shafer, in https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/58801-rivers (April 2002, before he joined Firaxis): "Rivers are placed in between spaces in Civ III, unlike in Civ II". A claim in the same thread that roads across rivers need Engineering is UNVERIFIED.
  • Wheeled units can't enter mountains, volcano, jungle or marsh except along a road. PEDIA PRTO_Catapult: "catapults are wheeled so they may not enter $LINK<mountain=TERR_Mountains>, $LINK<volcano=TERR_Volcano>, $LINK<jungle=TERR_Jungle> or $LINK<marsh=TERR_Marsh> terrain unless along a road." BIQ impassableByWheeled is set on exactly those four terrains.
  • "All Terrain as Roads" (Explorer) and "Ignore Movement Cost" per terrain. PEDIA PRTO_Explorer: "unarmed, non-combat units that treat all terrain as if it were a road." EDHELP Ignore Movement Cost: "Units with a terrain movement bonus will ignore movement costs of the selected terrain types." README-V 1.21f: "Updated All Terrain As Roads unit ability to work with water units."
  • Air units ignore terrain costs; water units pay terrain cost. README-V 1.29f: "Changed air units to ignore movement costs of terrain types". README-V 1.21f: "Updated water unit movement to take into account the cost of the terrain type."
  • Naval movement bonuses: Great Lighthouse +1 (PEDIA BLDG_Lighthouse: "all naval unit movement rates are increased by one."), Magellan's +1 (PEDIA BLDG_Circumnavigation: "The movement rate of all naval units is increased by one."), Seafaring trait (PEDIA GCON_Strengths: "Ships move faster and are less likely to sink in the sea or ocean.").
  • Partial moves. A unit with 1/3 move left can still enter any tile. UNVERIFIED (the Civ3 rule for entering a tile with fewer moves than its cost was not found in a source).
  • Using enemy roads. UNVERIFIED (no fetched source states whether units can use roads in enemy territory).
  • Units that win an attack advance into the tile only if it is empty. README-V 1.21f: "Fixed bug involving extra movement costs if the unit can't advance after combat." The exact advance rule is UNVERIFIED.

1.10 Naval units

  • Curragh and Galley must end turns in coast; they risk sinking in sea or ocean. PEDIA PRTO_Galley: "A galley must end each turn in a $LINK<coastal=TERR_Coast> square -- they are in danger of sinking if they end their turn in a $LINK<sea=TERR_Sea> or $LINK<ocean=TERR_Ocean> square. If $LINK<The Great Lighthouse=BLDG_Lighthouse> is built, galleys may safely end turns at sea, but never ocean squares." (PRTO_Curragh has the same rule.)
  • Caravel is safe in sea, at risk in ocean. PEDIA PRTO_Caravel: "may safely traverse $LINK<coast=TERR_Coast> and $LINK<sea=TERR_Sea> squares. If it ends a turn in an $LINK<ocean=TERR_Ocean> square, it is in danger of sinking."
  • The sink flags are per unit: "Sink in Sea" and "Sink in Ocean". EDHELP Unit Abilities: "Sink in Sea Unit dies if it ends its turn on a sea square Sink in Ocean Unit dies if it ends its turn on an ocean square". CFC-RULES: "can possibly sink and die without the right technology". The techs that make sea and ocean safe are given in PEDIA TERR_Sea (Astronomy) and TERR_Ocean (Navigation or Magnetism) for trade and travel; whether those techs remove the sink check for units that have the flag is UNVERIFIED.
  • Sinking chance per turn ended in danger: 50%, or 25% for Seafaring civs (75% survive) (C3C 1.22 test). CFC-SINK: "If you are Seafaring, it's 75% of survival, regardless of difficulty level, terrain, ship, turns spent in water." / "If you are not Seafaring, it's 50%." Tested on curraghs and caravels in ocean, galleys in sea.
  • Ships do not attack land units; they only bombard them (PTW note above: "There are no direct naval attacks on land units -- only bombardment."). Whether a ship can attack a land tile that holds only non-combat units is UNVERIFIED.
  • Amphibious attack: only units with the Amphibious ability attack from a ship onto land (Marines; Viking Berserk). EDHELP Unit Abilities: "Amphibious Unit may attack from a ship onto land". PEDIA PRTO_Marine: "They can unload from a sea $LINK<transport=PRTO_Transport> into any coastal land square, including enemy cities and enemy-occupied squares." PEDIA PRTO_Berserk: "allows them to attack enemy units or structures directly from within sea-based transport vessels." No amphibious attack penalty or bonus was found in any source.
  • C3C: any unit can step off a ship onto a coastal tile that holds only non-combat units and capture them. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/plucking-workers-from-the-coast.72176/ (Fanny Brice, #1): "ALL units can attack enemy workers along the coast right off of transports." Chieftess (moderator, #9): "It's a new feature to prevent the coastal blockades with units and workers, especially in MP games."
  • Landing. Units unloaded onto land can't move again that turn (CFC artillery article: https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/military/how-to-use-artillery-defensively/ says landed enemy units "cannot move until the next turn"). The exact rule is UNVERIFIED.
  • Submarines are invisible except to subs, AEGIS Cruisers and Destroyers. PEDIA PRTO_Submarine: "They remain invisible until they attack a surface ship ... other submarines and $LINK<AEGIS Cruisers=PRTO_AEGIS_Cruiser> and $LINK<Destroyers=PRTO_Destroyer> can see them plainly." README-V: "You no longer declare war by accidentally passing over a hidden submarine."
  • Privateers hide their nationality (attack without declaring war). PEDIA PRTO_Privateer: "allowing them to attack and be attacked [without revealing the nationality] of the ship."
  • Transports. Ships are boarded by moving onto them from shore or loaded in a coastal city (PEDIA GCON_Transport). Transport units can't carry other transports (Mike Breitkreutz, Firaxis, https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/53195-some-questions-for-the-developers-on-editing-and-the-civ3mod-file: "No. Transport units cannot carry other transport units.").
  • What happens to carried units when a ship sinks or loses is UNVERIFIED (presumably they die with it).

1.11 Air units

  • Air units stay in cities, airfields or carriers and fly missions within operational range. PEDIA GCON_Air_Missions: "Air units are not moved around the map like other units. Instead, they are stationed in a city, and assigned missions which they execute immediately against targets within their operational range."
  • Missions (C3C): Air Superiority, Bombing, Precision Strike, Recon, Re-Base.
    • Air Superiority. PEDIA: "The plane attempts to intercept enemy aircraft that attack targets within the inner half of its operational range."
    • Bombing. PEDIA: "The plane flies to the target and attacks, sometimes affecting city improvements, and sometimes population."
    • Precision Strike. PEDIA: "Performed by $LINK<stealth aircraft=PRTO_Stealth_Fighter>, precision strikes destroy city improvements only, unless there are none in which case population is affected." README-V ("Changes"): needs the Smart Weapons advance; "Useful only against cities, this mission targets improvements."
    • Recon. PEDIA: "The plane flies to the target and reveals the map." It can't be intercepted (CFC-RULES: "(Recon and Rebase can not be intercepted)").
    • Re-Base. PEDIA: "a distance equal to up to Six times the plane's operational range."
  • Interception chance: 50% per interceptor, 5% against stealth. EDHELP Chance to Intercept Enemy Air Missions: "Default is 50%." ... Stealth Missions: "Default is 5%." alexman (Firaxis), APO-ZOC #11: "A fighter on air superiority has a 50% chance to intercept a bomber (5% if the bomber is stealth)." / "If it fails to intercept, it can try to intercept the next incoming bomber." / "If it succeeds, it's done intercepting for that turn."
  • Flak and Mobile SAM defend only their own tile. alexman (APO-ZOC #11, as summarized): Flak and Mobile SAMs only defend against aircraft attacking their own tile. SAM Battery: PEDIA BLDG_SAM_Missile_Battery: "[SAM Missile Batteries] attack enemy air units that attempt to attack the SAM site's city." Anti-air stat, BIQ: Flak 2, Mobile SAM 4, AEGIS Cruiser 3, Battleship 2, Destroyer/Cruiser/Carrier 1. How the anti-air stat is rolled is UNVERIFIED.
  • Air units on interception stay on it when their carrier moves (README-V 1.21f).
  • Air and sea units in a captured city are destroyed. CFC-CSE (Xerxes314): "air and sea units caught in a city are automatically destroyed. They have no combat value." README-PTW: "Fixed bug that caused air units to not be destroyed if they are not in a city." Which units this covers exactly is UNVERIFIED.
  • Airfields (PTW+) work like an airport for air units; one use consumes a worker; pillaging destroys them. PEDIA GCON_Airfields: "Airfields function in the same manner that a city with an airport would for air units." / "Airfields can be pillaged like a terrain improvement, which also destroys the airfield."
  • Helicopters carry foot soldiers only, 3 at a time (BIQ capacity 3). Other capacities (BIQ): Transport 6, Galleon 4, Carrier 4 air units, Nuclear Submarine 1 tactical nuke. PEDIA PRTO_Helicopter: "Helicopters may carry foot soldiers only -- no mechanized units allowed." PEDIA GCON_Transport: "To board a helicopter a ground unit must start its turn in a city with the chopper and use the Load command button."
  • Paratroopers airdrop from a city with an Airport (or an airfield, PTW 1.04f). PEDIA PRTO_Paratrooper: "When a paratrooper is active in a city with an $LINK<airport=BLDG_Airport> it may perform an airdrop." README-PTW 1.04f: "Added ability to airdrop from airfields." Drop range is the operational range (BIQ: Paratrooper 6, Modern Paratrooper 8).
  • Air unit stats (BIQ, C3C): Fighter A4 D2 B3 range 6; Jet Fighter A8 D4 B3 range 9; F-15 A8 D4 B6 range 9; Stealth Fighter A8 D6 B6 range 12; Bomber D2 B12 RoF3 range 10; Stealth Bomber D5 B18 RoF3 range 16. How "attack" and "defense" enter air-to-air combat is UNVERIFIED.

1.12 Nuclear weapons

  • Manhattan Project enables nukes for every civ. PEDIA BLDG_Manhattan_Project: "Allows the construction of nuclear weapons by {all} civilizations. Nuclear Weapons can {Not} be built until this Wonder has been completed."
  • Tactical Nuke: bombard range 6, can be carried by nuclear subs. PEDIA PRTO_Nuke: "They can be loaded onto nuclear subs and launched from the sea." / "The bombard range of a Tactical Nuke is 6 squares." Needs Aluminum and Uranium. BIQ: 300 shields.
  • ICBM: unlimited range, can't move from the city that built it. PEDIA PRTO_ICBM: "An [ICBM] ... has an unlimited range" / "They cannot be moved from the city that builds them but can strike any target on the map." BIQ: 500 shields.
  • Using nukes angers the world. PEDIA PRTO_ICBM: "Nuclear weapons, if used, have a tremendous negative effect upon world opinion."
  • SDI (small wonder, needs 5 SAM Batteries): 75% chance to stop incoming nukes. PEDIA BLDG_SDI: "Offers a 75% chance of intercepting enemy ICBMs." EDHELP: "the selected Small Wonder will cause enemy missile attacks to fail 75% of the time." It also stops tactical nukes (https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/135536-strategic-missile-defense, MattPilot: "Infact it shoots down everything with the "nuke" flag.").
  • Blast effects: each unit in the blast has a 50% chance to die, and the city loses half its population. Only source is CFC-CSE (Valant2, vanilla): "Has a 50% chance to destroy each unit caught in its blast area" and a nuke "reduces a city's population by 50%." Blast radius, building losses, fallout pattern: UNVERIFIED.

1.13 Capture, enslavement, pillage

  • Non-combat units caught alone are captured. PEDIA PRTO_Worker: "Note that workers may be captured and used by rival civilizations." Artillery with no escort is captured too (thread https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/122803-siege-city-defense, Kef: "If they get captured, you're doing it wrong."). What a captured Settler becomes (Settler, or Worker(s)) is UNVERIFIED.
  • Captured workers are foreign and work at half speed (C3C). README-C3C: "CAPTURED WORKERS: In order to differentiate a foreign (i.e., captured or enslaved) worker unit, it now displays different unit art than the standard worker." / "Note: Captured workers work at 50% the speed of regular workers."
  • Barbarians destroy the workers and settlers they catch. https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/236659-unusual-barbarians (Quintillus): "They can't build them, and when they capture workers or settlers they are destroyed."
  • Capturing a unit counts as an act of war (UNVERIFIED in a fetched source; one search excerpt says so).
  • The "Capture" unit action only applies to capturable special units (kings, princesses) in game modes outside scope. EDHELP Capture: "the unit has the capability to capture units which are vulnerable to being captured (i.e. king units, princess units, etc.)"
  • Enslave (C3C): 1/3 chance after each win to turn the loser into a set unit type. PEDIA GCON_Enslavement: "A unit with the "enslave" capability has a 1/3 chance of enslaving a defeated unit everytime it wins a battle. ... the unit is turned into another unit type (as specificed in the editor) and is immediately under the control of the victorious player." Stock enslavers: Privateer, English Man-O-War (makes another Man-O-War: PRTO_Man-O-War), Mayan Javelin Thrower (makes a Worker: PRTO_Javelin_Thrower). BIQ slaveUnitType: Worker. Only the attacker enslaves (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/anyone-know-exactly-how-enslavement-works.107253, Willem: "It's the unit that is doing the attacking.").
  • Pillage destroys a tile improvement. EDHELP Pillage: "If checked, the selected unit may pillage terrain improvements." Pillaged barricades fall back to fortresses (PEDIA GCON_Barricades) and pillaged airfields are destroyed (PEDIA GCON_Airfields). Which improvement goes first when a tile has several (for example road, then mine), and the movement cost of pillaging: UNVERIFIED.

1.14 Upgrades

  • Where: ground units in a city with a Barracks, ships with a Harbor, air units with an Airport. PEDIA BLDG_Barracks: "A city with a [Barracks] can be used to upgrade ground units." (BLDG_Harbor, BLDG_Airport say the same for naval and air units.) README-PTW: "Added information about upgrading units to civilopedia entries for Barracks, Harbor, and Airport." The new unit's strategic resources must be available in that city (Tom's Hardware, https://forums.tomshardware.com/threads/upgrades.167686/, "The Stare": "You must have any required strategic resources in that citys' resource display.").
  • Cost = (new shield cost - old shield cost) x multiplier gold. Multiplier 3 in C3C, 2 in vanilla/PTW. EDHELP Unit Upgrade Cost: "if the base unit costs 20 shields and its upgrade costs 40 shields, the difference is 20 shields. Under this example, then, the exact cost for the upgrade would be 60 shields (20 x 3 = 60)." BIQ upgradeCostPerShield 3. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/upgrade-cost.94826 (Ginger_Ale): "times 2 (in Vanilla civ and PtW) and times 3 in Conquests."
  • Leonardo's Workshop halves the cost. PEDIA BLDG_Inventor's_Workshop (all three versions): "Obsolete military units can be upgraded at half price."
  • Experience is kept, except elite drops to veteran (community). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/upgrade-or-disband.231677 (Tone, #13): "elites are dowgraded to vets but all others hold their status."
  • Upgrade All (Shift-U) upgrades every unit of the active unit's type; fortified units stay fortified. README-V: "Upgrade All: To use the Upgrade All order (SHIFT-U) you must activate a unit. All units of this type will then be upgraded." README-V 1.21f: "Upgrade all now leaves fortified units fortified."
  • Unique units are in the normal upgrade chain (README-V 1.17f: "Civ-specific units are now part of the regular upgrade chain."; README-V 1.21f: "War chariots now upgrade to knights.").
  • An upgraded unit heals to full / keeps damage / keeps its moves: UNVERIFIED.

1.15 AI and difficulty

  • Difficulty does not change combat odds except through the barbarian bonus. CFC-CSE (Valant2): the difficulty level "has NO effect on attack/defense calculations." No AI attack or defense bonus field exists in the editor's difficulty tab per CFC-RULES and EDHELP.
  • The AI gets free units, not combat bonuses. Thread 37490 (Pyrkaige, editor table): "Number of extra defensive units: 0 0 0 2 4 8" and "Number of extra offensive units: 0 0 0 1 2 4" (Chieftain..Deity), and "At Monarch and above, the AI civs get extra offensive and defensive units when they start the game."
  • AI barbarian bonus. TheNiceOne (thread 37490): "the AI has still a 200% bonus against barbarians even though the human player loses its entire bonus on deity." This conflicts with EDHELP's "any player (human or AI)" (see Disputed).

Disputed

  1. Stacking of defense bonuses: additive vs multiplicative. EDHELP calls the fortify, river and fortress bonuses "cumulative" and Valant2 (CFC-CSE) says "Bonus percentages add". vulture (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/64407-civ-3-combat-calculations-with-retreat-odds): "I believe the factors combine together as I've done above, rather than adding together to give a 70% increase". The manual (p. 110, via royfurr in thread 7886) says terrain is figured "BEFORE THE CITY SIZE AND WALLS TAKE EFFECT", which some read as compounding. Additive has the Firaxis help text behind it.
  2. Fortify inside walls or a fortress. EDHELP says cumulative. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/defense-bonuses-question.21715 (search excerpt): "the wall bonus supercedes the fortification bonus" and "in a fortress, there is no point in Fortifying." A related claim is that units in cities count as fortified automatically. Neither claim was confirmed.
  3. Promotion against barbarians. Theoden (thread 115547): promotion odds are halved against barbarians. Catt (thread 80836): against barbarians "the promotion odds do not apply" (read as: no promotion).
  4. Army cost. BIQ (C3C): 400 shields. CivFanatics FAQ v3.0: "200 shields". Probably a version difference (vanilla vs C3C); vanilla BIQ was not checked.
  5. Do armies retreat? https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/are-armies-fast-units.84239/ (Longasc): "No, Armies NEVER retreat even if they have increased movement." Chieftess: "Armies DO retreat, atleast in C3C." README-V says an army inherits "mobility" only when all its units have it.
  6. Fast defender retreat. boogaboo (thread 100878) says the retreat bonus works on defense too. Offa (thread 104978) says "Zero chance if defender has 1 HP, or fast defender", which may mean "against a fast defender".
  7. Retreat formula. Offa's att / (def + 50) vs BomberEscort's ranges, which don't fit that formula exactly. The game's code is not known.
  8. Building and citizen bombard defense: 16 or 4. README-V 1.17f and BIQ say 16. EDHELP still says "Default is 4" (likely text left over from before 1.17f).
  9. Barbarian bonus for the AI. EDHELP: the bonus applies to "any player (human or AI)" at the game's difficulty. TheNiceOne (thread 37490): the AI always uses the Regent value (200%).
  10. Leonardo's Workshop. Civ3's Civilopedia says it halves upgrade costs (all versions). A CFC post (Randy, thread 94826) says "Militaristic Halves the upgrade cost of units", and some posters describe free upgrades (Civ2-style). Trust the Civilopedia; the militaristic claim is unconfirmed.
  11. ZOC hit chance. Harovan (APO-ZOC): the shot always fires and works like a normal attack. Willem: "more like 30%". Flintlock's reverse-engineered 1 - 16D/(16D + A) (thread 682034) is the most specific.
  12. Seafaring and sinking. CFC-SINK found survival depends only on the trait (50% vs 75%). Older posts tie it to sea vs ocean squares; the unit's Sink in Sea / Sink in Ocean flags decide where it can sink, the trait decides how often.

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • The per-round formula A/(A+D) and 1 HP per round are community-derived. No Firaxis statement was found. Rounding of modified strengths is unknown.
  • Which unit defends a stack, and how ties are broken.
  • Exact promotion chances (no BIQ field). The 1/2, 1/4, 1/8 table comes from one forum post; Catt's 1/8 vs 1/4 vet-to-elite figure is attributed second-hand to Soren Johnson.
  • Leader rules: the 1/16, 1/12 and half-on-defense figures are community numbers. Whether a unit marked "*" can make a second leader. How "one leader at a time" fits with "more than one leader in one game turn".
  • Whether the retreat roll uses base movement or moves left to decide "fast", and whether a retreating attacker keeps its remaining moves.
  • Whether defensive bombard can kill with a lethal unit, and how Walls' bombard defense 8 combines with the base 16.
  • Size of the "Bombardment in Ports" vulnerability.
  • Which units can fortify; whether units in cities get the fortify bonus automatically.
  • Movement: entering a tile with fewer moves left than its cost; using enemy roads and railroads; river crossing costs (none found); whether roads over rivers need a tech.
  • Naval: whether Astronomy/Navigation/Magnetism remove the sink check for units flagged Sink in Sea / Sink in Ocean; what happens to cargo when a transport dies; whether ships can attack a coastal tile holding only non-combat units; whether units that land from ships lose their remaining moves.
  • Air: how attack, defense and anti-air values are rolled in interceptions and against Flak/SAM; whether a stealth fighter can intercept (MoonWolf, thread 86619: "stealth fighters haven't the ability to shoot down incoming bombers/fighters").
  • Nukes: blast radius, the 50% unit kill and 50% population loss (single vanilla-era source), building damage, fallout, launch restrictions.
  • Capture: what a captured Settler becomes; whether capturing counts as declaring war; whether a Leader caught alone is captured or killed.
  • Pillage: order of improvements removed and movement cost.
  • Upgrades: does an upgraded unit keep damage and moves; is the elite-to-veteran drop real.
  • Town walls giving ZOC (civ-wiki.de claim only).
  • Web research stopped when the session's web-search budget ran out. CivFanatics forum threads were also rate-limited (HTTP 429), so several threads found by search were never opened: "city defense discussion" (689330), "Fortress vs. Fortifications" (287626), "Defensive artillery in city" (329600). They may settle items above.

Back to the top

2. City economy

How a Civ3 city turns tiles into food, shields and commerce, and what eats them. Scope is the base game, PTW and Conquests at their final patches (1.29f / PTW 1.27f / C3C 1.22). Scenarios, mods and the special Conquests game modes are out of scope.

Source key

Local sources come from the installed Civ3 Complete (Steam) and are quoted exactly:

  • [PEDIA] - Conquests Civilopedia, Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt (C3C 1.22). Cited by entry key, e.g. #GCON_Food.
  • [BIQ] - default C3C rules, Conquests/conquests.biq, read with the repo's QueryCiv3 reader. Values are raw BIQ fields. Shield costs are not quoted from the BIQ here: the building cost field as read (e.g. Library 8) does not match the in-game cost (80), so the reader layout for that field is unconfirmed.
  • [EDHELP] - C3C editor help, Conquests/Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm, cited by topic.
  • [ED-SUPP] - Conquests/EditorSupplement.txt.
  • [RM129] - vanilla patch notes, README.txt (sections 1.16f to 1.29f).
  • [RMPTW] - civ3PTW/readme.txt.
  • [RMC3C] - Conquests/readme.txt (v1.20 and v1.22 notes).

Web sources are cited by URL. A fetch tool extracted their quotes, so they may differ slightly from the page text. Spot-check any web quote before treating its exact wording as load-bearing. The corruption formulas are transcribed from alexman's article and are worth re-checking against the page before implementation.

1. Tile yields

  • Base terrain yields (C3C defaults). Each worked tile gives its terrain's food/shields/commerce plus improvement bonuses. BIQ values:

    Terrain Food Shields Commerce Irrigate Mine Road Cities allowed
    Grassland 2 0 0 +1 +1 +1 yes
    Plains 1 1 0 +1 +1 +1 yes
    Desert 0 1 0 +1 +1 +1 yes
    Flood Plain 3 0 0 +1 0 +1 yes
    Tundra 1 0 0 0 +1 +1 yes
    Hills 1 1 0 0 +2 +1 yes
    Mountains 0 1 0 0 +2 +1 no
    Forest 1 2 0 0 0 +1 yes
    Jungle 1 0 0 0 0 +1 yes
    Marsh 1 0 0 0 0 +1 no
    Volcano 0 3 0 0 0 0 no
    Coast 1 0 2 - - - no
    Sea 1 0 1 - - - no
    Ocean 0 0 0 - - - no

    Source: [BIQ] TERR records. The Civilopedia tables agree on the land values, e.g. [PEDIA] #GCON_Food: "Floodplains 3 4 if irrigated. ... Hills 1 cannot be irrigated." and #TFRM_Mine: "Hills 1 shields, 3 if mined. ^Mountains 1 shields, 3 if mined." The water values are under UNVERIFIED (open question 1).

  • Bonus grassland (grassland "with shield") gets +1 shield. Source: [PEDIA] #TERR_Grassland_with_Shield: "These have a rock outcropping to symbolize this and produce a $LINK<shield=GCON_Shields> if worked in a $LINK<city radius=GCON_Radius>." [PEDIA] #TFRM_Mine: "w/ outcropping 1 shields, 2 if mined."

  • Rivers add +1 commerce to every tile they border and supply fresh water for irrigation. Source: [PEDIA] #TERR_River: "Any square with a river along its edge produces one extra $LINK<commerce=GCON_Commerce> if worked by citizen laborers within a $LINK<city radius=GCON_Radius>."

  • Irrigation needs a river, a lake or an irrigated tile next to it, until Electricity. Source: [PEDIA] #TFRM_Irrigation: "Only squares adjacent to fresh water (river or lake) or to another irrigated square may be irrigated. After the discovery of $LINK<Electricity=TECH_Electricity>, you can irrigate a tile without proximity to fresh water."

  • Roads give +1 commerce on all the land terrains listed (per-terrain RoadBonus). Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Commerce table, e.g. "Grasslands 0 1 with road. ... Forests 0 1 with road." [BIQ] RoadBonus=1 for every land terrain except Volcano.

  • Railroads add to irrigation or mine output. Neither source gives the exact amount (UNVERIFIED, open question 2). Source: [PEDIA] #TFRM_Railroad: "Railroads also increase the output of $LINK<irrigation=TFRM_Irrigation> or $LINK<mines=TFRM_Mine>."

  • Bonus resources on a tile add (food/shields/commerce). From [BIQ] GOOD records (type 0 = bonus, 1 = luxury, 2 = strategic):

    Resource F/S/C Resource F/S/C Resource F/S/C
    Cattle (0) 2/1/0 Wheat (0) 2/0/0 Game (0) 2/0/0
    Fish (0) 2/0/1 Whales (0) 1/1/2 Oasis (0) 2/0/0
    Gold (0) 0/0/4 Sugar (0) 1/0/1 Tobacco (0) 0/0/1
    Tropical Fruit (0) 1/0/1 Gems (1) 0/0/4 Silks (1) 0/0/3
    Ivory (1) 0/0/2 Incense (1) 0/0/2 Spices (1) 0/0/2
    Furs (1) 0/1/1 Dyes (1) 0/0/1 Wines (1) 1/0/1
    Horses (2) 0/0/1 Iron (2) 0/1/0 Saltpeter (2) 0/0/1
    Coal (2) 0/2/1 Oil (2) 0/1/2 Rubber (2) 0/0/2
    Aluminum (2) 0/2/0 Uranium (2) 0/2/3

    Source: [BIQ] GOOD. Patch history: [RM129] 1.17f: "Game bonus resource now gives +2 food to its tile."

  • Harbor: +1 food on Coast/Sea/Ocean tiles. Offshore Platform: +1 shield on them. Commercial Dock (C3C): +1 commerce on water tiles that already make 1. Source: [PEDIA] #BLDG_Harbor: "Coastal, sea, and ocean squares produce one extra $LINK<food=GCON_Food>." #BLDG_Offshore_Platform: "causes all worked coastal, sea, and ocean squares to produce one $LINK<shield=GCON_Shields>." #BLDG_Commercial_Dock: "produce one extra Commerce in every water square that already produces one."

  • Colossus: +1 commerce in its city's tiles that already make at least 1. Source: [PEDIA] #BLDG_Colossus: "causes its city to produce one extra $LINK<Commerce=GCON_Commerce> in every square that already produces at least one."

  • Lakes: [PEDIA] says a fresh-water lake gives 2 food ("Fresh Water Lake 2 cannot be irrigated."). [RMPTW] says lakes are not their own terrain type: "Fresh water lakes are not a terrain type. They are composed of coastal tiles surrounded by land." So a lake tile is a Coast tile flagged as fresh water. Its food is under UNVERIFIED (open question 1).

  • City radius: tiles within 2 squares of the city that are also inside its cultural borders. Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Radius: "within the city's $LINK<cultural borders=GCON_Territory>, and ^* within two squares of the city."

Government tile modifiers

  • Despotism (and Anarchy) tile penalty: any tile yield above 2 is cut by 1. Both governments carry the BIQ flag. Sources:
    • [PEDIA] #GOVT_Despotism: "any city production square which produces more than two $LINK<food=GCON_Food>, $LINK<shields=GCON_Shields>, or $LINK<commerce=GCON_Commerce> in a despotic government instead produces one less."
    • CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "In Despotism, the standard government, all tiles that produce 3 or more food/shields/commerce will produce one less." Also: "That's why grassland (2 food), when irrigated (+1 food) in Despotism, will only produce 2 food (2 + 1 – 1 = 2)."
    • [BIQ] GOVT TilePenalty=1 for both Anarchy and Despotism, 0 for all others.
    • The editor's wording differs (see Disputed): [EDHELP] "Flags (Government Properties)": "Standard tile penalty Under the selected government, no tile may generate more than 2 food and shields."
  • The penalty still applies during a Golden Age. Source: CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "The bonuses from a Golden Age, however, are still subject to the Despotism tile penalty."
  • Republic and Democracy trade bonus: +1 commerce on tiles. Sources:
    • [BIQ] TradeBonus=1 for Republic and Democracy only.
    • CFC Civilopedia mirror, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/civilopedia/governments/ (Republic, Democracy): "Adds 1 commerce to any tile already producing at least 1".
    • [EDHELP] "Flags (Government Properties)": "Standard trade bonus Under the selected government, each tile generates an additional +1 to commerce." This wording omits the "already producing 1" condition (see Disputed).

City center tile

  • Civ traits change the center tile. Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Strengths:

    • Agricultural: "the center city square and irrigated deserts produce one more food."
    • Commercial: "The center city squares of all cities and metros produce extra $LINK<commerce=GCON_Commerce>, and less $LINK<corruption=GCON_Corruption> is experienced."
    • Industrious: "the center city square of all cities produces extra $LINK<shields=GCON_Shields> in cities and metros."
    • Seafaring: "Each city built along the coast receives a commerce bonus in the center city square".

    C3C Agricultural detail, from https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn): "Provides one bonus food in the city square in all governments except despotism." and "In despotism, the bonus food only appears if the city is by fresh water."

  • A city tile normally gets a road. It gets a railroad when the owner has Steam Power and the city is captured. Sources:

    • [RM129] 1.29f: "Changed city code so that a road/railroad is not placed under a city if a road/railroad is not allowed on the terrain type the city is built upon."
    • [RMC3C] 1.20: "Railroads will always appear under captured cities if you have Steam Power."

  • Free irrigation effect and minimum 1 shield on the center tile: UNVERIFIED. The only sources found are a Civ2-forum thread (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/food-shield-trade-of-city-tile.535361/: "in a city you automatically have irrigation and roads", "if the city center does not already produce a shield, one will be produced"), so they cannot be used for Civ3.

  • Founding on forest/jungle clears it: disputed and unverified. https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes (inca911): "When you found a city on a forest or jungle square, that square is immediately cleared to grassland." Vipsanius disputes the result terrain in the same thread.

  • Settling on a food bonus loses it. Source: CFC City Placement (C3C), https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/empire-management/city-placement/: "If you settle on food bonuses, you lose the food bonus."

2. Food, growth and city size

  • Each citizen eats 2 food per turn. Source: [BIQ] FoodConsumptionPerCitizen=2. [PEDIA] #GCON_Food: "[Every citizen must eat two food per turn or die.]"
  • Growth: surplus food fills the food box. When the box is full it empties and the city gains 1 citizen. Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Food: "When it is full, the storage is emptied and the city grows, adding one citizen to its population."
  • Food box sizes: 20 for sizes 1-6 (town), 40 for 7-12 (city), 60 for 13+ (metropolis). No editor field exists for these, so they appear to be hard-coded.
    • Realms Beyond micromanagement guide (1.17f), http://www.realmsbeyond.net/civ/mmguide.html: "At and below six food boxes are 20 squares, while they're 40 from size 7-12."
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/granaries-dont-work.53078/ (MadScot, 1.29f): "A size 5 city needs 20 food to grow" and a size 7 city "needs a full 40 to gro to size 8".
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/pottery-is-underrated.683382/ (Spoonwood): "But, the box changes from 40 total food to 60 total food iirc." and "that test confirms that changing from size 12 to 13 keeps 20 food in the box".
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/is-there-a-way-to-manipulate-the-number-of-accumulated-food-required-for-city-growth.338390/ (Czulu): "However i cant find that option in civilization 3 conquests editor."
  • AI cities' boxes are scaled by the difficulty cost factor. The human factor is 10. AI factors are Chieftain 20, Warlord 12, Regent 10, Monarch 9, Emperor 8, Demigod 7, Deity 6, Sid 4 ([BIQ] DIFF CostFactor). Source: [EDHELP] "Cost Factor (AI Bonuses)": "Determines the cost factor that is applied to AI growth, shields, and research. The player's cost factor is always 10."
  • Granary: when the city grows, only half the box is used up, so it keeps half its food. The Pyramids give a Granary in every city on the continent (C3C). Sources:
    • [PEDIA] #BLDG_Granary: "effectively doubles the rate of a city's growth by causing only half of a city's $LINK<food store=GCON_Food> to be depleted when the city grows."
    • [BIQ] Granary flag DoublesCityGrowthRate.
    • [PEDIA] #BLDG_Pyramids: "Puts a $LINK<Granary=BLDG_Granary> in every city on the same continent."
    • https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/empire-management/the-case-for-food-or-how-to-become-economically-dominant/: "A town size 6 or below has a food box of 10 when the Granary is full."
  • Granary at the 6→7 and 12→13 steps: disputed (see Disputed). The best-tested statement is that the granary keeps half of the previous box (10 food at 6→7, 20 food at 12→13) inside a box that has grown larger. Source: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/pottery-is-underrated.683382/ (Spoonwood): "The granary keeps half of the food in the box that it had at the previous size." Patch history: [RM129] 1.17f: "Fixed infinite city growth exploit (involving granaries and size 6 cities)."
  • Starvation: if production plus stored food cannot feed everyone, one citizen starves. The population number shows red while food is negative. Sources:
    • [PEDIA] #GCON_Food: "If a city cannot feed its population either from new production or stored food, one of its citizens {starves} and disappears."
    • CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "if it is red, the city is losing food and citizens are starving."
    • Resisters do not eat. [RM129] 1.21f: "Corrected Resistance Civilopedia chapter to reflect resisting citizens do not consume food."
  • Civil disorder stops food storage, production and revenue. Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Disorder: "^* $LINK<Production=GCON_Shields> ceases, ^* No revenue comes in from $LINK<commerce=GCON_Commerce>, and ^* No excess $LINK<food=GCON_Food> is stored."
  • Size limits: a town (size 1-6) needs an Aqueduct to pass 6, unless it is next to fresh water. A city (7-12) needs a Hospital to pass 12, and the Hospital needs size 7 or more. Shakespeare's Theatre also has the "allows level 3" flag. No cap is listed for metropolises. Sources:
    • [BIQ] RULE MaximumLevel1CitySize=6, MaximumLevel2CitySize=12, level names Town/City/Metropolis; Aqueduct AllowsCitySize2; Hospital and Shakespeare's Theatre AllowsCitySize3.
    • [PEDIA] #BLDG_Aqueduct: "A city with an [Aqueduct] can grow beyond population six. ^Note that a city adjacent to fresh water never requires an aqueduct."
    • [PEDIA] #BLDG_Hospital: "A city with a [Hospital] can grow beyond population 12. You must have a population of 7 or higher to build a hospital."
    • CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "need a Hospital (available with Sanitation) to grow past size 12."
  • No food carryover beyond the box. Source: CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "No – there is no shield or food carryover for cities, just like there is no beaker carryover when researching a technology."

3. Shields, production and switching

  • Shield cost = BIQ cost × cost factor (human 10, AI per difficulty). Source: [EDHELP] "Cost (Improvement Properties)": "Note that this value is multiplied by a cost factor which determines how many shields it actually costs to build the selected improvement/wonder. The human players cost factor is always 10."
  • Production multipliers are a percentage of base shields and add together. BIQ Production is in 25% steps: Factory 2 (+50%), Manufacturing Plant 2, Coal/Hydro/Solar 2, Nuclear 4 (+100%), Iron Works 4. One power plant per city. Sources:
    • [EDHELP] "Production (Improvement Properties)": "in multiples of 25% (so if the value is set at 2, the building gives +50% total shield production)."
    • [PEDIA] #BLDG_Factory: "increases $LINK<shield production=GCON_Shields> in its city by 50%."
    • https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/everything-you-wanted-to-know-about-power-plants/ (PTW 1.21f): Nuclear "adds a 100% production bonus to base shields". Its example: 32 base + 16 (factory) + 16 (plant) + 16 (Manufacturing Plant) = 80. Hoover Dam gives a Hydro Plant to every city on the continent ([PEDIA] #BLDG_Hoover_Dam).
  • Waste comes off shields first (see section 5).
  • No shield overflow. Surplus shields beyond the item's cost are lost. Sources:
    • CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "If you have extra shields being produced then are needed, they'll just go to waste."
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/short-rushing.119305 (SJ Frank): "then you end up wasting 20 shields of production on the next turn."
  • Wealth turns shields into gold. Disputed rate (see Disputed). Sources:
    • [PEDIA] #BLDG_Wealth: "[Wealth] causes shields to be converted to gold at a ratio of four to one. ^When your civilization learns $LINK<Economics=TECH_Economics> the ratio is reduced to two to one."
    • [BIQ] ShieldsCostPerGold=4; Wealth has the Capitalization flag.
  • Mobilization (Nationalism) gives +1 shield on tiles making at least 1 while building military units, and only military units and improvements can be built. Source: [PEDIA] #GCON_Mobilization: "During mobilization a city building a military unit produces one extra $LINK<shield=GCON_Shields> in every square that is already producing at least one."
  • Clearing a forest sends 10 shields to the nearest city's build, once per tile per game, and never to a wonder. Sources:
    • [BIQ] ForestValueInShields=10.
    • [RM129] 1.16f: "Shield bonus from clearing forest can only be received once per game."
    • CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "When you chop them down, 10 shields will be added to the current thing being built in the nearest city (provided it isn't a wonder)".
  • Civil Engineer (C3C, Replaceable Parts) gives +2 shields, for improvements and wonders only. Sources:
    • [BIQ] CTZN Construction=2.
    • [EDHELP] "Construction Bonus (Citizen Properties)": "Applies only while building improvements and wonders (never to units)."
  • Production switching penalty between categories (unit / improvement / wonder): UNVERIFIED. No Civ3 source confirming a percentage penalty was found (open question 3). The rules that are confirmed:
    • A hurried item cannot be switched to a wonder. CFC FAQ, https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ ("Why can't I build a wonder" entry). The page states it as: if you hurried production on an item, the game "won't let you" switch it to a wonder (wording as extracted).
    • Shields from disbanding cannot go into a wonder. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/disbanding-units-obtaining-extra-shields.118679 (Renata): "you won't be able to switch to a wonder until the current build completes."
