Tassadar, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/we-love-the-king-days.34249/: "it cut down shield waste like a courthouse but it doesnt cut down commerce corruption."
  • Celebration benefits by government. The manual says "the city enjoys certain benefits, depending on your civilization's type of government", but neither it nor the Civilopedia lists any government-specific benefit. The C3C editor has no celebration-conditional government flag.
  • Content or happy faces from wonders.
    • Hanging Gardens: the manual says "Makes 3 content citizens happy in the city where it's built and 1 in all others"; the C3C Civilopedia says "makes three unhappy citizens content … and one unhappy citizen content in all other friendly cities".
    • Mausoleum: the manual says "3 happy faces"; the Civilopedia says "makes three unhappy citizens content".
    • Cure for Cancer: the C3C wonders guide (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/empire-management/a-guide-to-great-wonders-and-how-to-use-them/) says "1 happy citizen in every city"; the Civilopedia says "one unhappy citizen content".
  • Wonder culture values. CFC's Civilopedia page (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/civilopedia/wonders/, probably the base game) gives Great Library 6, Sistine Chapel 6, Shakespeare 6, Newton 6, Cure for Cancer 4. The Complete manual chart gives 5, 4, 5, 5, 5. This is likely a version difference.
  • How war weariness ends. The Civilopedia says "the war weariness remains until the war stops". The CFC article measures a decay of 1/20 per peace turn, so war weariness lingers after peace ("43 turn until the war is totally forgotten").
  • Distance for flips. Pathfinding distance (Magaha, hedged) vs straight-line max+½min distance (the calculators).
  • Where Cte/Cty applies in the flip formula (see the table above).

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • The number of luxury points (slider) per happy face, and whether the default Marketplace carries the "+50% Luxury Output" flag.
  • The entertainer's output in luxury points (the CTZN Luxuries default). The Civilopedia says only "one happy face".
  • The order in which the game applies faces (base, luxuries and entertainers, buildings, military police, wonders, war weariness). Forum posts give an order, but none could be sourced verbatim.
  • The "reduced efficiency" of carried-over happy faces.
  • How many citizens become unhappy from "mother country" aggression. Forum guesses range from "one more unhappy" to "about 50%" of the foreign nationals.
  • The per-government AssimilationChance and resistance modifier defaults. Firaxis gives only "between a 1 and 4 percent".
  • How much each garrison unit counts toward quelling resistance.
  • War weariness under Feudalism ("Low"): is its level table the same as Republic's? And exactly how Police Stations and Universal Suffrage reduce it ("25%" and "1" per the CFC article).
  • How long disorder must last before improvements are destroyed (beyond the 20% riot chance) or the government falls.
  • The exact border shape at each radius level, and the tie-break rule for contested tiles between two cities.
  • What the civ-wide culture levels (Lvl. Multiplier: 1000, 2000 … 32000) do besides the labels.
  • How much AI attitude depends on cultural opinion.
  • The "less than half the leader's culture" condition for culture victory.
  • The flip-related items in the table above: the order of rival rolls, capitals, the free defender's type, propaganda as a flip modifier, and integer truncation of the culture ratio.
  • Which nationality a drafted unit or citizen has, and whether foreign nationals are drafted or sacrificed first.
  • Whether specialists count as content for the WLTKD "happy vs content" test.

Back to the top

4. Science, techs and wonders

This section covers research cost, beakers, eras, tech trading, the AI's research choices, free-tech sources, and all Great and Small Wonders in Conquests (C3C 1.22, with Vanilla 1.29f / PTW 1.27f differences where sources give them). Scenarios, mods and the special C3C game modes are out of scope.

Source key. Local files read for this section, from the Steam "Civilization III Complete" install (.../Sid Meier's Civilization III Complete/):

  • BIQ = Conquests/conquests.biq (C3C 1.22 default rules), read with this repo's QueryCiv3 (BLDG, TECH, RULE, WSIZ, DIFF sections). Building costs are stored at 1/10 scale ("In Civ3 files, building costs are stored at 1/10th of their actual value", C7Engine/C7GameData/ImportCiv3.cs). Shield costs below are BIQ x 10.
  • Pedia = Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt (Firaxis' C3C Civilopedia). This is not OpenCiv3's C7/Civilopedia/Text/Civilopedia.txt, which is our own text.
  • README-V = README.txt (Vanilla patch notes up to 1.29f). README-PTW = civ3PTW/readme.txt. README-C3C = Conquests/readme.txt. EdSupp = Conquests/EditorSupplement.txt.

4.1 Research cost

  • Formula (Vanilla 1.29f, reverse-engineered). Source: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/research-cost-formula-v1-29f.29485/ — "Research Cost = [MM * [10COST * (1 - N/[CL1.75])]/(CF * 10)] - Research done so far". The thread also gives a simplified form: "Research cost = DOWN{MMDM(COST/100) [1 - (N/(CL1.75))]} - Research done so far." The CivFanatics FAQ repeats the first form: https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "Research Cost = [MM * [10COST * (1 – N/[CL1.75])]/(CF * 10)] – Research done so far".
    • MM (map size factor) — "MM = map modifier(tech rate on world sizes tab in the editor)". BIQ WSIZ tech rate: Tiny 160, Small 200, Standard 240, Large 320, Huge 400. These match the thread's values.
    • COST — "COST = technology cost as on the civilization advances tab in the editor." The BIQ cost is the base (for example Bronze Working 3, Integrated Defense 360).
    • Check on a Standard map. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-will-the-ai-research-next.45559/ — "For a standard map (tech rate = 240), the AI needs 24 times the base cost of each technology in beakers." alexman's example: "So if Bronze Working were an empty tech, it would have a value of 256/18=14", where 18 = 72 beakers / 4 per turn.
    • N, CL ("civs that know it" discount) — "N = number of civs on the diplomacy screen that have discovered the tech." "CL = number of civs left in the game". Only contacted civs count toward N. Version note from the same thread: "CL1.5 in 1.21f is now CL1.75 in 1.29f." The 1.21f thread gives a different rounding form: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-trading-valuation.21925/page-2 — "Research cost = MMCOST/10 - UP{MM/100 * UP[COST10N/DOWN(CL*1.5)]} - Research done so far".
    • CF / DM (difficulty) — "CF = AI cost factor(as on the difficulty tab in the editor)" and "For the purposes of the research cost formula, CF has a maximum value of 10." Qitai's correction in the same thread: "Cost factor = {20, 12, 10, 9, 8, 6} for the various difficulty level", which gives DM {10, 10, 10, 11.1, 12.5, 16.7}. The author confirms: "the Monarch figure is 11.1". BIQ DIFF cost factors in C3C: Chieftain 20, Warlord 12, Regent 10, Monarch 9, Emperor 8, Demigod 7, Deity 6, Sid 4. Implementation reading: research cost x 10/min(CF,10), so x1.0 at Regent and below, x1.11 Monarch, x1.25 Emperor, x1.43 Demigod, x1.67 Deity, x2.5 Sid. The Demigod and Sid values are an UNVERIFIED extrapolation, because the thread predates PTW's extra levels.
    • Whom it applies to. Same thread: "This formula is relevant for research cost of the AI civs, not just the human player" and "techs are now more expensive for the human player on Monarch, Emperor and Deity." See Disputed, item 1.
  • Future techs. BIQ RULE: Future Tech Cost = 400. The editor field was added in Vanilla (README-V: "Editor: On General Settings, added Future Tech Cost."). How 400 enters the formula is UNVERIFIED.
  • Integer rounding. Commerce-to-beaker conversion floors, so some beakers are lost. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-research-slider.662300 (vorlon_mi) — "Some of those calculations use the "FLOOR" function, to produce an integer result that drops the fractional parts."

4.2 Minimum and maximum research time

  • C3C: 4 to 50 turns. BIQ RULE: MinimumResearchTime = 4, MaximumResearchTime = 50. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "For all three games, the minimum research length is 4 turns." and "For Conquests, that was upped to 50 turns as the maximum."
  • Vanilla history. README-V (1.16f): "The maximum number of turns it can take to research an advance has been increased from 32 to 40." README-V (1.29f): "Editor: On General Settings, added Min. Tech Turns." / "added Max. Tech Turns."
  • What the maximum means. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-research-slider.662300 (justanick) — "The time for researching a tech is limited to 4 turn as the minimum and 50 turns as the maximum." and "You cannot use up more than 50 turns of actual research, no matter how little you spend". jarred! in the same thread: "it'll still take the 50 turn max to finish a 200 beaker tech". Reading: as long as at least some research is funded, the tech completes on turn 50 at the latest.
  • What the minimum means. https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406 (David Weldon) — "Yes, there is a minimum of 4 turns for a tech advance, no matter how much overkill you use." See Disputed, item 2. Whether beakers above the 4-turn pace are lost or banked is UNVERIFIED.
  • Slider floor. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-research-slider.662300 (vmxa) — "at the start you cannot drop below 20% and research, unless you have a scientist running." Treat this as UNVERIFIED as a rule. It probably reflects the 50-turn cap, since research has to be fast enough to beat 50 turns.

4.3 Beaker overflow and loss

  • Excess from the completing city is lost; later cities feed the next tech. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/a-few-questions.225457 (TimTheEnchanter) — "The cities are processed one at a time in reverse order of their creation." and "Any excess beakers from the city that gets you to the next tech are lost." ElephantU, same thread: cities processed after the completing one add their research to the new tech.
  • Simpler player statements (all extra lost). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/are-extra-science-beakers-wasted.63391 (Rik Meleet) — "When a tech is researched all extra beakers are lost." https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "there is no shield or food carryover for cities, just like there is no beaker carryover when researching a technology." See Disputed, item 3.
  • Measured loss. The overflow mod's tracker found about 5% of beakers lost: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/mod-tech-overflow.398296/latest — "generated 7,562 research and lost 375 (4.96%)" (as reported in search results; not re-fetched).
  • Theory of Evolution discards stored beakers. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/theory-of-evolution-toe.60765 (Gengis Khan) — "All beakers stored when the ToE is completed are lost."
  • Research is applied at turn start. Pedia GCON_Research — "At the start of each turn, the science output of each city is added to the research project currently in progress".
  • C3C scientists. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) — "Scientists now produce 3 beakers per turn instead of 1."

4.4 Eras: required vs optional techs

  • Four eras. BIQ ERAS: Ancient Times, Middle Ages, Industrial Ages, Modern Times.
  • Every required tech of the current era must be known before advancing. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/revolutionizing-the-tech-tree-mechanics.102803 (Plotinus) — "a civ cannot progress from one era to another without first researching all the non-essential techs from the earlier one". "Non-essential" is evidently a slip for "required". https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/certain-clarifications.22543 (Mr Spice) — "Yes, you have to possess them, but there are plenty of other ways to accuire techs than to research them on your own."
  • You cannot research into the next era early. The structural evidence is in the BIQ. The first-tier techs of each later era have no prerequisites in the BIQ: Monotheism, Feudalism and Engineering (Middle); Nationalism, Steam Power and Medicine (Industrial); Rocketry, Fission, Computers and Ecology (Modern). The editor forbids cross-era prerequisites. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/revolutionizing-the-tech-tree-mechanics.102803 — GRM7584: "Era-specific techs can only have prereqs in their era, or NONE-era"; Paasky quotes the editor error "The required tech must be in the same era". So the era gate, not prerequisites, is what keeps next-era techs closed. No source says in so many words that you cannot research a next-era tech early, so the exact UI behaviour is an inference.
  • Trading and the Great Library cross eras. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/tech-prerequisites-in-previous-eras.472427/ (Quintillus) — "tech trading and the Great Library techs are always across eras." README-V (1.16f): "Traded advances that cause an era transition, now allows the player to select the advance to research in the new era."
  • Optional techs never block advancement. BIQ flag NotRequiredForEraAdvancement. The editor checkbox is "Not Needed for Era Advancement", and the F6 chart marks these techs: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/73736-what-technologies-don-t-you-need (vondrack) — "the optional techs have a slashed circle in the upper right corner."
  • Era-None techs. README-V: "Editor: Updated ERA_NONE techs to work as expected (you can never research them and they don't impede era advancement but they can be assigned as free techs)." README-PTW: if a civ cannot reach a non-era prerequisite, the techs sprouting from it "will not be required for advancement to the next era."
  • C3C optional techs (BIQ).
Era Techs Optional (not required)
Ancient 21 Literature, The Republic, Monarchy
Middle Ages 22 Chivalry, Printing Press, Music Theory, Democracy, Economics, Navigation, Free Artistry, Military Tradition
Industrial 23 Nationalism, Communism, Sanitation, Espionage, Amphibious War, Advanced Flight, Ironclads, Fascism
Modern 17 none

4.5 Conquests tech-tree differences (vs Vanilla/PTW)

  • Radio removed; Ironclads and Fascism added. README-C3C (1.20): "Radio Tech removed", "Electronics requirement removed from Motorized Transportation", "Civil Defense requires Electronics", "Advanced Flight now requires Electronics", "Radar Towers require Advanced Flight". https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) — Ironclads: "requires Steam Power, required for nothing, allows Ironclads unit, optional tech"; Fascism: "requires Nationalism, required for nothing, allows Fascism Government, optional tech". The BIQ confirms both (Industrial, optional, costs 100 and 130).
  • Philosophy became a flagged bonus tech. Same thread: "Philosophy now gives a bonus tech to the first civ to research it." BIQ: Philosophy has BonusTechToFirstCivThatResearches.
  • Diplomatic tech moves. Same thread (Arathorn): "Map trading moved from available with Map Making to Printing Press." and "Communications trading moved from available with Map Making to Navigation." README-C3C notes MPTournament rules moved map trading back ("MPTournament: Map Trading moved back to Map Making").
  • Tech count. Thunderfall, same thread: "it is now 81 with the removal of Radio". The C3C BIQ has 83 era techs (21+22+23+17). The 81 figure is UNVERIFIED and may be pre-1.22 or may exclude the two new optional techs.
  • Tech purchase price. Same thread (Arathorn): "Changed calculation on tech prices, in terms of gold required to buy." The new formula is UNVERIFIED.

4.6 Tech trading and tech brokering

  • Who can trade a tech. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/revolutionizing-the-tech-tree-mechanics.102803 (Hugin) — general techs "can be traded with any other civilization, as long as that civ has the prerequisite technologies". The BIQ has a CannotBeTraded tech flag; no C3C default tech sets it.
  • AI-to-AI trade rate. BIQ DIFF "AI to AI trade rate", exposed in Vanilla (README-V 1.21f: "Editor: Exposed AI to AI trade rate."). C3C values: Chieftain 110, Warlord 120, Regent 130, Monarch 140, Emperor 150, Demigod 160, Deity 170, Sid 200. Exact semantics are UNVERIFIED; it is presumably the AIs' price discount or willingness among themselves.
  • AI price drops as more civs know a tech; monopolies are dear. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "techs are very expensive to buy or research when no one knows them". The FAQ also names the classic brokering trade: "called a '2fer' or 'xfer' (2 for 1)."
  • Brokering. Buy a tech from one AI and resell it to the others. https://civfanatics.com/?p=171 ("Zero Percent Science") — the author calls himself a "Day Trader" and sells a tech for "a few gold per turn for 20 turns". https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/science-broker-analysis.10012 (Baron23) — "The AI also plays tech broker at times".
  • AIs pass techs on among themselves. https://civfanatics.com/?p=170 — "they'll trade it amongst themselves if you give it to one so give it to all of them at the same time."
  • The AI's trade value differs from its research value. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-will-the-ai-research-next.45559/page-3 (alexman) — "The AI places a value on each tech for trading purposes, which (according to Soren) is actually different". Vanilla also changed this: README-V (1.21f) "Adjusted advance trading rates for AI." The full trade-valuation formula is UNVERIFIED and belongs to the diplomacy section.

4.7 AI research choice (alexman) — already in C7Engine/AI/DevelopmentAI.cs

  • Method. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/what-will-the-ai-research-next.45559/ — "The AI places a value on each technology it can research next. The values depend on the various things allowed by each technology, as well as on the turns needed by that AI civ". It was found "by stripping all technologies from their benefits, adding them back one by one". "These values are cumulative." The values feed a weighted random choice: "techs with higher value were chosen more often" (alexman, quoted in https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/manipulating-ai-tech-research.657856/), and tjs282 there: "still basically just a weighting-factor for a pRNG dice-roll".
  • Values. "/turns" means divided by the turns the AI needs for the tech. Per turn: Empty tech 256, SS component 261, Government 259, Conscription 259, Mobilization 259, No trade 149. Flat: Defender (no resource) 198, Attacker (no resource) 134, Defender (resource required) 70, Naval transport 34, Resource 16, Double worker speed 8, Diplomats 8, MPP 8, ROP 8, Alliances 8, Embargoes 8, Trade over ocean 8, Attacker (resource required) 6, Wonder 6, Irrigation 4, No disease 4, Trade over sea 4, Bridges 2, Double wealth 2, Map trades 2, Communications trade 2, Small wonder 2, Specialist 2, Unit (no A/D/NT) 2, Improvement 2, Other tech 1, Recycling 1, Precision bombing 1, Worker job 1, Bonus tech 0, Reveal map 0, Sacrifices 0. "Optional: divide by 1.5".
  • Worked checks. "Iron Working allows swordsmen and iron, so it has a value of 22 more than if it were just an empty tech". That is Resource 16 + Attacker-needing-resource 6. And "for the Romans who build Legionaries (attacker and defender), Iron Working is worth an additional 70 points!", which is Defender-needing-resource 70.
  • Version. The list dates from Vanilla 1.29f (Bamspeedy cites "Civ3 1.29f"; Qitai on page 2: "the research cost changes is effective vanilla civ3 1.29, not PTW"). Yet it lists Bonus Tech, Reveal Map and Sacrifices, which are PTW-era editor flags. So it was most likely measured with a PTW-capable editor or game. C3C re-validation: UNVERIFIED.
  • "No trade: 149/turns" — best reading. The thread never explains it (WebFetch of pages 1–3: "The thread never explains either entry"). Its place in the list next to the other tech flags suggests the editor's "Cannot Be Traded" tech flag, which exists in the BIQ (TECH.CannotBeTraded, flag byte 2 bit 3). The comment in DevelopmentAI.cs that says "no BIQ tech flag found" should be corrected accordingly. No default C3C tech sets this flag, so leaving it out changes nothing in standard games. It only matters for mods. Interpretation UNVERIFIED.
  • "Other tech: 1" — unresolved. It is not explained anywhere in the thread. It cannot mean "is a prerequisite of another tech": Iron Working is a prerequisite of Construction, yet alexman's Iron Working total is exactly 22 (16 + 6) with no +1. Possible readings: something the tech enables that has no other category, or a tech received for free (Bonus Tech is listed separately at 0). UNVERIFIED. It is worth 1 point, so it is negligible.
  • Other nuances in the thread. Unique units count only when they differ from the unit they replace ("Bowmen... count as both attacker and defender"). Only direct effects count (Sanitation gets nothing for Battlefield Medicine). A benefit counts even if the civ already has it (Magnetism still gets Trade over Ocean). Flavors: Ozymandias, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/manipulating-ai-tech-research.657856/ — "I'm 99% certain that the effect of a Flavor is 5%." This is UNVERIFIED and not in DevelopmentAI.cs. Vanilla also fixed an AI bug: README-V "Fixed AI bug giving value to worthless techs."

4.8 Free techs

Great Library

  • Effect. Pedia BLDG_Great_Library — "The civilization that builds the [Great Library] gets any Civilization Advance already discovered by two other known civilizations for free." BIQ flag GainAnyTechKnownByTwoCivs; cost 400, Literature, obsolete with Education.
  • Only contacted civs count. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/great-library-question.78293 (sealman) — "You get all the techs that you do not have that are known to at least 2 civs that you have contact with." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-the-great-library-work.26689 (Catt) — "If you only know one civ, the Great Library will do nothing for you."
  • Timing. Same thread (robinm) — "The Library checks all civs you have met at the beggining of each of your turns".
  • Obsolescence. Vanilla: README-V "Great Library: This Great Wonder is now obsolete when Education is researched." Obsolescence is checked after the turn's grants. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/strange-tech-glitch.112341 (Renata) — "it is possible to gain techs long past education in circumstances like yours". robinm (26689) — "you continue to recieve all of the other techs the GL made available to you for that turn only."
  • Prerequisites and editor order. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/quick-answers-newbie-questions.318752/page-275 (justanick) — "You can only gain those techs that you have the prerequisite for." "Music theory seems to be one of them, amphibic warfare is another one and possibly space flight,too." "As i recall it the techs are checked in the order in which they are listed in the editor." So within one turn, a tech whose prerequisite is granted later in the list waits a turn. PTW fix (README-PTW): "Fixed a bug with non-era techs that allowed players to potentially receive them for free from the Great Library (NOTE: this also fixes a similar bug that would allow players to receive techs that they do not have the prerequisites for if the order of the techs in the editor was changed)."

Theory of Evolution

  • Effect. Pedia BLDG_Theory_of_Evolution — "Grants two Civilization Advances as soon as completed." BIQ flag TwoFreeAdvances; 600 shields, Scientific Method, never obsolete.
  • Which techs. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/theory-of-evolution-toe.60765 (Yndy) — "First tech is the one you were currently researching." The second is the next one you pick. Turner: "Then you get a pop up box asking you which tech you'd like to research next." Stored beakers are lost (Gengis Khan, quoted in 4.3). See Disputed, item 6 for "random techs".
  • Patch fixes. README-V: "Fixed bug which sometimes caused you to not get any techs from Theory of Evolution." README-PTW: "Fixed bug with free tech popups from Theory of Evolution."

Philosophy (C3C)

  • Effect. Pedia TECH_Philosophy — "{Bonus Tech} The first civilization to research this tech is awarded a free tech." BIQ: only Philosophy carries the bonus flag.
  • The player picks the free tech, from what is researchable at that moment. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/free-tech-tricks.90177 (DaveMcW) — "Finish research or pop Philosophy from a hut, and you can pick any of the Middle Age techs for free!" This is the case where Philosophy was the last required Ancient tech, so the era advanced first. https://civfanatics.com/?p=152 — "If you manage to get Philosophy first, you get Republic as your free tech!"
  • Version note. https://civfanatics.com/?p=152 — "none of them work in PTW." The free-tech tricks are C3C (Philosophy) and Vanilla/C3C (Scientific). The CivFanatics FAQ lists the Philosophy rule among Conquests changes: "Philosphy now grants a free technology if researched first".

Scientific trait

  • Effect. Pedia GCON_Strengths — "Scientific city improvements (like research labs) are easier to build and the civilization receives a free Civilization Advance at the start of every era." https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "Upon entering a new age, they receive a free technology (one of the starting technologies for that age)."
  • Selection is random among the new era's researchable techs, and seems to avoid techs AIs already have. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/free-tech-tricks.90177 (DaveMcW) — the trick yields "a different free tech that none of the AIs have"; with the Russian triple slingshot there is "a 33% chance to learn Education or Gunpowder as your scientific tech". README-PTW: "Improved random free tech selection for the Scientific civ trait." The exact selection rule is UNVERIFIED.
  • Timing. https://civfanatics.com/?p=152 — after the "new era" popup, the free tech shows up once the turn is processed ("check your research screen").

Scientific Great Leaders (C3C)

  • Spawn. Pedia GCON_Leaders — "Each time a technology is researched for the [first] time by any civilization there is a chance that a Scientific leader will arise (this chance is increased slightly if the researching civilization has the "scientific" strength." Rates: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) — "has a 3% chance of appearing if a Civ is the first to discover a tech (5% for Scientific civs)." See Disputed, item 4.
  • Uses. Pedia — "A Scientific Leader can be used to increase the scientific output of all cities of his civilization, thus starting an Age of Science" and "can also be used to hurry the construction of any [improvement] (including a Great Wonder)". Pedia GCON_Age_of_Science — "a 20-turn period in which the scientific research of each city in that civilization's empire is 25% more productive."
  • Age of Science is broken in the shipped game. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) — "this functionality does not work. The button is there, but it does nothing." Post 2 of that thread says the same about 1.22.
  • Rule switch. README-C3C: "Implemented Allow Scientific Leaders Rule". EdSupp: "Allow Scientific Leaders Rule has been added and is active as a Default Rule. With this disabled only militaristic leaders are allowed."

Cost = BIQ cost x 10 (shields, before any production modifiers). TA = can become a Tourist Attraction (BIQ flag, matching the Pedia GCON_Tourist_Attraction list). Effect quotes are from the Pedia BLDG_* entries.

Cross-checks:

  • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ (Arathorn) agrees with the BIQ on the new wonders: Statue of Zeus "200 shields, Mathematics, requires Ivory, 4 cpt, Rel and Mil, produces Ancient Cavalry unit every 5 turns, expires with Metallurgy"; Mausoleum "200 shields, Philosophy, 2 cpt, Sci and Sea, 3 unhappy citizens made content, never expires"; Temple of Artemis "500 shields, Polytheism, 4 cpt, Rel"; Knights Templar "300 shields, Chivalry, 2 cpt, Rel and Mil ... expires with Steam Power". Thunderfall: "There are now 11 SWs and 29 GWs". The BIQ also has 29 GWs and 11 SWs.
  • C3C changes to existing wonders (Arathorn, same thread). Shakespeare's "also allows a city to grow beyond size 12 and now costs 450 shields". Colossus "traits from Expansionist, Commercial, Religious to Expansionist, Seafaring, Commercial". Universal Suffrage "trait from Militaristic to Agricultural". Pyramids and Cure for Cancer "add trait Agricultural". Leonardo "still halves the upgrade cost". SirPleb: "The Great Wall has the new feature of putting walls in every town on the same continent."
  • Vanilla wonder patches (README-V 1.16f). "United Nations Wonder: This Great Wonder is now enabled by Fission." "Art of War: The effects of this Great Wonder are now restricted to the continent it is built on." "Adam Smith's Trading Company: This wonder now pays the maintenance costs for all trade-based city improvements ... instead of all improvements with a maintenance cost of one gold."
  • Tourist attraction income (Pedia GCON_Tourist_Attraction). A wonder qualifies after it has "stood the test of time" and then earns gold: 1000–1500 years +2, 1501–1750 +4, 1751–1875 +6, 1876–2000 +8, 2001–2250 +10, 2251–2500 +12, 2501+ +14. Arathorn: "It starts at 2 gpt after 1000 years and can grow as high as 14 gpt."

4.11 Wonder rules

  • One per world, never rebuilt. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "Great Wonders can only be built once, by one civilization, and only they get the benefit of that wonder." "However, Small Wonders can be built by each civilization". https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/quick-answers.117535/page-32 (Turner) — "once a great wonder is gone, no one can rebuild it." Same thread: small wonders can be rebuilt (paraphrase in the fetch result).
  • Only one city per civ may build a given wonder at a time. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "First off, you can only build a wonder in one city."
  • Wonders cannot be hurried with population, gold or forest chops; a city with rushed shields cannot switch to a wonder. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "you can’t rush a wonder by sacrificing population (Despotism, Communism, Fascism)," / "by rushing it with gold (other governments), or by forest chops." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/partial-rush-build-of-wonders.8145 (Ferd) — "Once you rush something, the wonders are greyed out for that city and you can't switch to them." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/disbanding-question.18365 (Bamspeedy) — with forest or disband shields in the box, "that particular city won't be able to switch to a wonder." Pedia GCON_Hurry_Production only mentions "improvements or units".
  • Switching shields between wonders is free. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/building-wonders.8756 (Ruffin) — "switch all the production over to the new wonder (no penalty in Civ3)". This is the standard "prebuild" (Palace or another wonder as placeholder): https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/quick-answers.117535/page-32. Apolyton tip on the timing limit: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406 (Travathian) — "If it gets to one turn left you can switch to other units/improvements, but NOT wonders."
  • Switching between categories (unit ↔ improvement ↔ wonder). No Civ3 source found for a percentage penalty. The 50% "categories" rule found online (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/help-build-a-wonder.174050/) is a Civ2 forum thread, so it is excluded. Shields carry over to a different item: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/57954-solution-to-avoid-micromanagement (Franses) — "Shields are carried over in case you change production to something that costs more (or less) than the shields produced." UNVERIFIED (see open questions).
  • A rival completes the wonder you are building. Vanilla added notices: README-V "Players now receive a message when a wonder is made obsolete." / ""beginning wonder" pop-ups now only appear if you have an embassy or spy with the corresponding civ." Shields: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/wonder-building.27531 (Archullus) — "Switch to a less favourable wonder; or Get abortive production cost." https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/building-wonders.8756 — after switching to a Bank the original poster was "wasting about 400 shields". Reading: the shields stay in the box, a free switch to another wonder keeps them, and a switch to something cheaper wastes the excess on completion. Exact rule UNVERIFIED. The "reimbursed in cash" claim (forums.civfanatics.com/threads/list-of-bugs-ive-experienced.139564) is from a Civ4/BtS thread and is excluded.
  • Obsolete wonders. Obsolete wonders drop out of the build list. Their effects stop, but their culture and Golden Age triggers are presumably unaffected (UNVERIFIED). README-V: "Players now receive a message when a wonder is made obsolete."
  • Great Leader hurry.
    • Vanilla/PTW: military leaders could rush Great Wonders. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ — "In Conquests the rushing ability is limited to Small Wonders, but in Vanilla or Play the World it can rush a Great Wonder."
    • C3C, military leader: Pedia GCON_Leaders — "A Military Leader can be used to either create an army or to hurry production of an Improvement or Small Wonder in a city." Arathorn — "Military Great Leaders (MGLs) can no longer rush Great Wonders."
    • C3C, scientific leader: Arathorn — "An SGL can rush a wonder (either Great or Small)."
  • Golden Age from wonders. Pedia GCON_Golden_Age — "If a civilization builds a Great Wonder (or Wonders) that corresponds to [both] its unique strengths, its Golden Age can be triggered." BIQ RULE Golden Age duration = 20. Details belong in the Golden Age section.
  • Coastal, river and resource requirements (BIQ). Colossus, Great Lighthouse and Magellan's need a coastal city (CoastalInstallation). Hoover Dam needs a river (MustBeNearRiver). Statue of Zeus needs Ivory, Manhattan Uranium, Apollo Aluminum, and Iron Works Iron + Coal.
  • Wonder victory (C3C option, only if enabled). README-C3C — "The "Wonder Victory" is actually a Time Limit type of victory condition; i.e., when the Wonder Victory is in effect, the game ends immediately upon completion of the last Great Wonder." Victory-point games are out of scope.

Disputed

  1. Does the difficulty factor scale the AI's research or only the human's? The 1.29f thread says the formula "is relevant for research cost of the AI civs, not just the human player" and that techs "are now more expensive for the human player on Monarch, Emperor and Deity", with CF capped at 10. The CivFanatics FAQ says "On Chieftain, the cost factor ... is 2 for the AI – meaning everything takes twice as long to do, from researching a tech, to building a worker." That reads as AI cost x CF/10, uncapped. The two readings conflict for the AI below Regent.
  2. Minimum research time. 4 turns (BIQ RULE; FAQ "For all three games, the minimum research length is 4 turns"; David Weldon) vs Special_K, https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406 — "You can research an advance in 3 turns, it just takes a ton of science." The maximum is 32 then 40 in Vanilla (README-V 1.16f) and 50 in C3C (BIQ/FAQ). A Steam post cited in searches claimed "4/40" for the default game, which is probably Vanilla/PTW.
  3. Overflow. "all extra beakers are lost" (Rik Meleet; FAQ "no beaker carryover") vs "excess from the completing city is lost, later cities' beakers go to the next tech" (TimTheEnchanter / ElephantU, city order = reverse founding order). The second is more specific and consistent with the first as a simplification.
  4. Scientific leader chance. 3% / 5% for Scientific civs (Arathorn; Turner in https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/great-leader-abilities.88505/latest) vs 2% / 5% (Ginger_Ale, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/leaders.102561 — "you get a 5% chance of getting one", with 2% for non-scientific civs). The Pedia says only "increased slightly".
  5. Can a C3C military leader rush anything? Pedia, FAQ and Arathorn: yes, improvements and Small Wonders. EdSupp: "NOTE: Military leaders can not rush." HellboundHeathcliff, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/great-leader-abilities.88505/latest — "Conquests MGLs can only rush improvements and small wonders or create an army." EdSupp is the outlier.
  6. Theory of Evolution techs. Current research plus next pick (Yndy, Catt 1.21f) vs philippe, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-the-great-library-work.26689 — "thoery of evolution picks random techs." Turner describes three techs in one well-timed turn (current research, then two picks).
  7. Wonder data from the CivFanatics wonder guide (https://civfanatics.com/?p=151, as extracted by WebFetch; not re-verified line by line) differs from the BIQ on Copernicus' Observatory (Invention vs BIQ Astronomy). It also lists Universal Suffrage under "Industrialisation" and spells several names differently. The BIQ is authoritative for C3C 1.22.
  8. Shields after losing a wonder race (see 4.11): kept for a free wonder switch vs partly wasted ("abortive production cost"). No authoritative source.

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • Whether the 1.29f research formula (CL*1.75, DM table) is unchanged in PTW 1.27f and C3C 1.22. alexman says the difficulty scaling "differs between PTW and vanilla" (page 2 of the AI-research thread, paraphrased by the fetch). DM for Demigod (CF 7) and Sid (CF 4) is extrapolated.
  • How Future Tech Cost (400) enters the cost formula.
  • What happens to beakers beyond the 4-turn minimum pace (lost vs banked), and the exact mechanism of the 50-turn cap (forced completion vs cost reduction).
  • Era gating UI. It is inferred that next-era techs simply are not offered until the era advances. No source states this outright.
  • alexman's "No trade: 149/turns": probably the BIQ "Cannot Be Traded" flag, unused in default C3C. "Other tech: 1" is unexplained, and the Iron Working total shows it is not "leads to another tech". Flavor weight "5%" (Ozymandias) is unconfirmed. How the values weight the random roll is unknown (DevelopmentAI.cs assumes linear).
  • The AI-to-AI trade rate's exact meaning, and the C3C "changed calculation on tech prices" formula (diplomacy section).
  • The Scientific trait free-tech selection rule (random among researchable new-era techs, possibly excluding ones the AIs know).
  • The Philosophy free tech: confirm that it is the player's next pick and is limited to currently researchable techs (it can be a Middle Ages tech only once the era has advanced).
  • The Civ3 production-switch penalty between units, improvements and wonders (none found for Civ3), and exactly what happens to wonder shields when a rival completes the wonder.
  • Whether obsolete wonders keep culture and Golden Age eligibility.
  • Iron Works' effective tech gate (Coal visibility).
  • Search budget note: the session's web-search budget ran out before the Philosophy-pick and production-penalty questions could be searched further; follow-up searches may resolve them.

Back to the top

5. Governments

Scope: base game, Play the World (PTW) and Conquests (C3C), patched (1.29f / PTW 1.27f / C3C 1.22). Scenarios, mods and the special C3C game modes are out of scope. Where PTW/vanilla differ from C3C, the difference is called out. C3C is the target ruleset.

Sources used

Key Source
PEDIA Civ3 Complete install, Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt (the C3C 1.22 in-game Civilopedia). Entries are cited by key (GOVT_Despotism, GCON_Unit_Support ...). The government stat blocks are mirrored online at https://civfanatics.com/civ3/civilopedia/governments/
BIQ Conquests/conquests.biq (C3C 1.22 default rules), GOVT / RULE / DIFF / RACE sections, read with the repo's QueryCiv3 BIQ reader (field names from QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Govt.cs).
BIQ-PTW civ3PTW/civ3X.bix (PTW) and civ3mod.bic (vanilla) GOVT sections, raw-decoded with the same record layout. The two files have identical GOVT data.
EDHELP Conquests/Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm, the official C3C editor help (decompiled). Cited by page name.
README README.txt (vanilla patch notes up to 1.29f) and Conquests/readme.txt (C3C 1.20 to 1.22).
SCRIPT Conquests/Text/script.txt (in-game advisor and popup text).
CFC-GOV https://civfanatics.com/civ3/civilopedia/governments/ ("This article covers Civilization 3 Complete V1.22.")
CFC-WW https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/how-does-war-weariness-work/
CFC-CORR https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/everything-about-corruption-c3c-edition/
CFC-ESP https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/a-study-of-espionage-missions/
CFC-FAQ https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/frequently-asked-questions-civ3-play-the-world-conquests.170282/

Note: the repo copy C7/Civilopedia/Text/Civilopedia.txt is OpenCiv3's own text ("It is not Civ3's Civilopedia"), so it is not used as a source here.

C3C has eight governments: "There are eight forms of government that you can use to control your civilization." (PEDIA GCON_Governments). They are Anarchy, Despotism, Monarchy, Republic, Feudalism, Communism, Fascism and Democracy. There is no Tribalism in stock C3C. Tribalism appears only in mods. Vanilla and PTW have six (no Feudalism, no Fascism): the BIQ-PTW GOVT section has count 6.

5.1 Master table (C3C 1.22, from BIQ, matching PEDIA)

BIQ enum decoding:

  • Corruption: 0 Minimal, 1 Nuisance, 2 Problematic, 3 Rampant, 4 Catastrophic, 5 Communal. This matches PEDIA's labels one for one.
  • Hurrying: 0 none, 1 forced labor, 2 paid labor.
  • War weariness: 0 None, 1 Low, 2 High.
  • Worker rate: 2 = 100%. EDHELP Worker Rate: "The "base" worker rate is 2. Each digit increases the rate by 50%. Thus, 1=50% work rate (bad), 2 = 100% (normal), and 3 = 150%".
Government Prereq tech Corruption/Waste Tile penalty Trade bonus Worker rate Hurry Draft/turn Military police Free units Town/City/Metro Gold per extra unit War weariness Assimilation %/turn Rate cap Spies / Diplomats Immune to Other flags
Anarchy (transition type) Catastrophic (4) yes no 1 (50%) none 0 0 0/0/0, FreeUnits = -1 1 None 1 10 Regular / Regular - TransitionType=1, RequiresMaintenance=0
Despotism none (DefaultType) Rampant (3) yes no 2 (100%) forced 2 2 4/4/4 1 None 1 10 Regular / Regular - DefaultType=1
Monarchy Monarchy Problematic (2) no no 2 (100%) paid 2 3 2/4/8 1 None 2 10 Regular / Regular -
Feudalism Feudalism Problematic (2) no no 2 (100%) forced 2 3 5/2/1 3 Low 3 10 Regular / Regular -
Republic The Republic Nuisance (1) no yes 2 (100%) paid 1 0 1/3/4 2 Low 2 10 Regular / Regular -
Communism Communism Communal (5) no no 2 (100%) forced 2 4 6/6/6 1 None 4 10 Veteran / Regular - allows Secret Police HQ
Fascism Fascism Nuisance (1) no no 4 (200%) forced 2 4 4/7/10 1 None 2 10 Veteran / Regular - Xenophobic=1, ForceResettle=1
Democracy Democracy Minimal (0) no yes 3 (150%) paid 1 0 0/0/0 1 High 4 10 Regular / Regular Initiate Propaganda (mission index 6)

(Spies/Diplomats: BIQ SpiesAre/DiplomatsAre 1 = Regular, 2 = Veteran. EDHELP Spy Experience: "Determines the experience level of spies (Conscript, Regular, Veteran, Elite) under the selected government.")

PTW / vanilla differences (BIQ-PTW)

  • Only 6 governments (no Feudalism, no Fascism).
  • Communism unit support 2/4/8 in PTW/vanilla. C3C has 6/6/6.
  • Republic unit support 0/0/0 at 1 gold in PTW/vanilla. C3C has 1/3/4 at 2 gold.
  • All other GOVT fields match C3C: corruption, worker rate, hurry, draft, MP, war weariness, assimilation, rate cap and immunity.
  • Corroboration for the vanilla Communism value: CFC-FAQ-era GameFAQs guide (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/misc/civilization-iii-faqstrategy-guide-v3-0/, v3.0, Jan 2002): "under the non-democratic forms of government (Monarchy, Communism), you can support 2 units for each town (size 6 or below), 4 units for each city (size 7-12), and 8 units for each metropolis (size 13+)".
  • C3C note on Republic: CFC-FAQ: "in Republic in Conquests, you get very little unit support - and each unit past the limit costs 2 gpt to maintain!"

5.2 Per-government detail

Anarchy

Field Value
Corruption/waste Catastrophic: no production, no taxes, no research
Tile penalty Yes (Despotism penalty)
Workers 50%
Hurry Impossible
Draft / MP 0 / 0
Unit support No upkeep (BIQ FreeUnits = -1, per-size 0/0/0)
Maintenance None (RequiresMaintenance = 0)
War weariness None
  • PEDIA GOVT_Anarchy: "During that period corruption and waste are so high that no production occurs and no taxes are collected. Scientific research comes to halt, as well. There is no improvement maintenance when a civilization is in Anarchy."
  • CFC-GOV: "No city production, no research, Despo Penalty (see Despotism). Specialists still work. No maintenance costs or unit upkeep needed." BIQ agrees on the penalty: TilePenalty=1 for Anarchy.
  • Hurry: SCRIPT #GOVERNMENT_CANNOT_HURRY: "We are in the midst of a revolt, sir. We cannot hurry production." PEDIA GCON_Hurry_Production: "You have [no] production if your civilization is in Anarchy."
  • Draft: PEDIA GCON_Draft: "A civilization in Anarchy may not use the draft."
  • EDHELP Corruption Modifier (Difficulty Level): "The Corruption Modifier has no effect on players whose government experiences catastrophic corruption, cities in civil disorder, or capital cities."

Despotism

Field Value
Corruption/waste Rampant (distance factor x3/2, see 5.6)
Tile penalty Yes
Workers 100%
Hurry Forced labor (population)
Draft / MP 2 / 2
Unit support 4/4/4, 1 gold per extra unit
War weariness None
Start government Yes (DefaultType)
  • Tile penalty, PEDIA GOVT_Despotism: "any city production square which produces more than two food, shields, or commerce in a despotic government instead produces one less."
  • EDHELP Flags, "Standard tile penalty": "Under the selected government, no tile may generate more than 2 food and shields." The wording conflicts with PEDIA (see Disputed).
  • EDHELP Flags, "Default type": "All civilizations start the game under the selected government."

Monarchy

Field Value
Corruption/waste Problematic
Workers 100%
Hurry Paid (gold)
Draft / MP 2 / 3
Unit support 2/4/8, 1 gold per extra unit
War weariness None
  • PEDIA GOVT_Monarchy: "Corruption and waste are significant, but are ameliorated to an extent by loyalty to the King."

Feudalism (C3C only)

Field Value
Corruption/waste Problematic
Workers 100%
Hurry Forced labor
Draft / MP 2 / 3
Unit support 5/2/1, 3 gold per extra unit
War weariness Low
  • PEDIA GOVT_Feudalism: "your vassals are wary of continual warfare and war weariness may result. You do not suffer the production problems associated with despotism, however. Maintenance cost per unit per turn is 3 gold instead of 1."
  • Note the inverted support curve: towns support more than metropolises. CFC-GOV: "Good for civs with small towns (under pop 6). You might actually want small towns because of the unit support".
  • "Feudalism pikemen support" is not in stock C3C. PEDIA, BIQ GOVT and EDHELP contain no unit-type-specific support rule. UNVERIFIED as a stock rule; probably a mod.

Republic

Field Value
Corruption/waste Nuisance
Trade bonus Yes (+1 commerce, see Disputed for "only on tiles already >= 1")
Workers 100%
Hurry Paid (gold)
Draft / MP 1 / 0 (no martial law)
Unit support 1/3/4, 2 gold per extra unit (C3C)
War weariness Low
  • PEDIA GOVT_Republic: "your government is affected by war weariness, which can cause significant civil disorder problems in times of war, especially if you are the aggressor. Maintenance cost per unit per turn is 2 gold instead of just 1."
  • CFC-GOV: "Adds 1 commerce to any tile already producing at least 1 = the commerce bonus."

Communism

Field Value
Corruption/waste Communal: the same in every city, independent of distance (see 5.6)
Workers 100%
Hurry Forced labor
Draft / MP 2 / 4
Unit support 6/6/6 (C3C), 1 gold per extra unit
War weariness None
Espionage Spies are Veteran
Special Can build the Secret Police HQ (a second "palace" for corruption)
  • PEDIA GOVT_Communism: "One positive aspect of Communism is its effect upon corruption and waste: all cities suffer the same, limited effects."
  • PEDIA BLDG_Secret_Police_HQ: "Only civilizations under Communist government can build the Secret Police HQ. Changing from Communism will cause the Secret Police HQ bonuses to stop."
  • README (C3C 1.20): "Secret Police HQ no longer gives corruption OR OCN bonus when player leaves Communism."
  • README (vanilla 1.17f): "Corruption calculations for Communism are now flat for the number-of-cities factor."
  • CFC-GOV: "you can whip foreign people in newly conquered cities to prevent revolts". Plausibly a consequence of forced-labor hurry. UNVERIFIED as a distinct rule.

Fascism (C3C only)

Field Value
Corruption/waste Nuisance
Workers 200%
Hurry Forced labor
Draft / MP 2 / 4
Unit support 4/7/10, 1 gold per extra unit
War weariness None
Espionage Spies are Veteran
Flags Xenophobic, Forced Resettlement
  • PEDIA GOVT_Fascism: "Your cities lose population points upon a change to fascist government, Newly conquered cities also lose population, and do not generate culture until a popular majority has been assimilated."
  • EDHELP Flags, Forced Resettlement: "When switching to the selected government, each town in the nation loses 1 population, each city loses 2 population, and each metropolis loses 3 population."
  • EDHELP Flags, Xenophobic: "no culture is gained in cities where less than half of the population of the city is from the same race as the controlling civilization."
  • CFC-GOV: "Higher chance of espionage mission success". Explained by SpiesAre=2 (Veteran), the same as Communism.
  • "Fascism can't be bribed / is immune to propaganda": not supported by the data. Fascism's ImmuneTo is -1 (none). The per-government "Cannot Bribe" setting is the CanBribe field of the GOVT-vs-GOVT matrix. In conquests.biq it holds the uninitialized value 0xCDCDCDCD for every pair, so it does not distinguish Fascism. UNVERIFIED.

Democracy

Field Value
Corruption/waste Minimal (distance factor x3/4)
Trade bonus Yes
Workers 150%
Hurry Paid (gold)
Draft / MP 1 / 0
Unit support 0/0/0: every unit costs 1 gold
War weariness High. Can cause a revolt into Anarchy
Propaganda Immune
  • PEDIA GOVT_Democracy: "war weariness is a significant problem and war must be entered into only after much consideration."
  • Propaganda immunity: BIQ ImmuneTo=6 (Initiate Propaganda). SCRIPT #ESPIONAGE_MISSION_INITIATE_PROPAGANDA_IMMUNE: "The $CIVADJ1 people are immune to propaganda." CFC-ESP: "Democracy is immune against propaganda."
  • CFC-GOV, Anarchy: "It also happens when WW reaches 100% when under Democracy." CFC-WW, Democracy "Level 3: Revolt".

5.3 City size categories and unit support

  • Size thresholds, BIQ RULE: MaximumLevel1CitySize=6, MaximumLevel2CitySize=12, level names Town, City, Metropolis.

    • EDHELP Level One City Max Size: "Default is 6." EDHELP Level Two City Maximum Size: "Default is 12."
    • CFC-GOV: "Town: 1-6 population / City: 7-12 population (requires fresh water or an aquaduct) / Metro: 12+ population (requires a hospital ...)". (Metro is really 13+, per the RULE maximum of 12 for level 2.)

  • Support rule, PEDIA GCON_Unit_Support: "Generally, each military unit must be supported with one gold per turn from its treasury. However, each form of government defers these costs for a certain number of units, depending upon how many towns, cities, or metros the civilization controls."

  • Formula, CFC-GOV: "4 towns and 3 cities under Monarchy have 4×2 + 3×4 = 20 unit support. That means that every unit over the cap of 20 cost you 1gpt." For each extra unit, the cost is UnitCost gold: Republic 2, Feudalism 3, all others 1.

  • EDHELP Free Units Per Town: "how many "free" units the selected government is allowed per level one city. Note that this number is above and beyond any free units allowed by default." The "by default" link points to the government FreeUnits field (0 for all real governments, -1 for Anarchy).

  • AI-only difficulty bonuses, BIQ DIFF (AdditionalFreeSupport / UnitSupportBonusForEachSettlement):

    Level Bonus
    Chieftain to Regent 0/0
    Monarch 4/1
    Emperor 8/2
    Demigod 12/3
    Deity 16/4
    Sid 24/8

    EDHELP Additional Free Support: "how many extra units the AI can support, in addition to any existing governmental bonuses ... cumulative with all other support bonuses."

  • Which units count: the vanilla-era guide (URL above) says "This count includes Workers, and also includes any Settlers that haven't built or joined cities yet." This is UNVERIFIED for C3C, and PEDIA says only "each military unit".

5.4 Martial law (military police)

  • EDHELP Military Police Limit: "how many military units (units with attack rating greater than zero) can be garrisoned in a city to reduce unhappiness."
  • CFC-GOV: "Military Police Limit: Max number of units in a city that makes 1 unhappy citizen content."
  • Citizens quelled per unit come from the difficulty level: BIQ DIFF MilitaryLaw=1 on all levels. EDHELP Number of Citizens Quelled by Military: "how many rioting-citizens are quelled with the presence of military units."
  • Limits: Despotism 2, Monarchy 3, Feudalism 3, Communism 4, Fascism 4. Republic, Democracy and Anarchy: 0.

5.5 Hurrying (rushing) and the draft

  • Method by government, PEDIA GCON_Hurry_Production:
    • "Under Despotism, Communism, Feudalism, or Fascism you hurry production in your cities by forced labor. Disillusioned by your harsh rule, citizens will leave your city".
    • "Under Monarchy, Republic, and Democracy you can pay overtime ... This costs gold from your treasury; the amount depends upon how much work remains to be done."
  • Forced labor rate. BIQ RULE CitizenValueInShields=20. EDHELP: "how many shields are produced by "sacrificing" a point of population ... Default is 20 shields."
    • README 1.21f: "The first citizen is now worth 20 shields instead of 40 shields, just like all the other citizens."
    • README 1.17f: "Eliminated 'Despot Pop Rushing'." (The exact meaning is not stated.)
  • Pop-rush unhappiness lasts 20 turns. BIQ RULE TurnPenaltyForEachHurrySacrifice=20. README 1.21f: "Draft anger and hurrying unhappiness reduced to 20 turns." It was 40 in 1.17f: "Unhappiness from population rushing production increased to 40 turns."
  • Pop-rush limits:
    • SCRIPT #HURRY_NOT_ENOUGH_PEOPLE: "Rushing this project would cost the lives of too many citizens!"
    • Forum (eg577, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/hurry-production.95782/): "you cannot "whip" more than half of your citizens at once" and "twice that many citizens if there are no shields contributed yet". UNVERIFIED.
    • Wonders cannot be pop-rushed. CFC-FAQ: "you can't rush a wonder by sacrificing population (Despotism, Communism, Fascism)".
  • Gold rush rate. BIQ RULE ShieldValueInGold=4. EDHELP Shield Value in Gold: "how many gold pieces it costs to buy each shield when hurrying production of a city improvement. Default is 4 gold."
    • Empty production box doubles the cost. Forum (petermarkab, same thread): "Multiply by 8 if no shields have been contributed."
    • Unit vs improvement vs wonder multipliers are UNVERIFIED (see open questions).
  • Leaders: EditorSupplement.txt: "NOTE: Military leaders can not rush." Great (scientific) leaders can (PEDIA GCON_Hurry_Production).
  • Draft:
    • PEDIA GCON_Draft: "When a civilization develops Nationalism its citizens can be drafted ... A city in a Democracy or Republic can draft [one] citizen per turn ... Communism, Despotism, Fascism, Feudalism, or Monarchy can draft [two] ... You may only draft citizens from a city with a population of 7 or higher."
    • Draft anger lasts 20 turns: BIQ RULE TurnPenaltyForEachDraftedCitizen=20.
    • Units are conscripts: Spearman, Pikeman, Musketman, Rifleman, Infantry or Mech. Infantry by tech.
    • README 1.29f: "Corrected the Communism draft rate listed in the Draft Civilopedia entry to 2."

5.6 Corruption types (government factors only; full model in the corruption section)

CFC-CORR (C3C), quoted:

  • Distance factor Gd: "Gd = 3/2 for Rampant corruption (Despotism) / 3/4 for Minimal corruption (Democracy) / 1 otherwise".
  • Optimal-city-number factors: "Gr = 0.1 for minimal or nuisance corruption / 2 for communal corruption / 0 otherwise" and "Gp = 3/8 non-communal corruption / 3 for communal corruption" (Gp multiplies the number of corruption-reducing wonders: Forbidden Palace, SPHQ).
  • Communal distance: "For communal governments, distance corruption does not actually depend on distance. Each city is considered to be the same distance from the Palace." The value is "d = (MaxD) / 4 where MaxD = (MapW+MapH)/4".
  • Communal rank: "all cities have the same rank, which is half the total number of cities in the empire, rounded down."
  • Catastrophic (Anarchy) is fixed and bypasses the formula (EDHELP quote under Anarchy).

5.7 Revolution, anarchy length, and switching

  • When you can switch:
    • Script: "We cannot have a revolution until we discover different forms of government." Each government needs its prerequisite tech (table above). EDHELP Prerequisite: "Selects the civilization advance (if any) required before this government type is enabled."
    • You cannot re-revolt during anarchy. SCRIPT #ALREADYREVOLTING: "Sir! we are already undergoing a revolt..!"
    • Revolution is started from the Domestic Advisor (PEDIA GCON_Revolution).
    • Learning a new government's tech triggers an offer. SCRIPT #NEW_GOVERNMENT_AVAILABLE: "our people yearn for a change in government to ... Shall we encourage a revolution?"
  • Involuntary anarchy:
    • PEDIA GCON_Disorder: prolonged disorder can lead to "An overthrow of your government and descent of your empire into anarchy."
    • Democracy war weariness can do the same (CFC-WW level 3 "Revolt").
  • Transition government. BIQ: Anarchy has TransitionType=1. EDHELP Flags: "When changing governments (i.e., having a revolution), civilizations temporarily transition to the selected government."
  • Anarchy length:
    • PEDIA GOVT_Anarchy: "Anarchy seldom lasts longer than a few turns."
    • The domestic advisor reports the remaining turns. SCRIPT: "We should have the government back under control in about $NUM0 turns."
    • CFC-FAQ (C3C-aware): "For Religious civilizations, anarchy only lasts 1 turn in Vanilla and Play the World, and 2 turns in Conquests." and "For non-Religious civilizations, the formula is: 1 (2 for Conquests) + random number between 1-4".
    • CFC-GOV: "It lasts from 2 to 8 turns, dependent on the size of your civilization / map size."
    • Forum relay of Soren Johnson (vanilla era, Padma, 2003-04-22, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/going-to-monarchy.51241/): "Soren confirmed (way back in February) that the length is always between 1 - 5 turns on a random basis" plus "an additional 0-3 turns depending on number of cities".
    • Forum (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/number-of-turns-for-revolution-calculation.108456/, Turner): "Conquests raised that briefly. 8 and 9 turn anarchy was seen."
    • See Disputed.
  • Religious trait. PEDIA GCON_Strengths: "Religious civilizations do not experience prolonged periods of anarchy during revolutions". No wonder affects anarchy length in C3C: the Statue of Zeus entry (BLDG_Zeus) only says it "produces an Ancient Cavalry every 5 turns". The "Statue of Zeus/Liberty shortens anarchy" idea is Civ2.
  • AI cap. BIQ DIFF MaxGovernmentTransitionTime: 0 (no cap) for Chieftain, Warlord and Regent; Monarch 4, Emperor 3, Demigod 2, Deity 2, Sid 1. EDHELP: "the maximum number of turns the AI must remain in the transition government type ... The default value of zero means there is no maximum".
  • Government-locked buildings switch off when you leave the government. README C3C 1.20: "Fixed error with Government required buildings not turning off once switched from that government."

5.8 AI favourite / shunned government

  • EDHELP Favorite Government: "If a government is selected, the civilization will immediately switch to this government type as soon as it becomes available."
  • EDHELP Shunned Government: "the civilization will avoid this government type at all costs, and if it is forced to operate under this government type, it will immediately switch to another government type as soon as another government type becomes available."
  • C3C defaults (BIQ RACE, Favourite / Shunned):
Civ Fav Shun Civ Fav Shun
Rome Republic Communism Iroquois Communism Monarchy
Egypt Despotism Republic England Democracy Fascism
Greece Democracy Despotism Mongols Despotism Democracy
Babylon Monarchy Despotism Spain Monarchy Despotism
Germany Republic Communism Scandinavia Monarchy Republic
Russia Communism Democracy Ottomans Republic Despotism
China Communism Feudalism Celts Despotism Feudalism
America Democracy Communism Arabia Republic Despotism
Japan Feudalism Republic Carthage Republic Communism
France Republic Fascism Korea Monarchy Republic
India Democracy Despotism Sumeria Monarchy Republic
Persia Monarchy Republic Hittites Fascism Republic
Aztecs Despotism Monarchy Netherlands Monarchy Despotism
Zululand Despotism Republic Portugal Democracy Fascism
Byzantines Republic Feudalism Inca Communism Republic
Maya Feudalism Democracy Barbarians none none
  • "Favourite Despotism" (Egypt, Aztecs, Zulu, Mongols, Celts) means the AI never voluntarily leaves the start government, if read literally. UNVERIFIED in play.

5.9 War weariness

  • Who suffers. PEDIA GCON_War_Weariness: "citizens living under a Republic, Feudalism, or Democracy become [war weary]". BIQ WW levels: Republic 1, Feudalism 1, Democracy 2, all others 0.
  • PEDIA, same entry: "When [you] initiate the conflict your people will tolerate it for a while ... When someone else declares the war, especially a traditional enemy, your people are more forgiving. Carrying on a defensive war, that is keeping your troops at home, is more tolerable ... the war weariness remains until the war stops."
  • Mitigation: Police Station ("combats war weariness", PEDIA BLDG_Police_Station) and Universal Suffrage ("Reduces war weariness in all cities", PEDIA BLDG_Universal_Suffrage).
  • Formula, CFC-WW (community research, version not stated, probably PTW):
    • Points: "Each civ have one wwp number against each of the other civs."
    • Levels: "-1: - 0 wh (war happiness) / 0: 0 - 30 normal, no effect / 1: 31 - 60 / 2: 61 - 90 / 3: 91 - 120 / 4: 121 -".
    • Republic: "Level 1: 25% unhappy people / Level 2: 50% / Level 3: 50% / Level 4: 100%".
    • Democracy: "Level 1: 50% unhappy people / Level 2: 100% unhappy people / Level 3: Revolt".
    • War happiness: "Level -1: 25% happy people" (all governments).
    • Combining: "Number of unhappy people is round down. The number from each civ is added together and subtract 25% for police station and 1 for US (Universal Sufferage)."
    • Point events:
      • "Subtract 30 wwp if the AI attacks you, except when AI is provoked by: use of nuclear weapons, failed spy mission"
      • "Add 1 wwp if you have units in enemys territory when in war."
      • "Add 1 wwp for each lost unit without defence value / improvement pillage/bombed / unit that are bombard down to 1 hp"
      • "Add 2 wwp when a human attacker is defeated"
      • "Add 2 wwp when a unit with defence value is attacked. (Even if you win)"
      • "Add 16 wwp when a size 1 city is captured 17 wwp for bigger cities."
    • Decay: "Subtract 1 wwp if level >= 1, no enemy inside your territory and no units in enemys territory." and "Subtract 1/20 of current wwp each turn in peace (round up)".
    • Bug (as reported): "For a human-AI battle, the AI gets the same penalty as the human".
    • Feudalism (Low) presumably uses the Republic (Low) table. UNVERIFIED inference: the article predates Feudalism.

5.10 Senate / forced ceasefire

  • Civ3 has no Senate. No Senate, ceasefire-forcing or forced-peace rule appears in the PEDIA, SCRIPT (advisor and popup texts), labels.txt, diplomacy.txt, BIQ GOVT fields or EDHELP. The only "Senate" mentions in Civilopedia.txt are historical prose (e.g. DESC_GOVT_Republic).
  • The Civ2 Senate is the origin of the idea. In Civ3 the equivalent pressure is war weariness, and the Democracy WW revolt.
  • Status: verified by absence in the game files. No CivFanatics page states it outright (my web searches turned up only Civ2 pages).

5.11 Espionage / propaganda modifiers (GOVT-vs-GOVT matrix)

BIQ stores three values per ordered pair of governments: CanBribe, BriberyModifier and ResistanceModifier. The editor shows BriberyModifier as "Propaganda Modifier Vs". EDHELP: "higher means a better chance of bribing ... Selecting "Cannot Bribe this Government Type" effectively prevents bribery from occurring."

C3C BriberyModifier (row = BIQ record, column = "vs"). It matches the CFC-ESP "Government modifier" table exactly:

row \ vs Anar Desp Mon Comm Rep Demo Fasc Feud
Anarchy 0 0 0 0 0 0 0 0
Despotism 15 0 -10 -15 -20 -30 0 0
Monarchy 20 15 0 -5 -10 -25 0 0
Communism 25 20 5 0 -10 -20 0 0
Republic 30 25 10 8 0 -5 0 0
Democracy 35 30 20 10 5 0 0 0
Fascism 0 0 -5 -20 -10 -10 0 0
Feudalism 20 15 0 -10 -10 -20 -25 0

C3C ResistanceModifier (row/col as above):

row \ vs Anar Desp Mon Comm Rep Demo Fasc Feud
Anarchy 0 5 5 5 5 5 0 0
Despotism -5 0 5 5 5 5 0 0
Monarchy -5 -5 0 5 5 5 0 0
Communism -5 -5 -5 0 -5 5 0 0
Republic -5 -5 5 5 0 5 0 0
Democracy -5 -5 -5 5 -5 0 0 0
Fascism -5 -5 0 -10 -5 -5 0 0
Feudalism -5 -5 5 5 0 0 5 0
  • EDHELP Resistance Modifier: "the modifier to a single population point's chance of continuing to resist each turn. Positive numbers mean that the resisting population has a greater chance of continuing to resist".
  • Propaganda success per citizen, CFC-ESP: "spy + government + culture - 5 * units - improvement - capital + nationality", and "If at least 3/4 of the citizen are persuading, the city will flip."
  • CanBribe is 1 for all pairs in PTW/vanilla and the uninitialized value 0xCDCDCDCD in C3C. Treat it as "can bribe" (no government is protected by it).
  • Assimilation. EDHELP Assimilation Chance: "the percentage chance, per turn, that a foreign national in city with the selected government type will assimilate". Values are in the master table.

5.12 Rate cap (max slider rate)

  • BIQ ScienceRateCap = 10 for every government in C3C, PTW and vanilla.
  • EDHELP Rate Cap: "Determines the maximum science funding (0-10) that the selected government can engage in. Default is always 10 (100%)."
  • So stock Civ3 has no per-government slider cap (unlike Civ2's 60/70/80%).
  • Weak corroboration, Apolyton (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/44662-low-science-quick-monarchy-tips): "I changed to a 100 percent science rating." (The government is not stated.) Mods set lower caps. A modder's Apolyton post (seen only as a search snippet) used Anarchy 5, Despotism 7 and Monarchy 8.

5.13 Implementation notes (C7 cross-check)

  • C7/Lua/civ3/ruleset.json governments[] matches BIQ on corruption, hurry, rateCap (10), war weariness, draft, MP, workerRate, free units and unitCost.

  • It has no fields for:

    • AssimilationChance
    • ImmuneTo (Democracy propaganda immunity)
    • DiplomatsAre / SpiesAre
    • Xenophobic / ForceResettle (Fascism)
    • the GOVT-vs-GOVT propaganda and resistance matrices

    Check whether the engine hardcodes these elsewhere before relying on them.

  • BIQ fields Toggle1/Toggle2/Toggle3 (QueryCiv3 names; meaning unknown) are uninitialized (0xCCCCCCCC) for the two C3C-only governments, Fascism and Feudalism. That strongly suggests the game ignores them.

Disputed

  1. Anarchy length.
    • CFC-FAQ: C3C = 2 + rand(1..4) turns (non-Religious), Religious = 2 (C3C) / 1 (vanilla/PTW).
    • CFC-GOV: "2 to 8 turns, dependent on the size of your civilization / map size".
    • Soren relay (vanilla, 2003): rand(1..5) + 0..3 by number of cities.
    • Forum reports of 8 and 9 turns in C3C, and a forum guess of "1-4 by size, and 1-5 random" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/number-of-turns-for-revolution-calculation.108456/).
    • PEDIA only says Religious civs avoid "prolonged" anarchy.
  2. Religious anarchy in vanilla. The GameFAQs guide says "no period of anarchy between governments". CFC-FAQ and the forums say 1 turn.
  3. Despotism tile penalty wording.
    • PEDIA: a tile producing more than 2 of food, shields or commerce "produces one less" (so 3 becomes 2, 4 becomes 3).
    • EDHELP: "no tile may generate more than 2 food and shields" (reads like a hard cap, and omits commerce).
    • PEDIA and CFC-GOV agree on "1 less" and include commerce.
  4. Trade bonus scope.
    • EDHELP: "each tile generates an additional +1 to commerce".
    • CFC-GOV: "Adds 1 commerce to any tile already producing at least 1".
  5. Gold-rush rate.
    • CFC-GOV and BIQ: 4 gold per shield.
    • Forum (petermarkab): "Multiply by 8 if no shields have been contributed" (doubling).
    • An older forum recollection (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/anyone-know-the-formulae-for-rush-building.18881, seen only as a search snippet) claims about 2 gold per shield for improvements and higher for units and wonders.
  6. Pop-rush rate. Forum posts disagree: "1 pop point per 30 shields" vs "1 citzen per 20 shields". BIQ/EDHELP/README say 20 (C3C and 1.21f+).
  7. Communism support. 6/6/6 (C3C BIQ and PEDIA) vs 2/4/8 (PTW/vanilla BIQ and the vanilla guide). These are version differences, not errors.
  8. Republic support. 1/3/4 at 2 gold (C3C) vs 0/0/0 at 1 gold (PTW/vanilla).
  9. Metropolis threshold. CFC-GOV says "Metro: 12+". BIQ RULE (max level-2 size 12) and the vanilla guide ("metropolis (size 13+)") say 13+.

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  1. The exact C3C anarchy formula (random range, and how civ size or city count adds turns). Needs a code-level source or a controlled test.
  2. Gold hurry cost structure beyond "4 gold/shield, doubled from an empty box": unit vs improvement vs wonder multipliers, and any non-linear term. My web search budget ran out before this was resolved.
  3. Pop-rush limits: the "max half the citizens" claim, the "double citizens from an empty box" claim, minimum city size, and whether foreign citizens are whipped first under Communism.
  4. Which units count against support in C3C: workers, settlers, explorers? Do they consume free slots? PEDIA says "military unit". The vanilla guide says workers and settlers count.
  5. Whether Feudalism's WW uses the same table as Republic (both BIQ level 1). CFC-WW predates Feudalism and Fascism.
  6. Whether CFC-WW's numbers (thresholds 30/60/90/120, 1/20 decay, +16/17 for city capture) hold in C3C 1.22. The article does not state a version.
  7. Direction of the GOVT-vs-GOVT matrices: is the row the initiator's or the target's government? CFC-ESP's prose does not say.
  8. "Fascism cannot be bribed": no supporting data found (CanBribe is uninitialized for every pair in C3C).
  9. "Feudalism pikemen support" and "Tribalism veteran chance": not stock C3C (no data in PEDIA, BIQ or EDHELP). Probably from mods.
  10. Whether favourite-government AIs really "immediately" revolt when the tech arrives. EDHELP says so; not observed. Also how the AI decides when it has no favourite.
  11. Whether Golden Age or WLTKD removes the Despotism tile penalty. No source found; WLTKD is documented only as reducing waste.
  12. Anarchy upkeep: PEDIA/CFC-GOV say no building maintenance and no unit upkeep. BIQ Anarchy FreeUnits=-1 with UnitCost=1; the reading "-1 = unlimited free units" is inferred.
  13. Does re-revolting (choosing a different government during anarchy) re-roll the length? It is blocked by "#ALREADYREVOLTING" for a new revolution, but whether the target government can be changed is unknown.

Back to the top

6. Diplomacy, espionage and AI attitude

Scope: the base game (1.29f), Play the World (1.27f) and Conquests (1.22). Scenarios, mods and the special Conquests game modes are excluded.

How to read this section:

  • Every rule has a source and a short verbatim quote. "Local install" means the files of a Steam Civilization III Complete install (Conquests 1.22), read directly: Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt, Conquests/Text/script.txt, Conquests/Text/diplomacy.txt, Conquests/Text/labels.txt, the readmes, and Conquests/conquests.biq (read with this repo's QueryCiv3 parser).
  • Vanilla-only text comes from Text/Civilopedia.txt in the same install.
  • Community findings are labelled with the tester, version and date where the source gives them. CivFanatics studies use a sign convention where a negative attitude number is good for you.
  • Anything without a source is marked UNVERIFIED.

6.1 Contact and the diplomacy screen

  • The Diplomacy button needs first contact. Source: vanilla README.txt (manual errata, local install). Quote: "The Diplomacy button becomes enabled after your first contact with another civilization."
  • Foreign Advisor lines need contact with both civs. Source: vanilla README.txt, Fixes v1.17f. Quote: "Relationship lines are no longer displayed on the Foreign Advisor unless the player has contact with both civilizations."
  • There are five attitude states, which the advisor mouse-over shows. Source: vanilla README.txt (1.17f). Quote: "The five states an AI can be in on the Diplomacy screen, can now be seen on the mouse over advisor messages". Their names are in Conquests labels.txt: "gracious", "polite", "cautious", "annoyed", "furious".
  • Communications (contacts) can be traded. They are introductions to a third civ, and Printing Press unlocks the trade. Sources: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Agreements, quote: "{Communications}. These are introductions to a third civilization you can grant or receive from a rival." Also conquests.biq TECH flags, where Printing Press has "Enables Communication Trading". Vanilla Civilopedia TECH_Printing_Press, quote: "Can now trade communications between civilizations."
  • PTW fix: the AI cannot trade communications before it has the tech. Source: civ3PTW/readme.txt. Quote: "Fixed bug that allowed the AI to trade communications before they had the tech that allowed it."
  • A world map does not give contact. Source: CivFanatics, "Basic trading tips" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/basic-trading-tips.71236/). Quote (as surfaced by search): "trading World Maps will show you the world known by the other civ but will not give you contact with civs you have".
  • What triggers first contact is UNVERIFIED. It could be units adjacent, units seeing each other, or borders touching. No primary source was found (see open questions).

6.2 Embassies

  • Writing enables embassies. An embassy is built in the rival's capital and costs gold. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Embassies. Quotes: "When a civilization learns $LINK<Writing=TECH_Writing>, it can begin to build its intelligence network. The first step is to build [embassies] in rival capital cities." and "Embassies cost gold from your treasury to build, and diplomatic missions cost gold to execute." In conquests.biq, Writing has the flag "Enables Diplomats".
  • You can make agreements when either side has an embassy. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Agreements. Quote: "When your civilization builds an $LINK<embassy=GCON_Embassies> in a rival's capital city, or when a rival builds one in your capital, you can negotiate [Diplomatic Agreements]".
  • An embassy shows the other civ's wars and peace. Source: CivFanatics Civ3 FAQ (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/). Quote: "you can see what civilizations they are at war/peace with on the F4 Foreign Advisor screen."
  • Establishing an embassy always succeeds and costs gold by formula (see 6.11). The conquests.biq ESPN base cost is 20. Source: CivFanatics, "A Study of Espionage Missions" (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/a-study-of-espionage-missions/). The study says Build an Embassy and Investigate City always succeed. A local conquests.biq dump gives "Build an Embassy | diplomat=True base=20".
  • War closes the embassy automatically. Source: Civ3.com FAQ, quoted on Apolyton (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/41397-espionage-you-can-t-steal-techs-from-civs-you-are-at-war-with). Quote: "your embassy in their capitol is automatically closed".
  • Wonder pop-ups need an embassy or a spy. Source: vanilla README.txt. Quote: ""beginning wonder" pop-ups now only appear if you have an embassy or spy with the corresponding civ." Also: "Spaceship parts pop-ups now only appear if you have a spy with the corresponding civ."

6.3 Diplomatic agreements: kinds and the techs that enable them

  • There are four agreement kinds. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Agreements. Quotes:

    • Military Alliance: "You agree to declare war on a despised third party."
    • Right of Passage: "You agree to allow his or her military units to move through your $LINK<territory=GCON_Territory> and use your transportation network (roads and railroads)."
    • Trade Embargo: "An agreement that neither of you will $LINK<trade=GCON_Trade> with or [allow] trade with a third party."
    • Mutual Protection Pact: "An agreement to declare war upon any civilization that attacks your partner."

  • Tech gates in Conquests (conquests.biq TECH flags, local dump):

    • Writing: Diplomats, Military Alliance.
    • Map Making: Right of Passage.
    • Printing Press: Communications.
    • Navigation: Map Trading.
    • Nationalism: Mutual Protection Pact, Trade Embargo.

    The Conquests Civilopedia agrees. TECH_Writing: "Can now negotiate military alliance." TECH_Map_Making: "Can now negotiate right of passage". TECH_Navigation: "Can now trade $LINK<world maps=GCON_Maps> in diplomacy." TECH_Nationalism: "Can now negotiate mutual protection pact and trade embargo". CivFanatics FAQ, quote: "you cannot trade World/Territory Maps until you research Navigation."

  • Vanilla differs (see Disputed). In vanilla, Writing also gives Right of Passage and Map Making gives map trading. Source: vanilla Text/Civilopedia.txt. TECH_Writing: "Can now negotiate military alliance and right of passage". TECH_Map_Making: "Can now trade $LINK<world maps=GCON_Maps> in diplomacy."

  • An MPP can be offered to you before you have Nationalism. Source: CivFanatics search summary of "control over rop/mpp" and Apolyton "quick quest what else is needed for mpp". Quote (search snippet): "an AI with Nationalism can offer you an MPP even if you lack the tech". Weak source; treat as UNVERIFIED.

  • Locked alliances are scenario-only, so out of scope. Members have a permanent MPP. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Locked_Alliances. Quote: "A pre-designed scenario (only) might begin with some (or all) civilizations grouped into several "Locked Alliances"."

6.4 Trade routes, resources and embargoes

  • Resource trade needs connected capitals. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Trade. Quote: "Excess resources may be traded to other civilizations if the capital cities of both civilizations are connected."
  • Connection rules. Source: same entry.
