Quote: "there is a road or railroad running between them, there are harbors in both cities and a visible water route between them, or both cities contain an airport" (paraphrased list; the original is three bullet lines). A road through enemy territory or a blockaded harbor does not count. Quote: "two cities are not connected if the only road/rail that connects them passes through enemy territory, or if a harbor is blockaded".
  • An embargo blocks resource and luxury trade. Source: Conquests script.txt DIPLOADVICETRADE_THEIREMBARGO. Quote: "We cannot trade resources or luxuries with the $CIVNOUN0 -- they have a trade embargo against us." Also DIPLOADVICETRADE_OUREMBARGO: "We have agreed not to trade with the $CIVNOUN0 -- we have a trade embargo against them."
  • The embargoed civ is told when an embargo starts and ends. Source: script.txt TRADEEMBARGO / TRADEEMBARGOENDS. Quotes: "$CIVNAME0 and $CIVNAME1 have signed a trade embargo against us." and "The trade embargo against us between $CIVNAME0 and $CIVNAME1 has ended."
  • The AI rarely trades its only copy of a luxury. Source: CivFanatics, "Civs and their luxuries", simwiz2 (Civ3 General Discussions) (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civs-and-their-luxuries.15068/). Quote: "An AI will almost NEVER trade a luxury that it only has one of." The "never trades its only resource" rule found elsewhere is a Civ4 rule (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/question-about-trading-resources.368926), so it is not used here.

6.5 Deal durations, renegotiation and peace treaties

  • Ongoing deals run for 20 turns. Source: Conquests labels.txt. Quotes: "This deal may be canceled peacefully after 20 turns." and "This deal will last for 20 turns."
  • "Always Renegotiate Deals" (1.29f) forces renegotiation every 20 turns. Peace treaties are exempt. Source: vanilla README.txt, Additions v1.29f. Quote: "Added "Always Renegotiate Deals" prefs which forces a renegotiation of every non-peace treaty deal every 20 turns."
  • The AI asks to end or renew at expiry. Source: Conquests diplomacy.txt AIRENEGOTIATE. Quote: "Friend $PLAYER0, this pact is about to expire. We believe that its usefulness is over." AIRENEGOTIATEPEACE: "$PLAYER0, our peace treaty is about to expire. For the sake of world peace, we will extend it if you desire."
  • A peace treaty lasts until someone declares war. It has a 20-turn term, and breaking it inside that term costs reputation. Source: CivFanatics, "question about peace treaties" (C3C 1.15) (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/question-about-peace-treaties.84591). marioh, quote: "If you sign a peace-treaty with a civ it will last until you or the other declares war." Taé Shala, quote: "there will be a reputation penalty if you break the treaty within the remaining turns."
  • A bare treaty renews itself; one with attachments does not. Source: same thread (marioh), as summarised in search. Quote: "If you don't have GPT/ROP/MPP/luxuries/resources attached with the peace treaty, then it will automatically renew until war is re-declared by either party."
  • Mobilization lasts until peace. Source: Conquests Civilopedia (Mobilization). Quote: "May not return to Normalcy until a Peace Treaty is signed."
  • Ceasefire length is UNVERIFIED for Civ3. The often-quoted "16 turns" comes from the Civ2 Official Strategy Guide, quoted in a Civilization II forum thread (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-long-does-a-cease-fire-last.55033). Quote: "a cease-fire lasts only 16 turns." That is not a Civ3 source. diplomacy.txt and labels.txt have no ceasefire strings (only AIPEACETREATY, AIGIVEPEACETREATY, USERPEACETREATY). The labels list "Embargo / Against / War / Peace / Mutual Protection / Right of Passage". That suggests Civ3 relations are War or Peace only, with no ceasefire state (UNVERIFIED).

6.6 Gold-per-turn deals and reputation

  • What breaks a 20-turn GPT deal. Source: CivFanatics article "Trading Reputation" (https://civfanatics.com/?p=280). Quote: "declare war on that AI (including MA/MPP)", "break an MA or MPP", "the trade route between you and the AI is cut".

  • No reputation hit if the AI ends the deal. Source: same article. Quote: "Of course you get no rep hit if the payee decides to end the deal by itself." Destroying a third party under an MA also ends the deal harmlessly. Quote: "Your rep isn't affected."

  • Reputation is per AI, but news of a betrayal spreads. Source: same article. Quote: "Each AI has a record of your reputation separately." An AI may also treat you as untrustworthy if it knows you broke a deal with another AI it is not at war with. Confirmed by Conquests diplomacy.txt AIREJECTINFORMEDOFBROKENDEAL. Quote: "$CIVNAME5 has informed me that you have broken deals with them in the past."

  • A ruined reputation is effectively permanent for GPT deals. Sources: "Trading Reputation", quote: "once you break a gpt deal you'll never be able to make such deal again". CivFanatics Civ3 FAQ, quote: "will never again for the game accept per-turn payments or deals from you." The article names two workarounds: renegotiating peace, or signing an MA against the betrayed AI ("B will forgive your betrayal to A").

  • An AI war declaration during a deal does not hurt your reputation. Source: CivFanatics Civ3 FAQ. Quote: "the AI declares war on you when you have a 20-turn deal going on" (not counted against you).

  • The AI has specific refusal lines for each kind of bad record. Source: Conquests diplomacy.txt.

    • AIREJECTBROKENDEAL: "My people remember too well the deals you have broken with us in the past."
    • AIREJECTPASSAGEVIOLATION: "the last time we had a right of passage treaty with $CIVNAME1, you sort of attacked us, didn't you?"
    • AIREJECTMILITARYRETRACT: "You have failed to fully honor diplomatic agreements with us in the past".

    So the engine tracks at least three kinds of record: broken deals, ROP violations, and alliance/treaty retractions. Each also has an "informed of" version for third parties.

  • The advisor flags liars and betrayers. Source: script.txt DIPLOADVICEFOREIGN_LIAR / _BETRAYAL. Quotes: "$LEADER0 is a [known liar and cheat.]" and "We know that $LEADER0 has [betrayed our friends] the $CIVNOUN1."

  • Sneak attacks. Declaring war with any of your units inside the target's territory counts as breaking an implicit Right of Passage. Source: CivFanatics, "Reputation for sneak attacks" (Civ3, July 2002) (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/reputation-for-sneak-attacks.28206). Catt, quote: "Reputation hits come not because of war, but because of declaring war with troops in enemy territory." Lt. 'Killer' M., quote: "having a Scout/worker/any other unit in enemy territory when declaring war is enough to be a ROP-breaker".

  • Whoever declares takes the hit, and a lapsed treaty means war. Source: CivFanatics, "How does the AI calculate reputation?" (Civ3, Jun 2002), Bamspeedy (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/how-does-the-ai-calculate-reputation.26122). Quotes: "The rep hit goes to whoever actually does the declaration of war." and "No peace treaty = war."

  • Razing ruins reputation. Source: "The Road to Diplomatic Victory" (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/empire-management/the-road-to-diplomatic-victory/). Quote: razing an AI city "will destroy your reputation." Also: "if you trash your reputation no amount of gifts and bribery can fix it."

  • Failed espionage hurts reputation. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Espionage. Quote: "Remember though, failed missions can seriously damage your reputation."

6.7 AI attitude: levels and modifiers

Primary source: CivFanatics "AI Attitude Study" (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/ai-attitude-study/), tested on PTW 1.01. Quote: "This test was with the 1.01 version of PTW". In this study a negative number is good.

  • Bands. Quotes: "-11 and lower = Gracious", "-1 through -10 = Polite", "0 = Cautious", "1-10 = Annoyed", "11 through 100 = Furious".
  • Starting value. The baseline is 0. Sharing a culture group gives -1. Aggression level adds a difficulty-dependent amount. For example, on Regent-Monarch AL5 gives +3 and AL1 gives -1, and on Emperor-Deity AL5 gives +3 and AL1 gives 0 (study, summarised). Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Culture agrees on culture groups. Quote: "nations of the same global culture tend to start near each other and are more tolerant in diplomacy."
  • Government. Same government: -1 (study, summarised). Quotes: "-5 Favorite government (only applies if both you and the AI are in this government)" and "+4 Shunned government (only applies if you are in this government and the AI is not)".
  • Agreements. Quotes: "-5 If you have a Right of Passage with the AI." and "-10 If you have a Mutual Protection Pact with the AI." A common enemy in war gives -3, an alliance -2, and both together -5 (study, summarised). Damage done to a common enemy gives -1 to -3 per act, and razing an enemy city -7, capped near -15. This decays by 1 per turn.
  • Trade. A recent map or tech trade, or an active 20-turn deal, gives -1. Trading or giving a resource gives -5. Signing an embargo gives -1 with the partner and +10 with the victim (study, summarised).
  • Gifts. About -1 per 10 gold of gifted gold or techs, capped at -10. Quote: "This bonus decays at about 10 gold/turn."
  • Culture lead. -1 if you lead in culture, +1 if the AI leads (study, summarised).
  • Tribute. Quotes: "-1 You pay tribute to the AI.", "+2 You demand tribute from the AI. Whether they actually pay the tribute or not." and "+1 If you call the AI's bluff when he demands tribute".
  • Units in territory. Being asked to leave gives +1. Being told to leave or declare war gives +4. Both are temporary and reverse when you comply (study, summarised).
  • Permanent penalties (study). Quotes: "+4 for breaking a peace treaty with that civ.", "+1 if you raze the city of another AI.", "+12 if you raze a city of that civ.", "Nukes: Victim of the nuke adds +32!", "Other civs will add on +16". Summarised from the study: breaking an alliance +12 (others +2); breaking an ROP +4, or +6 with units inside; selling a worker +1; accepting a culture flip +1 and refusing one -1.
  • Failed espionage. Quotes: "+4 for failing at an espionage mission (any mission or attempting to plant a spy)." (temporary) and "+1 for failing at an espionage mission" (permanent).
  • Power lead halves the good effects. Quote: "Power lead: If you have a power lead, most of the good effects (negative numbers) are halved." The study says ROP is the exception.
  • Things with no measured effect (study, summarised): owning resources the AI lacks, sharing a landmass, and culture pressure on border cities.
  • BIQ personality fields (conquests.biq RACE, local dump). Each civ has AggressionLevel (stored -2..2, shown as 1..5 in the editor), FavoriteGovernment, ShunnedGovernment and CultureGroup. It also has the AI build flags "Never"/"Often" for offensive/defensive/artillery land units, settlers, workers, naval, air, growth, production, happiness, science, wealth, trade, explore and culture, plus NoWonders/NoSmallWonders and the Manage/Emphasize governor flags. Examples from the dump: "Germany aggr=2 fav=4 shun=3", "France aggr=-2 fav=4 shun=6", "Mongols aggr=2 fav=1 shun=5". Government indices: 0 Anarchy, 1 Despotism, 2 Monarchy, 3 Communism, 4 Republic, 5 Democracy, 6 Fascism, 7 Feudalism.
  • Barbarians are a RACE entry with no personality. Dump: "A Barbarian Chiefdom aggr=0 fav=-1 shun=-1 culture=-1". There is no diplomacy with barbarians: the Civilopedia GCON_Barbarians describes only villages and raiders, and no diplomacy text targets them. UNVERIFIED as an explicit rule.

6.8 AI trade valuation

  • The AI-human trade markup is hard-coded. It depends on difficulty, the AI's aggression level and attitude. The editor's AI-AI trade rate does not apply to it. Source: TheNiceOne, "AI-human trade revisited" (1.29, Jan 19 2003) (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-human-trade-revisited.42110; mirror https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/86979-ai-human-trade-revisited). The summarised table:

    Difficulty Markup by AL1..AL5
    Chieftain 0% at every AL
    Warlord 0, 0, 1, 2, 3%
    Regent 2% from AL2, +2% per AL (8% at AL5)
    Monarch 3% from AL2, +3% per AL (12% at AL5)
    Emperor 4% from AL1, +4% per AL, capped at 16%
    Deity 5% from AL1, +5% per AL, capped at 20%

    Buying: price = base x (1 + bonus). Selling: price = base / (1 + bonus). Quote: "the AI-human trade bonus may be reduced by 1 or 2 %" (at Polite or Gracious).

  • Tech price base. The human's research cost is divided by the AI cost factor on Monarch and above. Quote: "Monarch 0.9, emperor 0.8, deity 0.6." and "the human player's research cost is divided by the AI's cost factor". These match conquests.biq DIFF CostFactor (local dump: "Monarch costFactor=9", "Emperor costFactor=8", "Deity costFactor=6", "Demigod costFactor=7", "Sid costFactor=4", against Regent's 10).

  • The tech price falls with known civs and with civs that have the tech. Source: same post. Quote: "number of known civs and number of civs that already has the tech". The exact function is UNVERIFIED. The commonly cited "x1.5 to x2 depending on how many civs know it" formula is Civ4 (CvTeamAI::AI_techTradeVal; https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-valuation-of-techs.658224), so it is not used here.

  • Things that do not change the price (TheNiceOne, summarised): relative culture, relative military, culture group, and favourite or shunned government.

  • Attitude does change the asking price. Source: Zwingli, "AI Attitude and Trade" (Civ3 1.29, Jul 18 2003), Persia at Deity buying monopoly Radio (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-attitude-and-trade.59079/). Quotes: "Gracious** - 681 gpt (620 gpt)", "Cautious - 705 gpt (641 gpt)", "Furious (unlimited demands) - 939 (854)", and "For each attitude point drop below Cautious, Persia charged about 0.32% more for the tech". This conflicts with TheNiceOne (see Disputed).

  • AI-AI trade rate is a BIQ difficulty field. Source: conquests.biq DIFF AIToAITradeRate (local dump: Chieftain 110 rising by 10 per level to Deity 170, Sid 200). Vanilla README.txt, quote: "Editor: Exposed AI to AI trade rate."

  • Exploits removed by patches. Source: vanilla README.txt. Quotes: "You can no longer demand very large amounts of gold in diplomacy.", "Fixed AI exploit with regards to giving cities away in diplomacy." and "The AI no longer trades during the players turn."

  • The advisor grades deals on five levels. Source: script.txt DIPLOADVICETRADE_DEAL_*, which match diplomacy.txt AIWEAKREJECT/AINEUTRALREJECT/AISTRONGREJECT/AITOTALREJECT. Quotes: "This deal will probably be acceptable.", "We're getting close to a deal here.", "I doubt they will accept this proposal.", "They will probably be insulted by this deal.", "They would never accept such a deal."

  • Selling techs for large GPT can drain the AI economy for 20 turns. Source: "Crippling the AI's Research Capability" (https://civfanatics.com/?p=170). Quotes: "There is a limit to how much the AI will pay you" and "cripple their economic and scientific growth for 20 turns".

  • The AI offers loans. Source: diplomacy.txt AILOANOFFER. Quote: "We are always happy to lend you money, $PLAYER0 - at surprisingly high interest rates, that is." Loan terms are UNVERIFIED.

6.9 Tribute and demands

  • The AI demands tribute and threatens. Source: diplomacy.txt AIDEMANDTRIBUTE. Quote: "Think well, $PLAYER0: if you give us tribute, our friendship will continue. If not, we will have to seriously re-evaluate our relationship." If you refuse ("call the bluff"), the outcome is AIBLUFFISCALLED or war. Quote: "Very well, then. We shall leave you to think about the future".
  • The AI can also give in to your demands. Sources: diplomacy.txt AIGIVETRIBUTE, quote: "We will give you $GET3 as you request. But we do this not out of fear of you and your minions, but out of a love of peace." The user side, USERWILLGIVETRIBUTE: "We will give you what you want - this time."
  • Attitude effects of tribute: see 6.7 (+2 for demanding, +1 for calling a bluff, -1 for paying).
  • When the AI gives in is UNVERIFIED. The only formulas found ("boldness x 10%", "fear", "dominance") are from Civ1, Civ2 or Civ4 threads (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/some-notes-about-diplomacy.678722/ is Civ1; https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/foreign-relations.76336 is Civ2), so they are not used here.

6.10 Units in foreign territory

  • The AI warns, then demands withdrawal or war. Sources: diplomacy.txt AIBORDERWARNING, quote: "it appears that some of your troops have - inadvertently, I'm sure - wandered into our territory, near $CITY3. Kindly remove them." USERBORDERWAR (the human's version of the same threat), quote: "Remove your forces from our territory or declare WAR!"
  • A promise to withdraw creates a temporary implicit ROP. Breaking that promise counts as backstabbing. Source: Evie, "Reputation for sneak attacks" (Civ3, 2002). Quote (summarised from the page): a "We'll move our troops out asap" reply without a withdrawal creates a temporary ROP.
  • Diplomacy can remove units from territory. PTW 1.27-era readme, quote: "Hidden Nationality units are no longer forcibly removed from enemy territory via diplomacy." This implies other units are forcibly removed (civ3PTW/readme.txt). The exact teleport rule is UNVERIFIED.

6.11 Espionage

  • Two tiers. Diplomatic missions open with Writing. Spy missions need Espionage and the Intelligence Agency. Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Espionage_Missions. Quote: "You can launch certain diplomatic missions at any time after the discovery of $LINK<Writing=TECH_Writing>." Also "Spy missions become available after you have discovered $LINK<Espionage=TECH_Espionage>." TECH_Espionage: "Build an $LINK<Intelligence Agency=BLDG_Intelligence_Center> and you can conduct $LINK<Espionage Missions=GCON_Espionage>." conquests.biq BLDG has an "AllowsSpyMissions" flag.

  • There are no spy units. Source: CivFanatics Civ3 FAQ. Quote: "Spies are not units in the game." Civ3.com FAQ on Apolyton, quote: "will no longer require you to use spy units".

  • The mission list (C3C). Source: Conquests Civilopedia GCON_Espionage. Missions: Establish Embassy, Plant Spy, Steal Technology, Steal Military Plans ("reveals the locations of all their military units"), Initiate Propaganda ("If successful, the people will revolt and $LINK<defect=GCON_Conversion> to your side."), Expose Enemy Spy, Investigate City, Steal World Map, and Sabotage Production ("destroy all the $LINK<shields=GCON_Shields> in the production box of a rival city").

  • Plant Disease was removed in vanilla. Source: vanilla README.txt. Quote: "Plant Disease: This espionage mission has been removed."

  • Steal Plans shows units on the Military Advisor for one turn. Source: vanilla README.txt. Quote: "On the turn a Steal Plans mission is successful, when you select the civilization ... you can mouseover a unit and it's location will be marked on that screen's mini-map."

  • Which agent can do what (conquests.biq ESPN, local dump). Each line gives the diplomat flag, the spy flag and the base cost:

    • Build an Embassy: diplomat, not spy, base 20.
    • Investigate City: diplomat and spy, base 10.
    • Steal Technology: diplomat and spy, base 10.
    • Steal World Map: spy only, base 1.
    • Plant Spy: spy only, base 60.
    • Steal Plans: spy only, base 10.
    • Initiate Propaganda: spy only, base 100.
    • Sabotage Production: spy only, base 10.
    • Expose Enemy Spy: spy only, base 80.

  • Requirements. Investigate City and Steal Technology need only an embassy. The other five need the Intelligence Agency, an embassy and a planted spy. Source: CivFanatics, "Successful Espionage" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/successful-espionage.422226). Civ3.com FAQ on Apolyton, quote: "only against Civs with whom you've established an embassy and planted a spy".

  • War. Embassy missions stop at war because the embassy closes. A planted spy keeps working. Source: Civ3.com FAQ on Apolyton. Quote: "Even if you go to war and your embassy is closed, your spy will continue to function as long as it is not caught."

  • Cost formulas. Source: "A Study of Espionage Missions". Here dist is the distance to your nearest city, level is 1 for a town, 2 for a city and 3 for a metropolis, and the leading number is the BIQ base:

    • Embassy: "20 dist*level + base + pop".
    • Investigate: "10 dist*level + base * pop".
    • Steal Tech: "10 distlevel + base * techstechrate/100".
    • Steal World Map: "1 dist*level + base * mapsize".
    • Plant Spy: "60 dist*level + base".
    • Steal Plans: "10 dist*level + base * unit".
    • Propaganda: "100 dist*level + base * pop * (1 + opp_cult/your_cult)".
    • Sabotage: "10 dist*level + base * shield".
    • Expose: "80 dist*level + base".

    The study says "Carefully" halves the cost and "Safely" doubles it. The readme says the cost was exposed in the editor. Quote: "Editor: Added mission cost to Diplomats and Spies page."

  • Success chances (same study):

    • Chance of escaping (Immediate/Carefully/Safely): Embassy 40/55/65, Regular spy 60/75/85, Veteran spy 70/85/95.
    • Success is 80% if not caught, giving Embassy 32/44/52, Regular 48/60/68, Veteran 56/68/76.
    • Plant Spy: Embassy 50%, Conscript 20%, Regular 50%, Veteran 60%, Elite 75%.
    • Embassy and Investigate City always succeed.
    • Expose Enemy Spy always fails if there is no spy.

    The escape table is confirmed by Kool Keith and Jazzmail in "Steal Technology" (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/steal-technology.232487/post-5709659). Jazzmail, quote: "You have to multiply the values in the table by 0.8."

  • Spy experience comes from government. conquests.biq GOVT "SpiesAre": Communism and Fascism give veteran (2), the others regular (1). "DiplomatsAre" is regular for all. CivFanatics FAQ, quote: "the chances to plant a spy depend on your government, but it is around 50-60%."

  • Failure lockout.

    • Source: civ3PTW/readme.txt. Quote: "If you fail a spy mission, you will automatically fail any spy mission against that same civilization for next 2-4 turns."
    • The study (C3C) differs: a failed Plant Spy sets a flag that clears with a 1/3 chance each turn, and other failures do not set it (see Disputed).
    • MAS (Steal Technology thread), quote: "its more difficult to succeed a second time, as your opponent is weary."

  • A caught spy can lead to war. Source: CivFanatics FAQ. Quote: "If it is not successful, the AI will become mad at you and might declare war." The attitude cost is in 6.7.

  • Propaganda (study, summarised). Your spy is never caught. Each citizen's chance of being persuaded is:

    spy bonus (veteran 10) + government modifier + culture factor

    • 5 x garrison - courthouse (20) - capital (40) + 20 for your own nationals

    The city flips when at least 3/4 of its citizens are persuaded. Otherwise the persuaded citizens become "confused" for some turns.

    • Democracy is immune. conquests.biq GOVT Democracy has ImmuneTo=6, the Initiate Propaganda mission. script.txt, quote: "The $CIVADJ1 people are immune to propaganda."
    • Government modifiers (conquests.biq GOVT_GOVT BriberyModifier; rows are the sender's government, columns the target's, in the order Anarchy, Despotism, Monarchy, Communism, Republic, Democracy, Fascism, Feudalism):
      • Despotism 15/0/-10/-15/-20/-30/0/0
      • Monarchy 20/15/0/-5/-10/-25/0/0
      • Communism 25/20/5/0/-10/-20/0/0
      • Republic 30/25/10/8/0/-5/0/0
      • Democracy 35/30/20/10/5/0/0/0
      • Fascism 0/0/-5/-20/-10/-10/0/0
      • Feudalism 20/15/0/-10/-10/-20/-25/0
      • Anarchy 0 across the row.
    • Culture factors (conquests.biq CULT "chance of successful propaganda"): in awe of 30, admirers of 25, impressed with 20, unimpressed by 10, dismissive of 5, disdainful of 3.
    • Courthouse. Conquests Civilopedia, quote: "The effectiveness of enemy spy propaganda missions against the city is reduced."

  • Steal Technology is blocked against civs you are at war with. Source: rysingsun in the "Steal Technology" thread, and the Apolyton thread title "espionage: you can't steal techs from civs you are at war with". Combined with the war closing the embassy, this follows from 6.2. Whether a planted spy can steal tech during a war is UNVERIFIED.

6.12 United Nations (brief)

  • Periodic majority votes. Source: Conquests Civilopedia (Victory, Diplomacy). Quote: "After the $LINK<United Nations=BLDG_United_Nations> is built, there are periodic votes by the council to elect a Leader. To be elected, a candidate must win a majority of all votes."
  • Candidates. Quote: "There are always two, and sometimes three, candidates." A civ qualifies if it "have built the UN", or controls "at least 25% of the world's territory", or "at least 25% of the world's population". If only one civ is eligible, "the second candidate is the civilization with the largest population."
  • The UN owner decides whether to hold the vote, every 11 turns. Source: CivFanatics FAQ. Quote: the choice "comes around every 11 turns". Also script.txt DIPLOVICTORYVOTEOPTION: "Would you like to hold elections for UN Secretary-General?" and "Election was inconclusive; no majority was found."
  • An AI must be Polite or better to vote for you. Source: AI Attitude Study (summarised).

Disputed

  • Right of Passage tech. Vanilla Civilopedia says Writing ("Can now negotiate military alliance and right of passage"). Conquests BIQ and Civilopedia say Map Making. The C3C GCON_Embassies text still says Writing ("That done, you can sign Right of Passage agreements and Military Alliances"), a leftover from vanilla. Map trading: Map Making in vanilla, Navigation in C3C. For a C3C ruleset, follow the BIQ tech flags.
  • Does attitude change trade prices? TheNiceOne (1.29) found that attitudes below Polite give no extra markup ("no AI will demand more if falling below catious"). Zwingli (1.29) measured +0.32% per attitude point below Cautious, and Furious cost about 38% more than Gracious when combined with demands.
  • Spy failure lockout. The PTW readme says any spy mission fails automatically for 2-4 turns after a failure. The espionage study says only a failed Plant Spy sets a flag, cleared with a 1/3 chance per turn.
  • Embassy attitude sign. The AI Attitude Study lists "-2 If you have an embassy with the AI" (good) in one place, but its permanent-effects list reads it as +2.
  • AI reputation. Players disagree on whether AIs suffer reputation for their own treaty breaks. Myartar, quote: "There seems to be NO penelty to the AI on reputaion". Others are unsure.
  • Embassy and alliance gate. The CivFanatics FAQ and the Civilopedia say Military Alliance needs Writing. A forum post (search result) adds that a road or sea link to the AI capitals is needed for ROP and MA. That link requirement is likely confused with resource trade (UNVERIFIED).

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  1. First contact trigger. Adjacency, sight, or borders? Does meeting one civ ever give contact with its partners automatically?
  2. Ceasefire. Does Civ3 have one, and how long does it last? The "16 turns" figure is Civ2. The Civ3 text files have no ceasefire strings.
  3. Exact tech-trade valuation function in Civ3: how the number of known civs and civs owning the tech scale the price, and the wonder value term.
  4. When the AI gives in to tribute demands, and when it starts making its own demands (power ratio, aggression, attitude).
  5. When the AI agrees to or refuses ROP, MPP, MA and embargo (AINORIGHTOFPASSAGE: "Our bond is not sufficient to allow trust of this magnitude.") The attitude thresholds are unknown.
  6. How long attitude penalties last, beyond the study's decay rates (gifts at about 10 gold/turn, war damage at 1/turn). The temporary espionage +4 recovers over about 10-20 turns per the study, untested.
  7. Whether reputation ever recovers without the "renegotiate peace / ally against the victim" workarounds.
  8. Withdrawal on agreeing to leave territory: are units teleported out at once?
  9. Whether a planted spy can steal tech during a war.
  10. Loan terms offered by the AI (AILOANOFFER).
  11. AI pricing of cities, workers and maps in trades. There are no sources beyond "the AI values its towns highly" anecdotes.
  12. AI "won't trade techs with someone at war with its friend" or "won't trade while you're in a war you started". No Civ3 source was found. Such rules are attested for Civ4 only.
  13. Embassy-granting wonder (Marco Polo's Embassy): its presence and effect per version were not checked here. It belongs to the wonders section.

Back to the top

7. Map, terrain, resources, workers, barbarians and huts

Scope: Civ3 1.29f, PTW 1.27f, Conquests (C3C) 1.22, standard rules (conquests.biq). Scenarios, mods (C3X etc.) and the special C3C game modes are excluded.

Sources and how they are cited

Primary sources were read straight from the Steam "Civilization III Complete" install (D:\Steam\steamapps\common\Sid Meier's Civilization III Complete\, called <civ3> below). They are local files, not URLs, so each citation gives the file and the entry key.

Tag Source
BIQ <civ3>/Conquests/conquests.biq, the C3C 1.22 default rules. Read with this repo's QueryCiv3 (sections TERR, TFRM, GOOD, RULE, GOVT, DIFF, WSIZ). The quote is the field name and value.
PEDIA <civ3>/Conquests/Text/Civilopedia.txt (C3C). The quote follows the #KEY of the entry.
EDHELP The C3C editor help <civ3>/Conquests/Civ3ConquestsEdit.chm, decompiled. Cited by page name.
README-129 <civ3>/README.txt, the vanilla patch notes up to 1.29f.
README-PTW <civ3>/civ3PTW/readme.txt (PTW 1.27f).
README-C3C <civ3>/Conquests/readme.txt (C3C 1.20/1.22).
README-CMPL <civ3>/Civ Complete Readme.txt.
SCRIPT <civ3>/Conquests/Text/script.txt, the in-game messages.
MANUAL The original Civilization III manual (2001, vanilla). It came from a PDF text extract with no URL available, so page numbers are approximate.
INFOC CivFanatics Civ3 Info Center (Firaxis pre-release info): https://civfanatics.com/civ3/infocenter/

Web sources are cited by URL. Forum findings are community testing, not official documentation, so they are marked as such.

1. Terrain types

1.1 Base table (C3C BIQ TERR)

Terrain Food Shield Commerce Irrigation + Mine + Road + Defence % Move cost City allowed Colony Wheeled impassable Fort / Airfield / Radar / Outpost Pollution / global-warming effect (TERR "PollutionEffect")
Desert 0 1 0 1 1 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes none
Plains 1 1 0 1 1 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes becomes Desert
Grassland 2 0 0 1 1 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes becomes Plains
Tundra 1 0 0 0 1 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes none
Flood Plain 3 0 0 1 0 1 10 1 yes yes no all yes none
Hills 1 1 0 0 2 1 50 2 yes yes no all yes none
Mountains 0 1 0 0 2 1 100 3 no yes yes all yes none
Forest 1 2 0 0 0 1 25 2 yes yes no all yes base terrain (14)
Jungle 1 0 0 0 0 1 25 3 yes yes yes all yes base terrain (14)
Marsh 1 0 0 0 0 1 20 2 no no yes outpost only becomes Coast (11)
Volcano 0 3 0 0 0 0 80 3 no no yes none none
Coast 1 0 2 0 0 0 10 1 no no — none none
Sea 1 0 1 0 0 0 10 1 no no — none none
Ocean 0 0 0 0 0 0 10 1 no no — none none
  • Source for every cell: BIQ TERR fields Food, Shields, Commerce, IrrigationBonus, MiningBonus, RoadBonus, DefenseBonus, MovementCost, AllowCities, AllowColonies, ImpassableByWheeled, AllowForts/AllowAirfields/AllowRadarTowers/AllowOutposts, PollutionEffect. For example, Hills reads IrrigationBonus=0 MiningBonus=2 RoadBonus=1 DefenseBonus=50 MovementCost=2 Food=1 Shields=1 Commerce=0.
  • Meaning of the bonus fields. EDHELP "Irrigation (Terrain Terraform Bonuses)": "Determines the bonus to food production if a tile of the selected terrain type is irrigated." EDHELP "Road (Terrain Terraform Bonuses)": "Determines the bonus to commerce if a road is built on a tile of the selected terrain type."
  • A zero RoadBonus means no road can be built. README-129: "Restricted roads/railroads from being built on terrain that has no road bonus." So Volcano and water tiles cannot have roads.
  • Meaning of PollutionEffect. EDHELP "Pollution Effect": "Selects the terrain type (if any) that a tile of the selected terrain type will become if pollution stays on that tile for an extended length of time. For example, grassland would become plains, and plains would become desert." Value 14 is "Base Terrain Type".
  • Bonus grassland ("Grassland with shield"). PEDIA #TERR_Grassland_with_Shield: "Some grasslands contain mineral deposits ... These have a rock outcropping to symbolize this and produce a shield if worked in a city radius." README-PTW: "Updated placement of forests and jungles on bonus grassland to leave the bonus shield under the forest/jungle."
  • Fresh-water lakes are not a terrain type. README-PTW: "NOTE: Fresh water lakes are not a terrain type. They are composed of coastal tiles surrounded by land." PEDIA #GCON_Food nevertheless lists "Fresh Water Lake 2 cannot be irrigated", against 1 food for Coastal. See Disputed.
  • Mountains cannot hold cities. MANUAL p.~131 (Build/Join City): "You also cannot build on Mountains." This matches BIQ AllowCities=0. BIQ also has AllowCities=0 for Marsh and Volcano.
  • Wheeled units need a road. MANUAL ch.7: "wheeled units require a road to travel through Jungles and Mountains." BIQ also sets ImpassableByWheeled=1 for Marsh and Volcano.
  • Movement. EDHELP "Movement Cost (Terrain Properties)": "Determines the base movement cost for a tile of the selected terrain type."

1.2 Defence bonuses (cross-check)

PEDIA #GCON_Terrain_Combat lists Grasslands, Plains, Desert, Floodplains, Tundra, Ocean, Sea, Coast and Fresh Water Lake at 10%. It gives "Marsh 20%", "River 25%", "Forest 25%", "Jungles 25%", "Hills 50%", "Volcano 80%" and "Mountains 100%", with the footnote " This bonus applies to the defender if attacked from the other side of a river." These figures match BIQ exactly. The structural bonuses listed there are Fortified +25%, Fortress +50%, Barricade +100% and City +50%. The combat section owns those.

1.3 Despotism tile penalty (affects every tile yield)

  • PEDIA #GOVT_Despotism: "any city production square which produces more than two food, shields, or commerce in a despotic government instead produces one less."
  • The same penalty applies under Anarchy per the government section. That is not re-verified here.

2. Rivers

Effect Rule Source
Placement Rivers run along tile edges. PEDIA #TERR_River: "[Rivers] do not run [in] squares: instead they run along the edges of squares."
Commerce +1 commerce to any tile with a river on one of its edges. PEDIA #TERR_River: "Any square with a river along its edge produces one extra commerce if worked by citizen laborers within a city radius."
Fresh water Lets adjacent tiles be irrigated before Electricity. PEDIA #TERR_River: "rivers are sources of fresh water, allowing irrigation of adjacent squares without electricity."
Defence +25% (RULE.RiverDefensiveBonus=25), only when attacked across the river. It stacks with other bonuses. EDHELP "River (Defensive Bonuses)": "Default is 25. Note that this bonus is cumulative with other terrain and fortification-related defensive bonuses." The cross-river condition is from PEDIA §1.2.
Movement No movement bonus along rivers. Crossing a river cancels the road bonus until Engineering. MANUAL p.~59: "This game offers no movement bonus for river travel." and "Until you discover Engineering, your units do not enjoy the road bonus to movement when they cross a river." PEDIA #TECH_Engineering: "Roads now bridge rivers."
Flood plains Flood plains only appear along rivers, so they always carry the river +1 commerce. PEDIA #TERR_Flood_Plain: "[floodplains] appear along river banks". Sullla, https://sullla.com/Civ3/strat7.html: "The base numbers for floodplains tiles are 3 food, 0 shields, and 1 gold".
Ships Ships cannot enter rivers. MANUAL ch.7: "Ships cannot navigate any ground terrain in the game, including rivers, deltas, and flood plains."

3. Water tiles, harbour, offshore platform

  • The yields are in §1.1: Coast 1/0/2, Sea 1/0/1, Ocean 0/0/0 per BIQ.
  • Harbour. PEDIA #BLDG_Harbor: "Coastal, sea, and ocean squares produce one extra food."
  • Offshore Platform. PEDIA #BLDG_Offshore_Platform: "causes all worked coastal, sea, and ocean squares to produce one shield."
  • Movement safety. PEDIA #TERR_Coast: naval units risk loss outside coast "But after the discovery of astronomy, this danger vanishes." PEDIA #TERR_Sea: "[Sea] squares are significant barriers to trade and travel until the discovery of astronomy." PEDIA #TERR_Ocean: "... until the discovery of Navigation or Magnetism."
  • Resources on water. BIQ TERR/GOOD: Coast takes Fish, Sea takes Whales and Fish, Ocean takes none.

4. Tile improvements (worker jobs)

4.1 Allowed terrain and effects

Job Allowed on Effect Tech / resource prerequisite (BIQ TFRM Required, RequiredResource1/2)
Irrigate Desert, Plains, Grassland, Flood Plain. These are the terrains with IrrigationBonus > 0. + the terrain's IrrigationBonus in food (+1 on each). none. Irrigating without fresh water needs Electricity.
Mine Desert, Plains, Grassland, Tundra (+1); Hills, Mountains (+2). + MiningBonus in shields. none
Road All land except Volcano. + RoadBonus commerce (+1); movement cost 1/3 (RULE.MovementAlongRoads=3). none
Railroad A tile that already has a road. Movement free; raises irrigation and mine output (see §4.3). Steam Power + Iron + Coal
Plant Forest Plains, Grassland, Tundra (BIQ WorkerJobAllowed=Plant Forest). Tile becomes Forest. Engineering
Clear Forest Forest Becomes its underlying base terrain; 10 shields go to the nearest city. none
Clear Wetlands Jungle, Marsh Becomes Grassland or Flood Plain. none
Clear Damage Polluted, fallout or cratered tiles Removes the damage. none
Fortress Land with AllowForts=1. +50% defence, ZOC. Construction
Barricade (C3C) A fortress tile. +100% defence; stops enemy ground movement. Construction
Airfield (PTW+) AllowAirfields=1, neutral or own land. Air base. The worker is consumed. Flight
Radar Tower (PTW+) Own territory only. +25% attack and defence within 2 tiles. The worker is consumed. Advanced Flight (C3C 1.20+)
Outpost (PTW+) Neutral or own land. Vision 2/3/4 on flat/hills/mountains. The worker is consumed. Masonry
Colony A tile with a tradable resource outside your borders. Gives access to the resource. The worker is consumed. none

Sources for §4.1:

  • BIQ TFRM: Fortress Required=20 (Construction), Railroad Required=44 RequiredResource1=1 RequiredResource2=3 (Steam Power, Iron, Coal), Plant Forest Required=23 (Engineering), Airfield Required=58 (Flight), Radar Tower Required=63 (Advanced Flight), Outpost Required=1 (Masonry), Barricade Required=20. Mine, Irrigation, Road, Clear Forest, Clear Wetlands and Clear Damage all have Required=-1.
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Irrigation: "Only squares adjacent to fresh water (river or lake) or to another irrigated square may be irrigated. After the discovery of Electricity, you can irrigate a tile without proximity to fresh water."
  • MANUAL p.114 (Irrigate): "A suitable square can always be irrigated if it shares a side or a diagonal with a source of fresh water (terrain with a river running through it, a freshwater lake, or another irrigated square)." So the adjacency includes diagonals.
  • MANUAL p.113 (orders table): the Irrigate row lists "Desert, Grassland, Plains, Flood Plains". The Mine row lists "Desert, Hills, Mountains, Plains, Grassland". The Reforest row lists "Engineering Grassland, Plains, Tundra".
  • PEDIA #GCON_Food: "Hills 1 cannot be irrigated", "Tundra 1 cannot be irrigated", "Desert 0 1 if irrigated".
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Mine: "Grasslands 0 shields, 1 if mined. w/ outcropping 1 shields, 2 if mined. Plains 1 shields, 2 if mined. Hills 1 shields, 3 if mined. Mountains 1 shields, 3 if mined."
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Road: "units moving along roads expend one third of a movement point per square, regardless of terrain type." The table there gives +1 commerce with a road on Floodplains, Grasslands, Plains, Hills, Mountains, Forests and Jungles. PEDIA #GCON_Commerce adds "Marsh 0 1 with road."
  • MANUAL p.~86: "All terrain types produce commerce once penetrated by roads."
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Railroad: "Workers can build railroads after the discovery of Steam Power, but only if your civilization has access to the Strategic Resources Iron and Coal." MANUAL p.116: "You can only build them where you have already built roads."
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Clear_Forest: "contribute 10 shields to the nearest controlled city. The resulting terrain will be either grasslands, plains, or tundra, depending upon the base terrain beneath the forest." BIQ RULE.ForestValueInShields=10.
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Clear_Wetlands: "A worker can clear the jungle or marsh from a square, creating either floodplains or grasslands."
  • PEDIA #TFRM_Plant_Forest says "any grassland or plains square". BIQ and MANUAL also allow Tundra. README-129 (v1.16f): "Forests planted on tundra always display as pine." See Disputed.
  • Forest shields cannot be used for wonders. Apolyton (piratebrun, quoted in https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-play-the-world/85881-questions-about-terrain-and-the-explaination-the-prima-s-strategy-guide-gives): forests "CANNOT be harvested to speed up PALACES, SMALL WONDERS or WONDERS." This is community testing; the cities/production section should confirm it.
  • Fortress. PEDIA #GCON_Fortresses: "They get a 50% defensive bonus if attacked." and "They have a zone of control and fire at passing enemy units without receiving return fire." BIQ RULE.FortressDefensiveBonus=50.
  • Barricade. PEDIA #GCON_Barricades: "A Barricades can only be built on a square already containing a Fortress. Even if unoccupied, barricades automatically stops movement of a ground unit entering its square" ... "They get a 100% defensive bonus if attacked" ... "Barricades become a fortress if their square is pillaged."
  • Airfield. PEDIA #GCON_Airfields: "Construction of the Airfield consumes the worker." and "If it falls into the territory of another civilization, however, the airfield becomes solely useable by the player whose territory it lies in (unless they have yet to research Flight, in which case the airfield is destroyed)." The GCON text says "Airfields can only be built in tiles that have no other improvements, an airfield, a colony, a road or a railroad." PEDIA #TFRM_Airfield says "Airfields can only be built in tiles that are empty or have a road and/or railroad."
  • Outpost. PEDIA #GCON_Outposts: "The range of vision for the Outpost is 2 tiles on flat terrain, 3 on hills and 4 on mountains." ... "If an Outpost falls within the territory of any other civilization, it will be destroyed." ... "Outposts can only be built in a tile that is empty, has a mine, irrigation, a road and/or a railroad. You cannot build an Outpost on a tile with an Airfield or a Colony."
  • Radar Tower. PEDIA #GCON_Radar_Towers: "The range of the effected area is 2 tiles. Friendly units within the Radar Towers range get a +25% attack and defense bonus. This bonus can only be applied once, even if multiple towers overlap." ... "Should a Radar Tower fall into the territory of another civilization, it will be destroyed." README-C3C v1.20: "Radar Towers require Advanced Flight" and "Radio Tech removed".
  • Colony. PEDIA #TFRM_Build_Colony: "A colony is necessary only if the resource is not inside your borders. Note that when a city's borders expand to include a colony, the colony disappears because it is no longer needed. The worker is consumed by building a colony." PEDIA #GCON_Colony: "you need a road connecting the colony to one of your cities to get the benefits of the resource." SCRIPT #COLONY_BARBS: "Our $RESOURCE0 colony was overrun by a tribe of $BARBARIAN1s." So barbarians destroy colonies.
  • City tiles and roads. README-129: "Changed city code so that a road/railroad is not placed under a city if a road/railroad is not allowed on the terrain type the city is built upon." README-C3C v1.20: "Railroads will always appear under captured cities if you have Steam Power."

4.2 Craters, pollution damage

  • PEDIA #GCON_Craters: "When a bombardment attack which eliminates the last unit or terrain improvement in a square, a crater may be formed. Like pollution, the presence of craters reduces a square's production, commerce, and food output by one."
  • MANUAL p.89 ("Pollution's Effects"): "It reduces the production of food, raw materials, and commerce income to zero. Once the terrain is detoxified, production returns to pre-pollution levels." See Disputed.
  • Long-term pollution transforms the terrain (PollutionEffect, §1.1). PEDIA #GCON_Pollution (Global Warming): "It can cause forests and jungles to disappear, and grasslands to become plains, and plains to become desert. World pollution is the sum of all city pollution, nuclear explosions, and meltdowns." SCRIPT #GLOBALWARMING_RISING_COASTLINES: "Coastlines are rising!" This matches Marsh→Coast in BIQ.
  • MANUAL p.88: "Pollution appears within the City Radius of any city that is excessively productive (produces lots of shields) or has a very large population. ... The number of symbols corresponds to the probability each turn of a square within the city radius becoming polluted." The pollution formula belongs to the cities section.

4.3 Railroad yield bonus

  • PEDIA #GCON_Worker_Jobs: "Increases irrigation or mine output, and provides unlimited movement." MANUAL p.116: "Railroads also increase the yields of both irrigation and mines."
  • Community tests, Apolyton (David Weldon), https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406: "RR through something that has been irrigated = +1 food. RR through something that has been mined = +1 shield." "RR through forest does nothing. RR through unimproved squares? Nothing." "on a coal mine with RR I get 5, not 6".
  • Community tests, CFC (Yeti), https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/does-railroad-provide-extra-shield-on-mined-grassland-plain.74236/: "If there's a mine, RR adds 1 shield." and "If there's irrigation, RR adds 1 food." Also "Tundra + Mine + Railroad = 1 food, 2 shields, 1 gold".
  • Sullla, https://sullla.com/Civ3/strat7.html: forests "cannot be irrigated or mined, and they therefore get no benefit from rails." Deserts "with railroads can actually manage 2 food".
  • Working rule: +1 food on an irrigated tile and +1 shield on a mined tile. No bonus on an unimproved or forest tile. A competing reading exists (Disputed).

5. Worker job times

5.1 Base turns (TFRM "Turns to Complete")

Job C3C 1.22 (BIQ) PTW (CFC table)
Mine 12 12
Irrigation 8 8
Fortress 16 16
Road 6 6
Railroad 12 12
Plant Forest 18 18
Clear Forest 4 10
Clear Wetlands / Clear Jungle 16 16
Clear Damage / Pollution 24 24
Airfield, Radar Tower, Outpost 1 1
Barricade 16 — (C3C only)
  • C3C column: BIQ TFRM TurnsToComplete, for example Name=Clear Forest TurnsToComplete=4.
  • PTW column: Shiplord, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/hi-how-many-turns-for-each-worker-action.80432, which gives both a C3C table ("Clear forest 4") and a PTW table ("Clear forest 10").
  • EDHELP "Turns to Complete": "Determines the base rate (how many turns it takes) for workers to complete the selected worker job. This value can be further modified, depending on government type."

5.2 The formula

turns = base × terrain movement cost ÷ (government WorkerRate × worker strength/100 × trait × tech multipliers), with several workers pooling their work.

  • Apolyton (Nero Would, 19 Dec 2001), https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/50097-worker-improvement-times: "To calculate the number of turns to complete a job, multiply the "effort" for the job by the basic Movement Cost"; "foreign workers only count as half a worker"; "The Work Rate is 1 for Anarchy, 3 for Democracy and 2 for all other governments."; "Also, divide by 2 for Industrious civs, divide by 2 if you have researched Replaceable Parts." The same page says the effect of roads and railroads on movement cost is ignored, so the base terrain cost is used.
  • EDHELP "Worker Rate (Government Properties)": "The "base" worker rate is 2. Each digit increases the rate by 50%. Thus, 1=50% work rate (bad), 2 = 100% (normal), and 3 = 150% (speedy workers!!)."
  • EDHELP "Worker Strength (Unit Statistics)": "If the Worker Strength value of a worker is set at "50" that worker works at "half" (50%) speed, and thus would take twice as long".
  • Observed data (DoubleEdged, CFC 80432, 1 Mar 2004, non-industrious, Ancient era) matches base × move cost ÷ 2: "Build Mine: (6 grasslands,12 hills,18 mountains)" and "Build Road: (6 in Forest,Hills; 9 in Mountains,Jungle; 3 grasslands)".
  • Jack Merchant (same thread): "'Base' turns is not for your regular non-industrious worker, but for slave workers (who work at half speed)."

Government worker rate (BIQ GOVT WorkerRate)

Government Anarchy Despotism Monarchy Communism Republic Democracy Fascism (C3C) Feudalism (C3C)
WorkerRate 1 2 2 2 2 3 4 2
PEDIA "Worker efficiency" 50% 100% — — — 150% 200% —

PEDIA #GOVT_Fascism: "Worker efficiency 200%". #GOVT_Democracy: "Worker efficiency 150%". #GOVT_Anarchy: "Worker efficiency 50%".

Other modifiers

  • Replaceable Parts. PEDIA #TECH_Replaceable_Parts: "{New Ability} Workers work twice as fast."
  • Captured or enslaved workers. README-C3C: "Note: Captured workers work at 50% the speed of regular workers." README-CMPL says the same. BIQ RULE.Slave=1 (Worker). EDHELP "Slave Unit": "settlers represent two population points, so a captured settler would convert into two workers."
  • Industrious. PEDIA #GCON_Strengths: "Workers complete tasks faster". The PEDIA gives no figure; see Disputed for the multiplier (×2 in vanilla/PTW, ×1.5 in C3C).
  • Rounding of fractional turns is not documented (UNVERIFIED).

5.3 Derived turns, one normal native worker, WorkerRate 2, no trait, before Replaceable Parts

This table is DERIVED from base × move cost ÷ 2. The cells marked "obs" match DoubleEdged's observed numbers.

Job Move cost 1 (Desert/Plains/Grassland/Tundra/Flood Plain) Move cost 2 (Hills/Forest/Marsh) Move cost 3 (Mountains/Jungle)
Road 3 (obs) 6 (obs) 9 (obs)
Irrigation 4 — —
Mine 6 (obs) 12 (Hills, obs) 18 (Mountains, obs)
Railroad 6 12 18
Fortress 8 16 24
Plant Forest 9 — —

6. Natural resources
