  • Source: BIQ GOOD Type (0 = bonus, 1 = luxury, 2 = strategic), FoodBonus, ShieldsBonus, CommerceBonus, Prerequisite, AppearanceRatio, DisappearanceProbability, and the BIQ TERR→GOOD bit array. For example, Name=Iron ... Type=2 AppearanceRatio=160 DisappearanceProbability=800 Prerequisite=7 ShieldsBonus=1.
  • The PEDIA #TERR_* "may contain" lists match the terrain column exactly.
  • The PEDIA bonus tables (#GCON_ResourcesB/L/S) match BIQ, except that Dyes and Incense are swapped. See Disputed.
  • README-129: "Game bonus resource now gives +2 food to its tile."
  • CFC Resource reference, https://civfanatics.com/civ3resources.shtml, gives the same prerequisites ("Rubber requires Replaceable Parts", and so on).

6.2 Resource rules

  • Placement count. EDHELP "Appearance Ratio": "In an eight-player game, a value of 160 would mean 2 of each resource per player. ... Setting this value to zero means the resource will be distributed at random. Note that resource placement is also limited by which terrain types can support the given resource." INFOC: "Appearance ratio has no effect on the bonus resources."
  • Disappearance. EDHELP "Disappearance Probability": "A value of 800 would mean that there is a 1 in 800 chance each turn to "lose" the resource. Setting this value to zero means the resource will never disappear. Note that this value is not influenced by use; a resource can disappear even if a civilization has never used the resource." and "Note 2: "Lost" resources will always re-appear somewhere else on the map, so the fundamental amount of resources in a game world will never drop below the amount set in the appearance ratio."
  • README-129: "Fixed bug which sometimes caused depletion of resources so that there would be < 1 per civ."
  • SCRIPT #GOODDISAPPEARED: "This source of $RESOURCES0 has been exhausted!" and #GOODAPPEARED: "We have discovered a new source of $RESOURCES0!"
  • Visibility. PEDIA #GCON_ResourcesN: "Bonus resources are always visible in explored areas of the map." and "Strategic resources appear on the map when a Civilization Advance exposes their usefulness."
  • Terraforming leaves resources alone. Apolyton (inca911), https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/42626-distilled-civ3-tips-and-notes?p=953406: "changing the terrain of a resource square does not change the resource as it did in Civ2 via terraforming." This is community testing.
  • Connection. PEDIA #GCON_Trade: "Two cities are connected if any one of the following are true: there is a road or railroad running between them, there are harbors in both cities and a visible water route between them, or both cities contain an airport." The same entry adds: "two cities are not connected if the only road/rail that connects them passes through enemy territory, or if a harbor is blockaded by enemy naval forces." PEDIA #GCON_Territory: tradable resources "are easier to get when found within your territory (no need for a colony)." INFOC: "To access any resource, you need to build a road to that resource."
  • One source is enough. PEDIA #GCON_Trade: "A single resource icon is enough to supply the needs of an entire civilization."
  • Required resources. BIQ TFRM Railroad needs Iron and Coal (§4.1). Units and buildings are covered in their own sections.

7. Flood plains, disease, plague

  • BIQ TERR TerrainFlags: the "causes disease" bit is set on Flood Plain, Jungle and Marsh. The "cured by Sanitation" bit is set on Flood Plain only. DiseaseStrength=50 on all of them.
  • PEDIA #GCON_Disease: "[Disease] kills citizens and units that spend too much time in unhealthy areas like floodplains, jungles and marsh. Military units that fortify in these terrains are susceptible to disease." ... "Your workers can clear jungles or marsh and eliminate that risk. Floodplains continue to be risky until you discover sanitation."
  • PEDIA #TECH_Sanitation: "Disease from Floodplains is eliminated, and the effect of Plague is reduced."
  • Workers are immune. README-129: "Corrected Disease Civilopedia chapter to reflect workers are immune to disease."
  • EDHELP "Disease (Terrain Properties)": "By default, both Floodplain and Jungle are flagged as disease-causers. The "Disease Strength" dialog indicates how often disease will strike the indicated terrain. As an example, both Floodplain and Jungle have a 50% disease strength; the higher the number, the stronger the disease." See Disputed on marsh.
  • SCRIPT #UNITJUNGLEDISEASE: "Our $UNIT0 has died of disease from the jungle!" and #SUMMARY_DISEASE: "Disease strikes $CITY0."
  • Plague (C3C). PEDIA #GCON_Plague: "Plague, if and when it occurs, can wipe out city population and land units (except Kings). The effect of plague is exacerbated in larger cities, walled cities and cities with commerce and a trade network."
  • No disasters in multiplayer. README-C3C: "Disasters such as plague and volcanic eruption do not occur in multiplayer games."
  • The per-turn disease probability formula is UNVERIFIED.

8. Volcanoes

  • Volcano tile: 0/3/0, defence 80%, move cost 3. It allows no cities, colonies, roads or improvements (BIQ).
  • EDHELP "Volcano Eruptions (Disasters)": "Each volcano on the map will erupt at least once within the indicated eruption period (shown in years)."
  • PEDIA #GCON_Volcanic_Eruptions: "Most of the time, however, a dark cloud begins to emanate from the volcano's "mouth" a few turns in advance of an eruption -- but such a warning cannot be guaranteed." and "When a volcano erupts it spews forth lava -- in the form of pollution -- that will mar the landscape of many of the tiles adjacent to the volcanic mountain." Workers clear the fallout with Clear Damage.
  • SCRIPT: #VLC_ACTIVE "Volcano is now active!", #VLC_KILLS_UNIT "Our units were burned to a crisp by a lava flow!", #VLC_KILLS_CITY "$CITY0 is torched by the volcanic eruption!"
  • Community (Optional, CFC), https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/questions-regarding-volcanos.330741: "the chance decreases significantly as the game goes on." The cause is that the period is in game years.
  • The damage radius is disputed (Disputed list).

9. City radius and city spacing

  • 21-tile radius. PEDIA #GCON_Radius: "A square is part of a city's radius if it is within the city's cultural borders, and within two squares of the city." MANUAL p.75: "The potential area of development, called the City Radius, extends out from a city in an area three map squares wide-- two squares to the northeast, northwest, southwest, and southeast. The resulting "radius" looks like a fat X." MANUAL ch.3: "each city can assign citizens to generate resources in any of the 20 surrounding terrain squares. Since this city is new, however, the workable radius is temporarily limited to the nearest eight. The pattern of 21 squares with the city at the center is called the City Radius."
  • No radius growth in standard C3C. No base-game, PTW or C3C source found mentions the radius growing past the 21-tile fat cross. The C3C PEDIA #GCON_Radius text is unchanged. Larger radii exist only in mods (C3X), which are out of scope.
  • The city tile is always worked. MANUAL p.75: "The terrain a city occupies is especially important, because it is always being worked. You cannot take the workforce off this square".
  • Minimum distance. MANUAL p.~131: "Note that you cannot build cities in terrain squares directly adjacent to an existing city." So two cities need at least one tile between them, which makes the minimum distance 2. A Steam thread (https://steamcommunity.com/app/3910/discussions/0/3453716885198230413) shows the editor has no setting for this: "I didn't find an option for that in the editor." It is changeable only with C3X, which is out of scope.
  • Terrain under a new city. Apolyton (piratebrun, thread 85881 above): when a city is built on a forest, "it is converted to its base terrain." This is community testing.

10. Map generation basics (cheaply sourced only)

World sizes (BIQ WSIZ)

Size Width × Height Default civs DistanceBetweenCivs OptimalNumberOfCities TechRate
Tiny 60×60 4 11 14 160
Small 80×80 6 11 17 200
Standard 100×100 8 12 20 240
Large 130×130 12 18 28 320
Huge 160×160 16 24 36 400
  • EDHELP "Distance Between Civs": "Determines the desired minimum distance (in tiles) between the starting locations of civilizations on maps of the selected world size."
  • World setup. MANUAL p.~45: "Arid: Choosing this option gives your world a larger number of dry terrain squares, such as Plains and Desert. ... Wet: This option produces a larger number of wet terrain squares, such as Jungle and Flood Plain."
  • Seed. README-C3C v1.20: "Implemented ability to enter a seed value on the world setup screen." README-129: "Made a slight change to placement of goody huts and bonus grassland tiles." The generator algorithm itself is UNVERIFIED and out of cheap scope.

11. Barbarians

11.1 Activity levels

Level Meaning
No Barbarians "No goody huts and no barbarian camps"
Sedentary "Goody huts only; no barbarian camps" (EDHELP). The MANUAL instead says "Barbarians are restricted to their encampments. The surrounding terrain is free of their mischief."
Roaming "Lowest level of barbarian camp activity". MANUAL: "less frequently and in smaller numbers than at higher levels. This is the standard level of barbarian activity."
Restless "Median level of barbarian camp activity". MANUAL: "appear in moderate up to significant numbers, at shorter intervals than at lower levels."
Raging "Highest level of barbarian camp activity". MANUAL: "The world is full of barbarians, and they appear in large numbers."
Random "Randomly-selected level of barbarian camp activity"
  • Source: EDHELP "Barbarian Activity (Scenario Properties)" for the quoted level meanings, and MANUAL p.~45 as noted.
  • README-129: "Added Barbarian Activity: No Barbarians."
  • EDHELP "Select Barbarian Activity": "This controls how often barbarians will spawn, and how far they will stray from their encampments."

11.2 Camps, raids, uprisings

  • Camps replace leaders. MANUAL p.90: "If you find and obliterate an active tribe's village, you end the threat from that tribe (and get a bit of spare change in the process)." and "The village takes the place of the barbarian leader."
  • Camp dispersal pays 25 gold. SCRIPT #CAPTURE_BARBARIAN: "We dispersed a $BARBARIAN0 encampment and took 25 gold!"
  • No barbarian leaders and no ransom in Civ3. MANUAL p.~49 and INFOC: "There are no barbarian leaders. To get their gold, seek out and invade the barbarian encampments." The leader ransom does not exist. The CFC "ransom 50/100/150" thread (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/barbarian-leaders.27479) is about Civ2.
  • Where camps respawn. MANUAL p.90: "new ones arise in areas that are outside your cultural borders, in areas that are not currently seen. As time passes, they appear at even farther distances from civilization."
  • Coastal camps. INFOC: "Barbarian villages on the coast can spawn seagoing vessels."
  • Raids on cities. INFOC: "Barbarians do not capture undefended cities. Now, they just pillage the place, take some of your gold, and move on." SCRIPT #BARBARIAN_CAPTURE_CITY_PRODUCTION: "Our work on ... has been destroyed!" #BARBARIAN_CAPTURE_CITY_POPULATION: "Some of our people were lost!" #BARBARIAN_CAPTURE_CITY_GOLD: "They have carried away $NUM0 gold!" #BARBARIAN_DESTROY_WALLS: "They have destroyed the $WALLS3!"
  • Uprisings. INFOC (pre-release): "Barbarian uprisings are triggered the second time a civ enters a new age." SCRIPT #SUMMARY_BARBARIAN_EXPLOSION_CITY: "We have heard reports of a massive barbarian uprising near $CITY0!" Community (Apolyton, https://codehappy.net/apolyton/threads/40765-1.htm): "The major uprisings come from a camp that you neglected to wipe out." Harovan: "Their number seems to depend on the barb setting (raging produces stacks of about 24)." ledj: "only 8 barb horsies will show on an uprising episode" on Roaming.
  • Spawn limits (community, TimBentley 2008). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/barbarian-information.267101: "No barb camps will appear until everyone has settled and there are at least (2*NumPlayers) cities." "The maximum number of camps is the number of players". The maximum number of barbarians is "2, 3, or 4" "(for roaming, restless, and raging) * NumPlayers."
  • Units. BIQ RULE BasicBarbarianUnitType=6 (Warrior), AdvancedBarbarianUnitType=11 (Horseman), BarbarianSeaUnitType=29 (Galley). EDHELP: "Default is Warrior." / "Default is Horseman." / "Default is Galley." No source was found for barbarian units changing by era in the standard rules; open question.
  • Combat bonus against barbarians (BIQ DIFF AttackBonusAgainstBarbarians, %):
Chieftain Warlord Regent Monarch Emperor Demigod Deity Sid
800 400 200 100 50 25 0 0

EDHELP: "Determines the attack bonus enjoyed by any player (human or AI) against barbarians. A value of zero means the barbarians have no combat disadvantage". How it enters the combat formula belongs to the combat section.

  • Expansionist. PEDIA #GCON_Strengths: "passive minor barbarians are friendlier." MANUAL: "Barbarian villages are more lucrative."

12. Goody huts

12.1 Outcomes

Outcome In-game message (SCRIPT) Condition (CFC 93244, copied from Apolyton)
Gold #GOODY_MONEY "We got $NUM0 gold from the $BARBARIAN0 tribe's village." "The tile must not have any type of resource or luxury on it."
Map #GOODY_MAPS "The friendly $BARBARIAN0 tribe gave us maps of their region." "always available"
Nothing #GOODY_NOTHING "This $BARBARIAN0 village is deserted." "always available"
Tech #GOODY_TECH "The $BARBARIAN0 tribe has taught us ..." "Player must still be in Ancient Times."
Free unit ("mercenaries") #GOODY_MERCENARIES "This friendly $BARBARIAN0 village gave us a skilled $UNIT1." "There must be a unit available to the Barbarians as well as the player"
Settler #GOODY_SETTLERS "A friendly $BARBARIAN0 $UNIT1 wants to join our $GOVERNMENT2." "Player must not have a settler (active or in production) or any unit with the Settle AI strategy." "Player must have less cities than (TotalCities / NumActivePlayers)."
City #GOODY_CITY "An advanced $BARBARIAN0 village has joined us!" no listed condition. Community: "On deity you will never get a city".
Barbarians #GOODY_BARBARIANS "We have disturbed an angry $BARBARIAN0 $UNIT1." "Player must not have Expansionist trait." "There must not be a city within a 1-tile radius." "The player must have at least 1 city." "The player must have at least 1 military unit." "The unit popping the hut must not have the "All Terrain As Roads" ability."
  • Condition source: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/probabilities-of-goody-huts-c3c.93244 (C3C; the author built an Excel odds calculator; the attached image and file were not readable).
  • The MANUAL p.91 list of outcomes matches: town, advance, gold, military unit, hostile units, deserted, settler, map.
  • Difficulty. INFOC: "Greater chance of getting goodies when you visit a goody hut on lower levels." INFOC: "Conscript units can be received when exploring goody huts".
  • Expansionist (community). Same thread 93244: "expansionist can only get gold, map and warrior (equal probability." and "None-expansionists will only get map, warrior and barbarians" (both said of a specific high difficulty). Also "On deity you will never get a city, but settler is quite likely for expansionists."
  • Settler or city right after founding. Thread 93244: "You cant get a settler or city when building a city, even if you use your last settler to build the city."
  • Community odds (small samples). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/goody-hut-probabilities.24911/latest:
    • Regent: tech about 30%, gold about 10%, unit about 10%, map about 10%, barbarians about 35%, deserted about 5%.
    • Chieftain: tech about 50%, gold about 15%, unit about 10%, barbarians about 10%, map about 10%, nothing about 5%. "only Warriors and Horsemen are produced".
    • Deity, non-expansionist, after the first city: deserted about 55%.
  • Huts and barbarian settings. EDHELP: huts exist on Sedentary and above; "No barbarians" means "No goody huts". EDHELP "Goody Hut (Tile Properties)": a hut cannot share a tile with "a barbarian camp or starting location".
  • Bug fixes. README-129: "Fixed bug in Civ3 where Scout runs an extra space after revealing a goody hut." README-129 also changed hut placement in map generation (§10).

Disputed

  1. Railroad bonus. PEDIA and MANUAL say only "increases irrigation or mine output".
    • Community tests: +1 food if irrigated, +1 shield if mined, nothing on unimproved or forest tiles (Weldon on Apolyton 42626; Yeti on CFC 74236; Sullla).
    • Strollen (same Apolyton page): "railroads do work as they say in the manual that add 50% to any improvement (fractions round up)".
    • A Civ3 fandom wiki page (https://civilization.fandom.com/wiki/Improvement_(Civ3), not fetchable, HTTP 402) is reported to say "+1 shield to any tile that already produces at least one shield". No verbatim quote was obtainable.
    • Recommended: +1 food / +1 shield on improved tiles.
  2. Industrious worker multiplier.
    • Shiplord (CFC 80432): "In VC3/PTW an industrious native worker has a work rate of 4." / "In C3C, that same worker has a work rate of 3." That is ×2, then ×1.5.
    • Padma (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/industrious-civs.70768/post-1408719): "In versions prior to Conquests, the Industrious Workers worked at 200% the speed" / "In Conquests, they toned down the Industrious capabilities to only 150% on Non-Industrious (IIRC)."
    • Anarres (same thread): "I thought the bonus was set at 175% for C3C, not 150%?"
    • Frostyboy (same thread): "industrious civs work beteween 25% and 33% faster depending on the task."
    • Nero Would (2001, vanilla): "divide by 2 for Industrious civs".
    • Jack Merchant's figures ("2 for a road, 3 for irrigation and 4 for rails/mines") fit ×1.5.
    • Recommended: ×2 for vanilla/PTW, ×1.5 for C3C.
  3. Industrious and captured workers.
    • Frostyboy: "Captured workers work at half speed of NON-industrous workers whether or not the tribe you are playing is industrous."
    • Jack Merchant: "the industrious trait does NOT make slave workers faster."
    • Anarres: "a bug AFAIK ... they seem to have re-introduced the bug for Conquests."
    • bg2soatob: "My captured workersw always got the double bonus."
  4. Mountain worker multiplier. Nero Would and DoubleEdged's observations give 3× (mountain mine 18 turns). BNut on Apolyton 50097: "if its on a mountain it takes 4 times as long."
  5. Clear Forest base. C3C BIQ gives 4. The PTW table gives 10. A Civilopedia citation in CFC 80432 also gives 10 ("Clear forest 10").
  6. Pollution yield effect.
    • MANUAL: polluted tile output reduced "to zero".
    • PEDIA #GCON_Craters: "Like pollution, ... reduces a square's production, commerce, and food output by one."
    • An Apolyton bug report (https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/48566-bug-in-food-production) reportedly says polluted tiles still give food; it was not fetched verbatim.
  7. Road commerce terrains. PEDIA, MANUAL and BIQ all give +1 on every land terrain except Volcano. No counter-source was found, but in-game confirmation for Hills, Mountains, Forest, Jungle, Marsh and Tundra is advised.
  8. Plant Forest terrains. PEDIA #TFRM_Plant_Forest says "any grassland or plains square". BIQ, the MANUAL ("Grassland, Plains, Tundra") and README-129 (pine on tundra) also allow Tundra.
  9. Volcano yield. BIQ gives Shields=3. PEDIA #GCON_Shields gives "Volcano 0 cannot be mined".
  10. Volcano damage radius. Tom2050 (CFC 330741): "within the 8 tiles immediately surrounding the volcano"; "a city is ok if 2 tiles away". Commander Bello and timjack (https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/volcanoes.73430/latest): "They may damage up to two tiles away." / "random tiles within a two tile radius."
  11. Disease on marsh. BIQ flags and PEDIA #GCON_Disease include Marsh. EDHELP says "By default, both Floodplain and Jungle are flagged".
  12. Ocean food. BIQ Ocean is 0/0/0. Sullla: "ocean tiles produce only 1 food."
  13. Lake food. PEDIA #GCON_Food says "Fresh Water Lake 2". README-PTW says lakes are ordinary Coast tiles, and Coast is Food=1 in BIQ. The mechanism behind any +1 is unknown.
  14. Dyes and Incense commerce. BIQ gives Dyes +1 and Incense +2. PEDIA #GCON_ResourcesL gives "Dyes +0 +0 +2" and "Incense +0 +0 +1".
  15. Radar Tower prerequisite. PEDIA GCON/TFRM text and MANUAL say Radio. README-C3C v1.20 says "Radar Towers require Advanced Flight" and "Radio Tech removed". BIQ Required=63 is Advanced Flight. Use Advanced Flight for C3C.
  16. Sedentary barbarians. EDHELP: "Goody huts only; no barbarian camps". MANUAL: "Barbarians are restricted to their encampments."
  17. Hut barbarians before the first city. The Apolyton condition list requires "at least 1 city". etj4Eagle's Deity sample (CFC 24911) reports barbarians about 55% "before the first city".
  18. Airfield-eligible tiles. PEDIA #GCON_Airfields: "no other improvements, an airfield, a colony, a road or a railroad". PEDIA #TFRM_Airfield: "empty or have a road and/or railroad".

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • Goody hut gold amounts. No Civ3 source found for the low and high amounts. Exact outcome probabilities by difficulty are also unknown: the CFC 93244 calculator was not readable, and the CFC 24911 samples are small.
  • Hut tech outside the Ancient era. The condition says Ancient only, but a search summary of CFC 93244 quotes a commenter saying the Middle Ages odds are identical. That was not seen verbatim.
  • Mercenary unit choice. Which unit a hut gives, and how "Conscript" status is set, is unknown.
  • City tile yields. Free irrigation, a minimum of 1 shield or 1 commerce, and whether the city tile counts as an irrigation source were not sourced. Apolyton thread 85881 quotes the Prima guide as saying the city tile "is automacally improved in every way possible: irrigation, mining, and roads." Hand this to the cities section.
  • Whether rivers alone connect resources or cities for trade. PEDIA #GCON_Trade lists only road, rail, harbour and airport.
  • Rounding of fractional worker turns, and how several workers on one tile combine. "Divide by the number of workers" is the community view.
  • Global warming: the trigger thresholds and the per-turn odds.
  • Disease: the per-turn chance formula (DiseaseStrength=50) and its effect size.
  • Volcano eruption period. The default comes from the scenario/GAME data; conquests.biq has no GAME value read here.
  • Barbarian units by era in the standard rules: any upgrade beyond Warrior, Horseman and Galley.
  • Uprising trigger. The rule ("second time a civ enters a new age") comes only from pre-release INFOC.
  • The RULE.Slave default and the Worker Strength of captured workers in BIQ. The 50% may be a hard-coded flag rather than the Worker Strength field.
  • Hills irrigation. PEDIA, BIQ and Sullla all say hills cannot be irrigated. No counter-source was found. Listed here only because the question is commonly asked.
  • Search budget. The web search limit for this session was used up before the city-tile and hut-gold questions could be searched further.

Back to the top

8. AI behaviour, difficulty levels, civ traits, scoring and victory

Scope: Civ3 1.29f, PTW 1.27f and C3C 1.22, standard (non-scenario) games. The special Conquests game modes (Regicide, Mass Regicide, Elimination, Capture the Flag/Princess, Victory Points) are out of scope.

Each rule gives a source URL and a short quote from that source. Forum posts are labelled with the poster's name. "Tested" means the poster says they tested it. "BIQ (repo)" means the value is in C7/Lua/civ3/ruleset.json in this repository. _Console/RegenerateRuleset/readme.md says that file's difficulties and civilizations sections come from Civ3's conquests.biq, and that the only change is that "the highest is renamed from Sid to Creator". Those values are used here only to back up web sources, never as the only source. Treat them as secondary because the import code could have bugs.

1. Difficulty levels (BIQ DIFF section)

1.1 Table (C3C 1.22 defaults)

DIFF field Chieftain Warlord Regent Monarch Emperor Demigod Deity Sid
Citizens born content (human) 4 3 2 2 1 1 1 1
Max AI government transition time (turns) - - - 4 3 2 2 1
AI extra defensive units at start 0 0 0 2 4 6 8 12
AI extra offensive units at start 0 0 0 1 2 3 4 6
AI extra start unit 1 (Settler) 0 0 0 0 0 1 1 2
AI extra start unit 2 (Worker) 0 0 0 0 1 2 2 4
AI additional free unit support (per civ) 0 0 0 4 8 12 16 24
AI unit support bonus per city 0 0 0 1 2 3 4 8
Attack bonus vs barbarians (%) (human) 800 400 200 100 50 25 0 0
AI cost factor (human = 10) 20 12 10 9 8 7 6 4
% of optimal cities (human OCN) 100 95 90 85 80 70 60 50
AI-to-AI trade rate 110 120 130 140 150 160 170 200
Citizens quelled by military law 1 1 1 1 1 1 1 1
Score multiplier 1 2 3 4 5 6 7 8

Sources for the table:

  • Vanilla/PTW six-level table (Pyrkaige, 25 Nov 2002). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-difficulty-level-bonuses.37490/post-614347 gives Chieftain..Deity. It agrees with the table above except Deity, which it gives as OCN 70% and AI-AI trade 160 (the PTW values). Rows quoted: "AI cost factor (player is 10) | 20 | 12 | 10 | 9 | 8 | 6"; "Optimal-city % before corruption | 100 | 95 | 90 | 85 | 80 | 70".
  • PTW to C3C Deity change (Pyrkaige, 13 Nov 2003). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/difficulty-levels-ptw-vs-conquests.68642 reports "In PTW, the Deity AI to AI trade rate is at 160, while in C3C it's at 170." He gives the OCN as 70% in PTW and 60% in C3C. He also corrects the editor help: a Sid AI starts with 2 settlers and 4 workers.
  • Same change (TimBentley). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/civ3-conquests-additions-changes-list.104294/ says "The AI-AI trade ratio for diety increased from 160 in PTW to 170 in C3C, and the OCN decreased from 70% to 60%."
  • Demigod and Sid (Arathorn), same thread. "Demi-God AIs have a cost factor of .7"; "3 starting offensive units, 6 starting defensive units, 1 bonus settler, 2 bonus workers"; "Sid AIs have a cost factor of .4, 6 starting offensive units, 12 starting defensive units"; "human has 0% bonus vs. barbs and human has 50% of the normal OCN".
  • Sid (SirPleb, 14 Nov 2003). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/some-comments-after-playing-a-bit.68813 says "The big one - the AI's 'cost factor' is 4!" and "This means that the AIs need only 4/10th as much of anything as the human needs."
  • CFC FAQ. https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ lists the barbarian bonus as 800/400/200/100/50/25/0/0 and the AI anarchy cap as "4 turns on Monarch, 3 on Emperor, and 2 on Demigod". It also says "At Regent, the AI and you don't have any advantages nor disadvantages."
  • Score multiplier. See section 6.
  • BIQ (repo). difficulties in C7/Lua/civ3/ruleset.json has every value in the table above. Field names: numberOfCitizensBornContent, maxAiGovernmentTransitionTime, numberOfAIDefensiveStartingUnits, numberOfAIOffensiveStartingUnits, extraStartUnit1/2, additionalFreeUnitSupport, unitSupportBonusForEachSettlement, attackBonusAgainstBarbarians, aiCostFactor, percentageOfOptimalCities, aiToAITradeRate, militaryLaw.

1.2 What the fields mean

  • AI cost factor. It scales the AI's cost to build and research (units, improvements, wonders and techs), as a fraction of the human's 10. It does not change combat or movement.
    • https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-difficulty-level-bonuses.37490/post-614347 (thread summary): the cost factor applies to wonders, improvements and units, but not to combat or movement rates.
    • https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/ lists the bonuses above Regent as "extra starting units, faster production and research, and lower corruption".
  • Research penalty after patch 1.29f. For research, the patched game makes the human's techs cost more instead of making the AI's cost less. Tested by theNiceOne, 19 Jan 2003, patch 1.29: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/86979-ai-human-trade-revisited says "the human player's research cost is divided by the AI's cost factor (as found in the editor)". This only applies when the factor is below 1, so from Monarch up. Bamspeedy, in the 37490 thread above, also reports that later patches made the human, not the AI, the one adjusted by difficulty.
  • AI-to-AI trade rate. It sets how much AIs pay each other in tech trades (and maybe other trades). theNiceOne, same Apolyton thread: "the AI-AI trade bonus that can be found in the editor does not affect AI-human trade in any way." Nobody found the exact formula. Nor Me: "I cannot get reasonable results out of an AI-AI trade test."
  • % of optimal cities. This is a percentage applied to the human's optimal city number, the number of cities before corruption from city count rises. Arathorn (104294): "human has 50% of the normal OCN" at Sid.
  • Citizens born content, quelling and barbarian bonus apply to the human. The AI uses a fixed default level for these, and which level that is is disputed (see Disputed).
    • TheNiceOne (37490 thread) says the AI plays by Regent rules for the content, quelling, barbarian and corruption factors. Bamspeedy says the "default AI intelligence" setting controls this.
    • Pyrkaige (68642) says AI civs "play at the Monarch level (or did in PTW, and most likely still do)" for four variables.
    • BIQ RULE has a DefaultDifficultyLevel field (QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Rule.cs).
  • Max AI government transition time caps the AI's anarchy. CFC FAQ: "on Emperor, the cost factor is .8, and the AI gets a reduced anarchy time!"

1.3 Other AI advantages ("cheats") beyond DIFF

  • Map knowledge. The AI knows the map, and where resources will appear, before it should. These are community findings and are not confirmed by Firaxis.
    • TheNiceOne, 17 Jul 2002: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-complete-cheat-guide.27273/post-376681 lists as proven "The AI knows the map even before exploring." He says he "tested by making a simple map" and that the AI "went for future resources spots".
    • Bamspeedy, same thread: "If the AI has seen the map, it does in fact know if a future resource will be there or not."
    • TheNiceOne, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/the-ai-dont-cheat.33788/post-530614: "They know where your units are and a few other things."
  • No combat, border or wonder cheats (tested).
    • TheNiceOne (27273): combat "outcomes are as they should be" over many battles; "there's no AI wonder production cheats".
    • TheNiceOne (33788): on border rules, "the AI follows exactly the same rule as the human player."
  • AI galleys (Firaxis, second-hand). TheNiceOne (27273) reports that Soren Johnson said "an AI galley will never end a turn where it could sink."
  • Tech trading. On higher levels the AI charges the human more. theNiceOne (86979) gives the example "1000 / 0.6 * 1.15 = 1917 gold" on Deity.

2. Civilization traits (Conquests)

Each civ has two traits, and each trait gives one free starting tech. CFC FAQ https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "Each civilization has two traits, and each trait grants one starting technology." The trait bits are in BIQ RACE Flags[0] bits 0-7, in the order Militaristic, Commercial, Expansionist, Scientific, Religious, Industrious, Agricultural, Seafaring (QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Race.cs). The BIQ RULE value buildingDiscountForCivTraits is 0.5 (repo ruleset.json rules).

Trait Effect in C3C Free tech Source / quote
Militaristic Promotions are twice as likely. Barracks, Walls, Harbor and Coastal Fortress cost half. Warrior Code (or The Wheel) CFC FAQ: "Unit promotions to higher levels are twice as likely as non-MIL civilizations."; "Militaristic buildings like Barracks, Walls, Harbors, etc. are all half cost." Sullla https://sullla.com/Civ3/strat3b.html: "double the chance to promote every time one of their units wins a battle"
Commercial OCN +25%, which means less corruption. Extra commerce on the city tile at size 7+ and again at 13+. Alphabet CFC FAQ: "The OCN (Optimal City Number) is increased 25% for Commercial civilizations." The FAQ also says city centres get an extra commerce on becoming a City and again at Metropolis. Sullla (pre-C3C): "an extra gold piece for each city (size 7-12) and 2 extra gold for each metropolis (size 13+)".
Expansionist Starts with a Scout and is the only trait that can build Scouts. Never gets barbarians from goody huts, and gets better hut results. Pottery CFC FAQ: "Expansionist civilizations do not get barbarians from goody huts". Sullla: "they cannot get barbarians from goody huts."
Industrious Workers are +50% faster in C3C (+100% in vanilla/PTW). Extra shield on the city tile at larger sizes. Masonry CFC FAQ: Industrious workers build "100% faster in Vanilla and Play the World, and 50% faster in Conquests." Artifex: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/updated-trait-rankings-for-c3c-emperor.75226/ "+50% instead of +100%", no bonus for slaves.
Religious Temples and Cathedrals cost half. Anarchy lasts 2 turns in C3C (1 turn in vanilla/PTW). Ceremonial Burial CFC FAQ: "Religious buildings (Temples, Cathedrals) are all half price." The FAQ gives anarchy as 1 turn in Vanilla and PTW and 2 turns in Conquests. Artifex (75226): "2 turns instead of 1 turn".
Scientific Library, University and Research Lab cost half. Free tech on entering each new era. Scientific Great Leaders appear in C3C. Bronze Working CFC FAQ: "Scientific buildings (Libraries, Universities, Research Labs) are half price." The FAQ adds a free technology on entering a new age. Sullla: "free tech at the start of each era".
Agricultural (C3C only) +1 food on the city tile under every government except Despotism (under Despotism, see Disputed). Irrigated desert gives 2 food instead of 1. Aqueduct, Recycling Plant and Solar Plant are cheaper. Pottery (Masonry if also Expansionist) Arathorn (104294): "Provides one bonus food in the city square in all governments except despotism." TimBentley, same thread: "When in despotism, agricultural only adds 1 food to cities if they are on a river." LouLong https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/conquests-features-comprehensive-survey.61791: "Cheaper aqueducts, recycling plants and solar plants."
Seafaring (C3C only) +1 commerce on the city tile of cities on salt-water coast. Ships +1 movement and a lower chance of sinking. Coastal start much more likely. Cheaper Harbor (see UNVERIFIED). Alphabet (Pottery if also Commercial) Arathorn (104294): "Cities on the coast of salt water gain one extra commerce in the city square." LouLong (61791): "Ships have movement increased by 1"; "Decreased chance of sinking"; "Coastal start much more likely."

More trait rules:

  • Military Great Leaders. In C3C they can no longer rush Great Wonders, which affects Militaristic civs. stachnie (75226): "MGL's can't rush Great Wonders now".
  • C3C trait reassignments. Arathorn (104294) lists them, for example "Aztecs - from Militaristic, Religious to Agricultural, Militaristic" and "England - from Commercial, Expansionist to Commercial, Seafaring".
  • Golden ages come from owning Great Wonders that match the civ's traits, or from the first UU victory. That rule belongs in the golden-age section of this compendium and is not re-sourced here.

2.1 Per-civ AI personality (BIQ RACE, C3C defaults; BIQ (repo) values)

Aggression runs from -2 to +2 (Race.cs: AggressionLevel; // -2 to 2). Longasc's C3C list, in Ision's article https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/your-ai-opponent-by-ision.92917, uses a 1-5 scale. It puts Germany, Zulu and Mongols at 5 and France and India at 1, which matches +2 and -2 below.

The C3C setup screen also has an AI aggression setting that applies to all AIs. LouLong (61791): "Random World Size & Adjustable AI aggression levels."

3. How the AI behaves

3.1 Architecture and production choice (Firaxis)

  • Leader AI and unit AI. A leader AI handles the high-level picture and unit AIs ask it questions. Soren Johnson, interview of 26 Oct 2001: https://apolyton.net/forum/apolyton-civilization-site-general/civilization-lost-history/255541-soren-johnson-civilization-iii-interview-2-26-oct-2001 says "A leader AI keeps track of high-level concepts, like whether enough science is being generated". The same interview, as quoted in https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-general-archive/41443-interview-soren-johnson-on-ai?p=926594, says "the unit AI controlling a tank might ask the leader AI which enemy city would be best to attack."
  • Production. The city picks whatever item scores highest. Soren (255541): "the city simply builds the item with the highest value."
  • Personality. Leaders are modelled on history. Soren (255541): "an aggressive ruler preferring despotism and offensive ground units".
  • Naval and air. Soren (255541): "the AI is capable of launching naval invasions using a variety of land, sea, and air units". C3C added to this. LouLong (61791): "AI improvemement : picking bombardment targets; escorting key naval transports like carriers and transports."

3.2 Build-often / build-never flags (RACE) and unit AI strategies

  • The flags. RACE has three groups of flags:
    • Never flags in Flags[8..9]: Offensive, Defensive and Artillery land units, Settlers, Workers, Naval, Air, Growth, Production, Happiness, Science, Wealth, Trade, Explore and Culture.
    • Often flags in Flags[12..13], for the same categories.
    • Governor flags in Flags[4]: ManageCitizens, Emphasize Food/Shields/Trade, ManageProduction, NoWonders and NoSmallWonders. Source: QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Race.cs.
  • Small effect on units (tested, Ozymandias, 29 Nov 2023). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/manipulating-ai-unit-builds.686973 found the flags "Don't affect Unit Builds by more than ~5%". He also found "Selecting more than 3 (maybe 4) of these options will "confuse" the AI, causing it to ignore all of your selections". Predator145 (Jan 2025) gives a different, second-hand limit: "Alexmann who worked with Firaxis has confirmed many years ago that 6 is the max effective number". Source: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-build-often-building-categories.692300/
  • Which buildings each category covers (Predator145, 3 Oct 2024, same thread).
    • Growth: "Granary, Aqueduct, Hospital".
    • Production: Factory and power plants.
    • Science: buildings that multiply beakers.
    • Trade: "Improvements that give a gold income multiplier (market, bank, stock exchange)", plus Harbor and Airport.
    • Wealth: the shield-to-gold project.
    • There is no Military category: "there is no Military category to encourage the AI to build barracks/harbors/walls."
  • Unit mix (tested, Ozymandias, 686973, about 560 AI builds).
    • In peacetime the split is "D : O : other ~= 53% : 43% : 4%". The AI "likes" "between a 3:2 and 4:3 ratio of D:A units".
    • Artillery is about 1% of builds, and "the AI was proven not to take bombard into account."
    • The AI will "Re-Flag" a unit from offence to defence or back as it sees fit.
    • The AI keeps offensive and defensive units in balance ("YES" to "Does the AI seek equilibrium").
    • Builds are partly random, and difficulty and aggression did not change the mix.
  • Unit abilities the AI favours (tom2050, relayed in 686973): it "absolutely loves and adores" extra hit points. Blitz "would only increase the AI building them ... by around 4%."
  • The human can steer governors. Soren (255541): you can "tell its governors to never build artillery and air units."

3.3 Settling: spacing, gaps and expansion

  • Early spacing of about 4 tiles. Dominae, 31 Mar 2002, writing about a 4-tile spacing plan: https://apolyton.net/forum/miscellaneous/archives/civ3-strategy-archive/57896-early-gaem-deity-base-spacing-paradigm says "The AI pretty much follows this plan to the letter at all difficulty levels". Aeson, same thread: "I can sometimes build cities at a 5:6 ratio of what the AI does with 4 spacing."
  • Gaps that the AI later fills.
    • Brain, Mar 2005: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/other-civs-encrouching-on-my-borders.113953 says "The AI often leaves big gaps between their cities, which allows you to squeeze in."
    • The AI also fills empty land left inside a human's area. Grogs, same thread: the AI "just walked in and grabbed" an unclaimed area in the middle of his empire. Rohirrim: "If you can't block them completely, they just usually walk right past you and plop a city."
  • No settling in foreign borders or first-ring conflict.
    • Grogs (113953): "You can't build a city within another nation's borders, and the AI cannot build one within yours."
    • SJ Frank: "The AI will never settle a city that has first-ring conflict."
    • Exception: eldar says that if an AI is "reduced to a single settler, they will settle in place (if they can)".
  • Expansion.
    • Ision (92917) says the AI often builds settlers before its growth can support them.
    • "A sprawling Expansionist AI by the mid-Middle Ages is common".
    • The AI "will NEVER attempt a suicide run to find another land mass."
    • SirPleb (68813): "The AIs do not explore effectively, particularly large bodies of water."
  • Settler escorts. No Civ3-specific source was found (see UNVERIFIED). The thread on blocking AI settlers that the search found is about Civ5.

3.4 War: when it declares

No Firaxis formula was found. These are community observations from https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/when-does-ai-declare-war.244088:

  • MAS: "The most important factor is probably military strength."
  • Nergal: "General attitude, ie annoyed or furious are way more likely to attack than Cautious." Also "The Zulus and Germans are pretty much always up for a war" (aggression +2 in RACE).
  • Lord Emsworth: "If the AI gets something from you on per turn basis (esp.for free) it is extremely unlikely that you will be attacked." He also says that tribute demands follow aggression.
  • rysingsun: "they are far less likely to declare war if you have rop with them"; "they don't declare much when there is lots of land still to be settled".
  • civverguy: "they move a couple units in your borders and declare war on you the next turn."
  • I. Larkin: "if AI have reach the point that they can't support their military anymore they declare war no matter what..."
  • Units in their territory. The AI first asks you to leave. If you are still there the next turn, it can force you out or go to war. This is a forum summary from the search for "units in our territory" and has not been checked against a primary quote (see UNVERIFIED).

3.5 War: how it fights

  • Piecemeal attacks and poor artillery use. Ision (92917): AI attacks are "piecemeal", and "the AI has no concept of proper artillery use". AI armies are "rare as a white rhino".
  • Artillery mostly stays in cities (C3C). From https://apolyton.net/forum/civilization-series/civilization-iii/121250-does-the-computer-use-siege-units-in-conquests:
    • Jeem: "The AI does 'attempt' to use ground artillery offensively now, but it's still pretty rare".
    • ChrisiusMaximus: "they still seem to keep them holed up in their cities, and only seem to use them when I attack as a defensive strike."
    • Catt: "the AI won't usea stack of 10+ artillery units, with appropriate defense, to lay siege to a city".
  • Heavy garrisons, defensive escorts, quick naval retreat, small landings. Ision (92917): the AI keeps heavy garrisons in every city and escorts its offensive stacks with many defensive units. It retreats ships to port after minor damage, and its amphibious invasions use too few units.
  • AI-vs-AI wars. Ision: they are "wars of attrition" that drain the winner too.

3.6 Workers, sliders, government, trade

  • Too few workers. Ision (92917): "the AIs never-ever build anywhere near enough workers."
  • Luxury slider. Ision: the AI "will NOT use the luxury slider to compensate for any happiness issues". Instead it uses entertainers.
  • Changes government often. Ision: "Changing governments is a favorite AI past time, doing so FAR too often through the course of a game."
  • Government switching rules (chiefpaco, 27 Apr 2002). https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/about-governments.21298
    • A peaceful AI in Monarchy or Communism "often switches to Democracy or Republic".
    • After a long war it "switches to Comm or Monarchy".
    • "If the AI discovers a new govt while in Desp, it switches."
    • chiefpaco: "I think it is entirely based on War & war weariness."
    • It is unclear whether the favourite government is a goal or only affects attitude. Propaganda said that even with Democracy shunned, "all of them always choose Democracy." (see Disputed)
  • Scientific AIs broker techs. Ision: they gain techs and gold by trading techs on, and often end up with the most gold. Commercial AIs "rarely translates its inherent gold advantage out properly."
  • Early tech trading. eric2075 (68813): "the AI won't trade techs very easily in the early game either."

3.7 "The AI never / rarely does X" (community)

Observation Source / quote
Few or no armies Ision (92917): armies are "rare as a white rhino"
Coordinated artillery sieges Catt (121250), quoted in 3.5
Sending ships across ocean to explore Ision (92917): "will NEVER attempt a suicide run to find another land mass"
Ending a galley's turn where it could sink Soren via TheNiceOne (27273): "an AI galley will never end a turn where it could sink."
Building Barracks, Walls or Harbors in time (no Military category) Predator145 (692300)
Upgrading most units a modder in https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/ai-auto-producing-units-but-wont-upgrade.692035/ (search summary): it upgrades "occasionally, but has a habit of leaving most as the near useless initial unit"
Coming back from behind Ision (92917): the AI is "almost always incapable of 'coming back from behind.'"

4. Victory conditions (C3C defaults)

Victory Condition Source / quote
Conquest Defeat all other civs CFC FAQ https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/: "defeat all other civilizations"
Domination 66% of the land and 66% of the population. Ocean tiles don't count. Sea tiles count for score but not for domination. FAQ: "66% of the land AND population". BIQ GAME DominationTerrain / DominationPopulation (Game.cs). https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/maximizing-your-score/: "Ocean does not matter here, it is not included in your territory nor in the domination limit." and "Sea is important though – it counts toward increasing your territory but does not count towards the domination limit."
Space race Build all 10 spaceship parts after the Apollo Program. In the C3C BIQ, Apollo Program is a Small Wonder (isSmallWonder: true, 500 shields, buildSpaceshipParts, BIQ (repo)). FAQ: "build all 10 required spaceship parts"
Diplomatic (UN) The UN builder can call an election every 11 turns. A civ is a candidate if it has 25% of the land or population. One vote per civ, and winning needs a majority. FAQ: "a majority of the votes in the election"; "25% of the land OR population"; "every 11 turns"
Cultural (C3C) One city with 20,000 culture, or a whole-civ total that depends on map size: 60k Tiny, 80k Small, 100k Standard, 130k Large, 160k Huge. The whole-civ total must also be at least twice any other civ's (see UNVERIFIED). FAQ: "have either 20,000 culture in one city, or 100,000 culture across your empire"; in Conquests "60,000 for a Tiny map, 80,000 for Small, 100,000 for Standard, 130,000 for Large, and 160,000 for Huge." SirPleb (104294): "The required amount of culture for the "100K" cultural victory condition is variable in C3C, depending on the map size." BIQ GAME OneCityCultureWin / AllCitiesCultureWin (Game.cs).
Histographic (time) Highest score at the end of the game (2050 AD on default timescale) FAQ: "have the highest score at the end of the game"
Wonder victory (C3C, editor) Build all wonders. It is off by default. Thunderfall (104294): "Add Wonder Victory (all wonders built)"; repo allowWonderVictory: false

C3C's F8 screen shows the domination limit and how far the leader is with its spaceship. Arathorn (104294): "F8 now shows domination limit, spaceship parts completed by the leader, and a lot of other win condition information."

5. Score

5.1 Per-turn score and final score

  • Per-turn score. (Territory + 2*HappyCitizens + ContentCitizens + Specialists) * Difficulty
    • https://civfanatics.com/civ3/strategy/game-mechanics/maximizing-your-score/ gives this formula. Territory: "“Territory” is the number of tiles which are within your sphere of influence."
    • CFC FAQ (https://civfanatics.com/civ3/faq/) gives the same formula.
    • SirPleb, 20 Jan 2002: https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/counting-final-points.14617 says "Multiply the total by the difficulty level." He counts 1 per tile, 2 per happy citizen and 1 per content citizen or specialist. He calls it his understanding from testing.
  • Final score is the average of the per-turn scores. Maximizing-your-score: "The total of all your per-turn scores is divided by the number of turns played so far to get your actual game score." So late changes do little. col, https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/increasing-final-score.37091: "Unlike Civ2 where your score is calculated at the end, this doesnt help much."
  • Things that don't count.
    • Maximizing-your-score: "Wonders, cultural value, military size, science learned, etc. – none of these affect the score."
    • Bamspeedy (37091): "score doesn't take into account improvements, wonders, great leaders, diplomacy, etc."
    • Future techs are the one small exception (see UNVERIFIED for their value). Maximizing-your-score: "it counts for so little in score that it is not worth devoting any energy to it."
  • Difficulty multiplier. In C3C it is 1 to 8. SirPleb (68813): Conquests multipliers are "Chieftain: 1, Warlord: 2, Regent: 3, Monarch: 4, Emperor: 5, Demigod: 6, Deity: 7, Sid: 8". Before Conquests they were "Chieftain: 1, ... Deity: 6".

5.2 Early-victory ("finish") bonus

  • The bonus. A win before 2050 AD adds (2050 - FinishYear) * Difficulty, and BC years count as negative.
    • Maximizing-your-score: "If you win before 2050AD, you get a bonus which is added to your regular score." Its example is a Regent win in 310 BC: (2050-(-310))*3 = 7080.
    • SirPleb (14617): "The bonus is (2050 - CurrentDate) * difficulty." He says it is the same for every victory type.
    • Bamspeedy (37091): "the finish bonus which is only dependant on what year you win."
  • Based on year, not turns. Because it is based on the year, earlier wins get a larger bonus. BC-era wins benefit most because BC years count as negative. The term "Jesus score" was not found in any source (see UNVERIFIED).

Disputed

  1. Which difficulty the AI uses for content citizens, quelling, barbarian bonus and corruption/OCN.
    • TheNiceOne (37490) says Regent.
    • Pyrkaige (68642) says Monarch ("or did in PTW, and most likely still do").
    • Bamspeedy (37490) says the BIQ "default AI intelligence" setting (RULE DefaultDifficultyLevel) controls it.
  2. Deity values.
    • Vanilla/PTW Deity: OCN 70%, AI-AI trade 160 (Pyrkaige 37490).
    • C3C Deity: OCN 60%, AI-AI trade 170 (Pyrkaige 68642; TimBentley 104294).
    • Some posts called Deity's cost factor 0.7 (37490 thread summary). Others conclude Deity is 0.6 in every version and 0.7 is C3C Demigod.
  3. How the cost factor applies to research. Pre-patch, the AI's research is cheaper. In 1.29 and later, the human's research cost is divided by the cost factor (theNiceOne 86979; Bamspeedy 37490). Production is still scaled on the AI side.
  4. Commercial.
    • Sullla (pre-C3C): corruption -12.5%, raised to 25% in 1.29f, plus +1/+2 gold at City/Metropolis.
    • CFC FAQ: OCN +25%, plus +1 commerce at City and again at Metropolis.
    • These may describe the same mechanism in different words (OCN vs corruption %). Not resolved.
  5. Industrious in C3C.
    • CFC FAQ and Artifex: workers +50%.
    • stachnie (75226) thinks workers are no faster than non-Industrious civs' workers.
  6. Agricultural under Despotism.
    • Arathorn: no bonus under Despotism.
    • TimBentley: under Despotism the bonus applies only to cities on a river.
  7. Seafaring city-tile commerce.
    • Arathorn and CFC FAQ: all salt-water coastal cities.
    • StrategyWiki (via search summary only): the capital. StrategyWiki returned 403, so this is unchecked.
  8. Favourite government.
    • Soren (255541) implies it is a real preference ("aggressive ruler preferring despotism").
    • chiefpaco and Propaganda (21298): it may only affect attitude, and AIs "always choose Democracy" even when it is shunned.
  9. Build-often/never: how many flags are effective.
    • Ozymandias (tested): more than 3-4 and the AI ignores them all.
    • Predator145, citing alexman second-hand: 6.
    • C0rTeZ48 recalls more than 2 Build Often having no effect.
  10. AI amphibious ability.
    • Soren (255541) and the C3C notes say it can mount naval invasions and escorts transports.
    • Ision (92917) and several posters say its landings are too small to hold what they take.
  11. UN election interval.
    • CFC FAQ: every 11 turns.
    • A forum post in the search results: "usually every 20 turns or so". That post was not fetched.
  12. AI city spacing.
    • Dominae (57896): the AI follows about 4-tile spacing at all levels.
    • Brain (113953): the AI "often leaves big gaps".
    • These are consistent if the AI aims for wide spacing and fills gaps later, but nobody measured it.

UNVERIFIED / open questions

  • "Jesus score." No source uses this term. The likely meaning is the year-based finish bonus, where BC years count as negative, so a BC win gets more than 2050 points × difficulty. Confirm what the requester meant.
  • Future tech score value. Only "tiny" is sourced. A forum estimate of about one content citizen per future tech was seen in a search summary. https://forums.civfanatics.com/threads/scoring-of-future-tech.31261 returned HTTP 429.
  • C3C optimal city number per world size.
    • Vanilla: Tiny 12, Small 14, Standard 16, Large 24, Huge 32 (Pyrkaige 37490).
    • The repo's worldSizes has 14/17/20/28/36, but the regenerate readme says worldSizes is "not imported from the BIQ". Read BIQ WSIZ from conquests.biq to settle this.
  • Exact culture victory rule.
    • Unsourced here: whether the whole-civ total must be at least twice the second civ's (Giant Bomb search summary only).
    • The one-city 20,000 threshold in C3C (FAQ says 20,000). Read Game.OneCityCultureWin and AllCitiesCultureWin from conquests.biq.
  • When the space race is won. At launch or on arrival? Does capturing the capital destroy the ship? No Civ3-specific source.
  • Domination thresholds. The 66%/66% values are C3C editor defaults. In Civ3 1.29f, are they hard-coded at 66%? Only the FAQ is the source.
  • UN details. Is the UN builder always a candidate? Is the next most populous civ added when there is only one candidate? Seen only in the search summary of the CFC FAQ, not in a fetched quote.
  • Seafaring. The cheaper Harbor (Giant Bomb search summary only) and the exact sinking reduction have no number.
  • Industrious city-tile shield bonus. The size thresholds are paraphrased from the FAQ and not quoted verbatim.
  • Expansionist. The exact goody-hut table shift is unsourced.
  • Scout availability. Can only Expansionist civs build Scouts? Seen in a search summary only.
  • Settlers and workers. Does the AI always escort settlers in Civ3? No Civ3 source. How does the AI pick tiles for workers? No source.
  • War triggers.
    • The exact war-declaration formula (power ratio, attitude, aggression weight, effect of the C3C aggression setting).
    • The "units in our territory" sequence. The search summary says the AI asks once, then can force you out or declare war. No verbatim primary quote was fetched.
  • AI sliders. Is the AI's science/tax split fixed, or chosen? Only the luxury claim (Ision) is sourced.
  • AI free starting units. Which unit types are "defensive" and "offensive" (the best ones available at game start)? Not sourced.
  • Golden-age trigger. It depends on traits, but no verbatim source was fetched in this pass. Leave it to the golden-age section.

Back to the top

Appendix: where the game still differs

Each compendium section was compared with the engine code. The sourced discrepancies were fixed, with a test each and the source quoted in a code comment (commits 812b79d6 to 8dd1c95c). This file lists what is still open.

Waiting on the project owner

  1. Industrious city-tile shield. The Civilopedia (GCON_Strengths) says "the center city square of all cities produces extra shields in cities and metros". civ-wiki.de (Stadtfeldertrag), which the code cites, gives the extra shield only at size 13 and up. The code keeps metropolises only.
  2. Resource depletion. The C3C editor help says the chance "is not influenced by use; a resource can disappear even if a civilization has never used the resource". The code lets only resources in use run out, following CFC tests (ResourceDepletion.cs).
  3. Pop-rush rounding. The code rounds to the nearest citizen (owner's decision). DBear's CFC post says one citizen "per 20 shields or fraction thereof", which rounds up.
  4. AI luxury slider. Ision (CFC 92917) says the Civ3 AI "will NOT use the luxury slider". The AI here raises it to 50% when most cities are unhappy. This is an AI heuristic, so changing it needs an AI trial.
  5. AI government changes at war. Players report the Civ3 AI moving to Monarchy or Communism during long wars (chiefpaco, hedged). GovernmentAI never switches while at war, except out of Despotism.
  6. Techs across eras. Quintillus says trades and the Great Library cross eras. The code offers only techs of the current era or earlier.

Skipped: sources too thin

  • The AI-human trade markup table (TheNiceOne) is only summarized in the compendium, with no Demigod or Sid rows, and the tech price function is UNVERIFIED.
  • Rate of fire against buildings, citizens and improvements: no source says how shots pick targets.
  • Hut and camp messages in Civ3's wording: the engine has no barbarian tribe names yet.

Small fixes not yet done

  • An AI that doesn't found its capital on turn 1 never gets its difficulty start units (Player.MaybeSpawnBonusUnits). The fix needs a saved "units given" flag.
  • The resistance government modifier covers 6 governments from a CFC article. The BIQ has a full 8×8 ResistanceModifier table that also covers Feudalism and Fascism.
  • Hoover Dam needs a river at the city tile; the Civilopedia says within the city radius. Iron Works accepts Iron and Coal from anywhere, not just the city radius. Check the BIQ flag bit first (QueryCiv3/BiqSections/Bldg.cs).
  • The Great Library grants a whole prerequisite chain in one turn. justanick says it makes one pass in editor order.
  • A city with one turn left on its build can still switch to a wonder (Travathian says it can't).
  • Units can join an army in any of the owner's cities; README-V says only the city where it was built. Restricting that needs a saved origin city.

Not implemented (sourced Civ3 features)

  • Citizens and governments: assimilation of foreign citizens (BIQ AssimilationChance, 1–4% a turn by government); Fascism's Forced Resettlement and Xenophobic flags; riots destroying improvements in disorder (ChanceOfRioting); prolonged disorder overthrowing the government; mobilization; global warming.
  • Map and combat: bombardment making craters; plague; barricades blocking movement; colonies overrun by barbarians.
  • Espionage and diplomacy: the "carefully" and "safely" espionage levels; AI sabotage and propaganda missions; AI loans.
  • Attitude: many smaller terms (broken alliances, razing, a common enemy, culture-flip offers, territory warnings).
